# Wave 3 phase 1 (understand): synthesis and reader notes

Produced 2026-08-23 by the `wave3-understand` workflow (run `wf_13fdb0a6-c60`): thirteen readers,
one per wave-3 ticket, plus one synthesizer. All fourteen at `effort: 'high'` on the session model,
per the model and effort ruling in `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md`.
Every reader returned; none errored. Assessed against `origin/main` at `0bf207e5`.

Design source read from the unpacked standalone mock. Template line numbers below refer to the
unpack of `docs/design-handoff/2026-08-21-birdbrain-standalone/Birdbrain-standalone.html`
described in that folder's README.

Nothing was implemented. This document is notes.

## Headline

Phase 1 broke the prep doc's central serialization claim: the verifier is not #398's alone, because #399's acceptance criterion 2 requires a third outcome from the same two blocks of `src/shared/verify/evidencePackage.ts` that #398 rewrites, and #401 must additionally write `export.ts` and `ExportDialog.tsx`, which the map assigned to other branches. The wave has one genuinely unsolved mechanism, not thirteen: the evidence package's own bundled `manifest.jsonl` deliberately excludes the export entry that would carry `captureIds` (`export.ts:368-379` says so in a comment), so #398's AC 3 has no signed source to reconcile against, and every downstream part of that ticket sits on the answer. Two classification errors change the merge gate: #393 is evidence-affecting at the blocking tier on three independent paths and must not auto-merge, and #695 is a build that the doc reclassified as paperwork and dropped from every track and from the merge order. Three tickets turned out to be smaller than the prep doc implies (#702 is one deletion because the REC pill already ships at `TopBar.tsx:118-123`; #701 is medium because `radix-ui` ^1.6.1 is already a production dependency exporting a complete ContextMenu; #704 is one component plus one mount) and two turned out to be much larger (#399, #803).

## Blocking questions

Ranked most-blocking first. Each needs the maintainer rather than another agent. A recommendation
is given for every one.

### Q1. Where does the standalone verifier read a TRUSTED `captureIds` from for a selection-scoped package? The package's own bundled `manifest.jsonl` deliberately does not contain the export entry that quotes it: the snapshot is taken and zipped, then the export entry is appended after the zip is written, so `packageHash` cannot cover the entry that quotes it. The comment at `export.ts:368-379` spells this out and calls the one-entry lag acceptable. #398's AC 3 says the verifier reconciles artifacts against `captureIds`.

Tickets: #398, #399

**Why it blocks.** It decides the package format and every other part of #398 sits downstream of it. An implementer who does not notice will write the manifest field, wire the artifact filter, then discover at the verifier step that the signed source is not in the package, and will most likely reach for `evidence.json`, which the verifier's own code declares untrusted and which would let a tamperer add ids to `captureIds` to explain away a capture they removed. That is precisely the #580 detection the orphan test exists to hold. Guessing here produces a package that verifies and should not, on the wave's critical-path ticket.

**Recommendation.** Ship the signed export entry as its own package member. Restructure `generateReport` so `buildEvidenceZip` returns entries plus `packageHash` without writing, append the manifest entry (rolling back via the existing `rollbackManifestEntry` on failure), then `unshift` the full signed line as `export-entry.json` exactly the way `evidence.json` is `unshifted`, so it stays outside artifacts and outside `packageHash`. The verifier checks that file's signature against the bundled public key, checks its `prevHash` equals the bundled chain head, recomputes its `entryHash`, and only then trusts its `captureIds`. Reject the cheaper alternative (reconcile against `evidence.json` and label the scope untrusted) explicitly rather than by default; it is not defensible for an evidence-affecting change.

### Q2. Where do the bytes for #392's auto-capture come from: does the extension capture and hand the server MHTML in the same request, or does the server render the page itself in a hidden window?

Tickets: #392, #393

**Why it blocks.** It decides the entire wire contract, which is what #393 is then blocked on, and it decides what the resulting Capture claims about itself. `types.ts:38-40` defines 'extension' as operator-witnessed and 'background' as the silent hidden-window path, so a server-side render cannot honestly be called operator-witnessed, which is the ticket's own wording. An implementer who guesses the hidden-window route also finds that `recaptureService.enqueue` returns only accepted and rejected, with no capture id and no promise, so there is nothing to await or attach to; they would have to extend that interface or re-implement the exclusion enforcement `exclusionPolicy.ts` exists to centralise. Either guess is several hundred lines in the wrong direction.

**Recommendation.** Extension supplies the bytes. Make the tag and note routes accept an optional multipart MHTML payload in the shape POST /api/captures already takes, and make the flow canonicalize, look up an existing Capture, and if none exists ingest the supplied payload first and attach only after ingest returns. That keeps method 'extension', makes the no-orphan criterion structurally `true` rather than defensively `true`, holds the HTTP request open so #393's inline progress is a spinner on one awaited fetch, and needs no polling route. It also explains why the URL-lookup route exists at all.

### Q3. Is JavaScript enabled in the #401 Wayback replay webview?

Tickets: #401

**Why it blocks.** It decides both whether the feature works and how much hardening is load-bearing, and the acceptance criteria never mention it. The app's only existing webview runs javascript=no, which is exactly why nobody ever set a permission handler on the mhtml-sandbox partition. With JS off, archive.org replays of most modern pages render broken or blank and the compare pane is worthless. With JS on, the permission handlers, download blocking and navigation policy stop being defence-in-depth and become the only thing between third-party code and an evidence tool. An implementer who guesses ships either a useless pane or a materially larger attack surface, and on a never-auto-merge PR that is a full review round either way.

**Recommendation.** JavaScript ON, with every item in the hardening list mandatory rather than optional: dedicated partition, will-attach-webview with a `src` allow-list restricted to `https`://web.archive.org/web/, permission request and check handlers denying everything, will-download prevented, `setWindowOpenHandler` denying, and guest navigations confined to the same prefix. JS off defeats the stated purpose. If you are unwilling to run third-party JS in the app, the honest alternative is to drop the in-app replay entirely and keep only 'Open at archive.org', which is a scope decision rather than an implementation one.

### Q4. For #399's two export classes: do the mock's Notes, Extracted text and Selector hits checklist items become real package content, and does a Working Copy append an export entry to the case manifest?

Tickets: #399

**Why it blocks.** Two scope boundaries that together decide whether the branch doubles. AC 5 is 'Notes inclusion toggles correctly (Court exhibit excludes Notes)', but nothing writes operator notes into any package today, so if notes are not added, Court exhibit and Full evidence bundle produce byte-identical output and AC 5 is untestable. On the manifest question, the issue says a Working Copy has 'no export entry indistinguishable from an Evidence Package's', which reads equally as no entry and as a distinguishable entry. Guessing 'no entry' means an operator can extract every artifact from a case and leave no audit trace, which is a custody gap in an evidence tool. Guessing 'an entry' requires a field on a strict schema. Either guess is expensive to unwind after review.

**Recommendation.** Notes yes, extracted text and selector hits no: notes are named in an acceptance criterion and in the Court-exhibit definition, the other two appear only in mock metadata strings with no criterion behind them. And append an entry for a Working Copy, with `exportClass` 'working-copy' declared optional on the strict schema and OMITTED for evidence exports, never `null` and never 'evidence', following the same omit-when-absent discipline `manifest.ts` already applies six times. Coordinate that schema edit with #398 since both land in the same union member.

### Q5. Does #701's accelerator rule mean the menus ship ONLY actions the app already exposes inline or by keyboard, or does the ticket also build the missing inline routes (copy URL, copy `SHA-256`, duplicate, per-entity export, merge tags, backfill selector) so the mock's fuller action sets can ship?

Tickets: #701, #803

**Why it blocks.** It is the difference between a roughly 400-line branch and a multi-day one, and it changes what the menus contain, which is the whole deliverable. Of the mock's 13 capture actions the app has 6; of 8 note actions, 3; of 8 selector actions, 3; of 7 tag actions, 4. An implementer who guesses 'build the missing ones' produces a branch nobody scoped; one who guesses 'intersection only' ships menus a reviewer may call incomplete against the mock. Neither is recoverable at review, and the answer propagates to #803's four deferred kinds.

**Recommendation.** Intersection only. Ruling 1's own wording is 'reachable inline or by an EXISTING keyboard path' and 'a missing menu is never a missing capability', both of which read as a mirror of current capability rather than an expansion. File the missing capabilities as separate per-kind issues and let the registry absorb them later at near-zero cost. It is also the only reading under which 'ONE ticket, not a shared component plus staged per-surface adoption' is a sane call.

### Q6. Does #803 ship as one branch, and does the MHTML Parts tab render a per-part `SHA-256` the app has never computed?

Tickets: #803

**Why it blocks.** Two answers, both start-blockers. On size: at 1500 to 2500 changed lines across a new main-process service cluster, three new IPC channels, a five-tab screen, four context-menu kinds, an E2E screenshot rewrite and two published docs, this will not complete unattended in one cycle and it cannot auto-merge, so a dispatcher needs to know how many slots it consumes before it starts. On the hash: nothing in the tree hashes a MIME part, and the mock hands the implementer a caption asserting that every part is hashed at capture time and re-checked against the manifest. Both claims are `false` for this app. An implementer who guesses 'compute it' ships a hash column on an evidence screen that looks identical to the manifest-anchored hashes one pane above it.

**Recommendation.** Split into four labelled branches: (a) main-side readers plus IPC and their known-answer tests, (b) screen shell plus the E2E and docs rewrite, (c) the remaining tabs, (d) the four context-menu kinds after #701. On the hash, compute at display time over the raw encoded bytes, label the column '`SHA-256` (computed now)' with a one-line statement that only the whole MHTML file is anchored in the manifest, never render a shield or the word verified, replace the mock caption wholesale, and drop the part menu's 'Verify against manifest' item. If you would rather not add new hashing to an evidence screen at all, omitting the column is equally defensible and cheaper.

### Q7. How does #405's bundled demo Case get past the operator-name gate on a fresh install, and how does the fixture archive ship?

Tickets: #405

**Why it blocks.** `importCaseArchive` throws 'Operator name required' when settings.operatorName is empty, the default is empty, and the only writer is Settings then Operator. A fresh install is exactly the state that gets the demo Case and exactly the state with no operator name. This is unaddressed in the ticket, the ruling and the prep doc, so an implementer hits it at integration and either relaxes a gate that exists to keep unattributed evidence out of the chain or bolts on a first-run operator prompt nobody scoped, under time pressure at the end of the wave, in a blocking-tier file. The fixture question decides separately whether the branch adds a binary to git, a build script, a CI step and a signing key.

**Recommendation.** Carve out the bundled demo path with an explicit synthetic attribution rather than relaxing the gate: import it with a fixed operator string such as 'Birdbrain demo fixture', recorded in the import custody entry, so the manifest says truthfully who imported it and the gate stays intact for real archives. Check the archive in under resources/ and ship it via build.extraResources beside the extension entry: the archive verifies its chain against the key bundled inside itself, so a checked-in file needs no build-time signing. Pair it with a test that imports the shipped fixture against the current schema, so a later migration the frozen fixture cannot satisfy fails CI rather than an operator's first launch.

### Q8. Is #393's Selector action one click, matching the existing context-menu path, or the mock's two-step confirm popover with 'Watch for new hits' and 'Backfill - scan N existing captures' toggles?

Tickets: #393, #391

**Why it blocks.** The acceptance criterion and the mock give opposite answers and the gap is not cosmetic. Neither toggle has a backend: POST /api/selectors takes only `caseId`, pattern and label, auto-capture is HOTFIX-disabled throughout `background.ts`, and no backfill or rescan capability exists anywhere. An implementer who follows the mock either invents server surface (a second server ticket inside an extension ticket) or ships two checkboxes wired to nothing. One who follows the criterion ships a bar that does not match the design source, which the wave's definition of done rejects. #391 faces the same popover in the renderer.

**Recommendation.** Follow the acceptance criterion: one click, matching the context-menu path, no confirm popover in #393. File the typed confirm popover with watch and backfill as its own ticket blocked on the server work those toggles need. The mock is the pixel source, but its popover depends on capabilities that do not exist and that #392 does not add, so building it here means building them here.

### Q9. What does #391's Tag action do when the note has no capture, and what does the mock's 'Backfill - scan N existing captures' checkbox do given that `selectorLifecycle` already backfills unconditionally on every create?

Tickets: #391

**Why it blocks.** The mock applies the tag to the note itself, but the app has no note-tag relation: tags attach only through `capture_tags`, and two of the four NoteEditor mounts have no note and therefore no capture at all. An implementer who guesses 'apply to note.captureId' ships an action that throws or silently no-ops in half the mounts; one who guesses 'create the tag but apply it to nothing' ships a success toast for a write that did nothing. Separately, honouring the Backfill checkbox needs a parameter reaching `selectorLifecycle.ts`, which is blocking tier, and would silently convert a non-evidence auto-mergeable ticket into one owing an Evidence impact section and human review.

**Recommendation.** On Tag: create-or-reuse the tag by name (looking it up first, because tags.name is UNIQUE and `createTag` is a bare INSERT that throws on a duplicate), apply it to note.captureId or the anchor's `captureId` when one exists, and render the button disabled with an explanatory title when none does. On Backfill: render the checkbox checked and disabled with copy saying backfill always runs. That matches the mock's pixels, tells the truth about behaviour, keeps the diff renderer-side and costs nothing. Record both divergences on #708.

### Q10. What exactly does #695's one clear control clear: only the search query plus the selector filters, as ruling 4 says literally, or everything narrowing the list including format, date and favourites-only, as the mock's single `clearFilters` handler implies?

Tickets: #695

**Why it blocks.** It is the only acceptance-shaped clause the ruling contains, and the app already has three disjoint clear paths the answer either consolidates or leaves in place. An implementer who reads 'clears both' narrowly ships a THIRD clear control whose scope partially overlaps the other two, which is precisely the confusion the ruling is trying to prevent, and it is not fixable at review without redoing the header. It also decides whether `countActiveFilters` stays menu-scoped or becomes a general narrowing count, which changes what the Filter badge means.

**Recommendation.** One handler clearing everything: query, selector filters, format, date, favourites. That matches the mock, where a single `clearFilters` serves the dropdown item and both narrowed empty states, and it satisfies the ruling's actual concern. Surface it in three places all calling the same function: widen the existing selector banner into a narrowing strip naming every active narrowing with one x, keep the dropdown's 'Clear all filters' item pointed at it, and add the clear button to the narrowed empty state, which the app lacks entirely. Keep `countActiveFilters` menu-scoped and add a separate `isNarrowed` predicate.

### Q11. What form does #708's deliverable take, and does producing it close #708 or leave #708 open as the standing intake queue?

Tickets: #708

**Why it blocks.** The ticket says 'there is no other place to collect them' but never says where they go. A third comment on #708 keeps the register inside GitHub where the next wave's readers cannot grep it, and it is already failing there: two comments, 51 unreconciled items, six ruled items missing. Writing a tracked document while you intended #708 to stay the live register produces two sources that drift. Writing it and closing #708 leaves wave-3 implementers nowhere to send their own constraints, which is the failure #708 was opened to prevent.

**Recommendation.** Tracked document at `docs/specs/2026-08-23-standalone-mock-corrections-brief.md`, and #708 stays OPEN as the intake queue with that document as the dated snapshot the design side is actually sent. Do not put it in docs/design-handoff/, whose README says not to edit bundle contents. Split it into two labelled sections, 'Needs a design decision' and 'Recorded deviations, no response wanted', with counts in the opening line, and drop items the tree has already closed (C11 is fixed in `selectorRepo`, C8 is answered by ruling 4).

## Corrections to the prep doc

Deduplicated and ranked. `changes-the-plan` first.

### #393 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** Table row: '#393 extension in-page bar | `content.ts` selection bar, suppression protocol | #392 | Evidence: no', and 'Non-evidence tickets in this wave (#391, #393, and the extras) may auto-merge on required checks green plus an agent/pre-pass success verdict.'

**Correction.** #393 is evidence-affecting at the blocking tier on three independent paths and must never auto-merge. It cannot avoid `content.ts` (the bar mounts in the content script) or `background.ts` and `utils/api.ts` (the content script imports nothing from utils/api and its only fetch is a data: URL, so every server call is relayed through the service worker). Substantively as well as mechanically: the change injects a persistent element into the page that `saveAsMHTML` serialises and every screenshot slice renders, which is the exact defect class #386 was opened for. #391 is correctly classified.

**Evidence.** `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:161` (`background.ts`, blocking), :162 (`content.ts`, blocking), :164 (`utils/api.ts`, blocking), all read directly. `gh` `api` issues/393/labels returns enhancement,redesign only.

### #399 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** 'src/shared/verify/** and src/verifier/**. #398 alone. No other ticket touches the verifier.'

**Correction.** False, and two readers found it independently. #399's acceptance criterion 2 reads 'the standalone verifier says not a verifiable object rather than FAIL' for a Working Copy. Today `verifyEvidencePackage` returns pass:`false` with '`manifest.jsonl` missing from package' and `cli.ts` prints RESULT: FAIL with exit code 1. The verifier has no third outcome or marker check. That decision lives in the same blocks #398 rewrites to scope the capture set, which makes the #398-then-#399 serialization tighter than the map represents and creates a semantic conflict risk a textual merge will not catch.

**Evidence.** `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.ts:88`-95` (early return on missing manifest, read directly); `src/verifier/cli.ts` exit code is result.pass ? 0 : 1; `gh` issue view 399 AC 2.

### #398 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** '`appendManifestEntry` canonicalizes {...entry, index, `prevHash`, `schemaVersion`} through `canonicalStringify` and hashes that (`manifest.ts:357-364`), so an always-present field rewrites every legacy entry canonical body and breaks the chain on existing packages.'

**Correction.** The line cite is exact and the conclusion (omit the fields) is right, but the stated mechanism is wrong and it points implementers at the wrong file. Adding a key to the TypeScript input type cannot touch entries already on disk: verification re-canonicalizes each entry from its own parsed JSON, so a legacy entry keeps its bytes and its hash. The two ways this really breaks legacy chains both live in `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389`, which the prep doc never names: a required key makes every legacy export entry fail the .strict() parse ('Invalid entry shape'), and a Zod .default() injects the key into `schemaResult.data`, which is what the chain walker pushes and re-hashes ('Entry hash mismatch'). The writer-side omission rule survives for a different reason: writing scope 'case' produces a new on-disk shape that already-distributed verifier binaries reject under .strict().

**Evidence.** `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389` read directly, .strict() confirmed at :389; `src/shared/verify/`manifestChain.ts:82`-86`, :123, :130; `src/shared/verify/`canonicalJson.ts`:33` filters `undefined` only.

### #695 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** 'Paperwork items (#699, #663, #708, and the #695 and #707 outcomes) take no slot and can land any time after their rulings.'

**Correction.** #695 is not paperwork. Ruling 4 rules it to be BUILT: a client-side title and URL filter over the loaded list, intersecting with the selector filters, with one control clearing both. That is renderer code across three source files plus tests. As a consequence it appears in no track and nowhere in the merge order. It belongs in track C. Confirmed the ruling's premise holds: `listCaptures` is SELECT * with no LIMIT, the query fetches once with no pagination, and CaptureList maps every displayed capture with no windowing, so a client-side filter searches 100% of the case and no `false` negative is possible.

**Evidence.** `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md` 'Track shape and merge order' versus its own ruling 4; `src/main/services/db/`captureRepo.ts:14`-19`.

### #401 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** '#401 adds pinned-ref rendering to `reportHtml.ts`, which has no wayback code today, so its region is disjoint from #398's and #399's.' The #401 row lists only 'WaybackTab to panel, sandboxed webview, refs in the report'.

**Correction.** Incomplete on two counts. `reportHtml.ts` cannot render refs the export never loaded, so #401 must also write `src/main/services/export.ts` at the ExportData literal (262-291), the same blocking-tier file #398 edits. And the mock puts a Pinned Wayback snapshots block inside the export dialog, so #401 also writes `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx`, blocking tier and the file #399 rewrites. Neither file appears in the prep doc's #401 row. #401 also changes `captureColumns.ts` and the captures route, and breaks `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129`, which asserts today's full-bleed wayback layout and the wayback-lookup-btn `testid` the redesign removes.

**Evidence.** grep -ic wayback on `reportHtml.ts` and `ExportDialog.tsx` both return 0, run directly; /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html:12797-12811; assessment:236 and :245.

### #401 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** '`src/main/index.ts` already enforces evidence-viewer invariants and is blocking tier; whatever the panel needs belongs there rather than in a second place.'

**Correction.** Right about the destination, wrong about the shape. What `index.ts:234-247` enforces is a single hard-coded rule: allow one initial navigation whose URL starts with file://, block everything else, for every webview in the app. A Wayback webview must load `https`://web.archive.org and tolerate archive.org's redirects, so #401 has to MODIFY that invariant into a partition-aware policy, not add beside it. The codebase also has no will-attach-webview handler anywhere in src/, no permission handler on any renderer-facing session, and no will-download listener. That handler is simultaneously the MHTML evidence viewer's only navigation guard, so a wrong discriminator silently loosens it. This is the branch's highest-risk edit and the prep doc reads as if it were additive.

**Evidence.** `src/main/index.ts:234-247` and :152-158; `src/renderer/components/captures/MhtmlViewer.tsx:43-49`; grep across src/, tests/ and e2e/ for will-attach-webview returns nothing.

### #392 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** 'The capture server, `captureServer.ts`. #392 alone in this wave. The guard at `captureServer.ts:147-158` already covers every state-changing endpoint, so new routes inherit it rather than re-implementing it.'

**Correction.** Half right, and the unqualified form is dangerous. The guard tests c.req.method === 'POST' only, and Hono runs middleware registered before the matching route. A new route inherits the guard if and only if it is a POST registered after the app.use block. Registering above it, or using GET, PUT, PATCH or DELETE for a write, produces an unauthenticated write endpoint on loopback and nothing in the code prevents either. This bears directly on #392's URL-lookup route, which reads like a GET.

**Evidence.** `src/main/services/`captureServer.ts:147`-156` read directly, the method === 'POST' test confirmed at :149; the #392 reader's probe against the repo's own `hono` build returned POST-before-middleware 200, POST-after 401, PUT 200, GET 200.

### #405 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** '#405 is evidence-adjacent and carries no label', and 'Five evidence reviews, not four: #392, #398, #399, #401, #803.'

**Correction.** Stale on the first and wrong on the second. #405 carries evidence-affecting today, so the relabel the doc asks for was already applied by its own companion action, and the count is six evidence reviews, not five. #405 is the wave's last merge and therefore the one most likely to be rushed, and it earns the tier on its own merits: the demo mark's round trip runs through `caseRepo.importCaseRow` and the fresh-install question sits inside `caseArchive.ts`, both blocking.

**Evidence.** `gh` `api` repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/405/labels returns enhancement, evidence-affecting, redesign, run directly.

### #701 (changes-the-plan)

**Claim.** Ruling 1 and the #701 ruling comment: 'ui/ has no menu primitive today, so this ticket writes the accessible one.'

**Correction.** ui/ genuinely has no menu primitive, but the accessible one does not have to be written. `radix-ui` ^1.6.1 is already a PRODUCTION dependency at `package.json:145`, it re-exports @radix-ui/react-context-menu 2.3.2, and the repo already consumes it at `ui/tabs.tsx:3` with shadcn configured in `components.json`. Adopting it is a zero-new-dependency change supplying roles, roving `tabindex`, typeahead, focus return, Escape, outside-click, portal and collision-aware positioning. Hand-rolling those is roughly 250 avoidable lines the 90%-changed-line coverage gate then has to cover, and this repo has already got it wrong three times (CaptureMenu, CaptureDownloadMenu and ExportMenu all carry role=menu and Escape but zero focus management). This moves #701 from large to medium.

**Evidence.** `package.json:145` and `node_modules/radix-ui/package.json:50` read directly; `src/renderer/components/ui/tabs.tsx:3` read directly.

### #399 (changes-an-estimate)

**Claim.** Conflict map: '#399 also round-trips the field through `caseArchive.ts`', and the ruling comment's 'make sure the flag is in `collectCaseRows` / the import path'.

**Correction.** `caseArchive.ts` holds no cases column list and the export half needs no code at all: `collectCaseRow` is SELECT * so both new columns travel into `data.json` automatically. Only the import half needs editing, and it is `caseRepo.importCaseRow`'s hand-written nine-column INSERT. This matters because `caseArchive.ts` is blocking tier and a reviewer will look for its diff and not find one, and because #405's round-trip acceptance criterion lands on the same INSERT.

**Evidence.** `src/main/services/db/`caseRepo.ts:140`-146` and :148-169 read directly; the explicit column list ends at `exclusion_mode` with a comment explaining the pattern.

### #701 (changes-an-estimate)

**Claim.** Open items: '#686 and #687 should precede #701.' Ruling 1 states it harder: '#686/#687 land first.'

**Correction.** Tidy rather than required. #686 touches `ui/dialog.tsx` and the role=dialog half of the guard; #687 touches `CommandPalette.tsx`. A context menu is role=menu, never role=dialog, so neither fix reaches its code path. The Escape-precedence mechanism #701 actually needs already exists, is already used and is already tested: the [`data-selection-escape-guard`] opt-in at `useCaptureSelection.ts:85`. Keep a soft rule that #686 goes first if both hold a slot, because #686 owns that file; do not gate #701's dispatch on it.

**Evidence.** `src/renderer/components/captures/`useCaptureSelection.ts`:71`, :85, :90; `CaptureSelectionBar.tsx:173` and :233; `tests/components/`useCaptureSelection.test.tsx`:192`.

### #391 (changes-an-estimate)

**Claim.** '#391, #392, #398, #399, #401 all append to `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/types.ts` and `src/main/`ipcHandlers`.ts`.'

**Correction.** #391 appends to none of the three. Every channel and payload field it needs already exists: selectors:create carries origin, the tag channels are unchanged, and SELECTOR_ORIGINS already contains 'note' at `types.ts:660`. The doc's own grounding comment contradicts this line by calling the ticket renderer-only. #803 is missing from the list, and `src/preload/index.ts` is missing from the map entirely despite enumerating every channel by hand.

**Evidence.** `src/shared/ipc.ts:337-343` (`CreateSelectorParams.origin`); `src/shared/types.ts:660`; `src/preload/index.ts:54-63`.

### #702 (changes-an-estimate)

**Claim.** The #702 ruling comment: 'The mock hides it while recording (`showConnected`: `extConnected` && !`sessionOn`, line 13930)... Divergence from the design', and the framing that #702 builds a Recording indicator.

**Correction.** Two corrections, both changing what the branch is. The REC pill already exists at `TopBar.tsx:118-123`, already gated on `sessionActive`, and is already a structural and colour match for the mock. The whole behavioural change is deleting the three-line early return at `ConnectionStatus.tsx:8-10`. Separately, `showConnected` is dead state in the mock, consumed by no markup; the mock's Connected control is unconditional. The component that hides the chip is the app's own, from a commit predating the redesign, so this is a bug fix converging on the mock, not a divergence from it. Only one of the two ruled items is a real #708 entry. The ruling comment's template line numbers are also off by +393 (13861 and 13930 should be 13468 and 13537), which lands an implementer inside annotation-pin code.

**Evidence.** `src/renderer/components/status/ConnectionStatus.tsx:4-10` and `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx:114-126`, both read directly.

### #803 (changes-an-estimate)

**Claim.** Conflict map: 'Only #399 needs schema, claiming v31 for the Case `case_number` column.' And the ticket table treats #803 as one dispatchable branch.

**Correction.** The v31 line is stale within the same document, whose rulings section two pages earlier says v31 carries `case_number` AND the demo flag; an implementer reading the map alone writes a one-column migration and #405 is blocked. And #803 is a rewrite spanning a new main-process service cluster, three new IPC channels, a five-tab screen, four context-menu kinds, an E2E screenshot rewrite and two published docs, realistically 1500 to 2500 changed lines. It should ship as three or four labelled branches.

**Evidence.** The prep doc's 'Migration order' paragraph versus its own 'What the rulings changed' bullet; `e2e/readme-screenshots.spec.ts:525-560`, `README.md:62-65`, `website/content/docs/screenshots.mdx:78-84`.

## Sharpened conflict map

This replaces the conflict map in the prep doc. `changed` marks an entry the prep doc got wrong,
understated, or omitted.

### `src/shared/verify/evidencePackage.ts`

Tickets: #398, #399. Nature: serialize. Changed from prep doc: yes.

#398 lands the shape change: scoped 7.3/7.5 plus any widening of PackageVerifyResult. #399 supplies only the Working Copy marker file and its detection, on a branch cut from main after #398 merges. Two branches independently widening PackageVerifyResult is a semantic conflict a textual merge will not catch. Verified: `evidencePackage.ts:157-166` hard-FAILs for every chain capture whose pages/{id}.mhtml is absent, and :88-95 returns pass:`false` with '`manifest.jsonl` missing from package' for exactly the input a Working Copy is.

### `src/shared/schemas.ts (ManifestExportEntrySchema, 373-389)`

Tickets: #398, #399. Nature: serialize. Changed from prep doc: yes.

This file, not `manifest.ts`, is where legacy chains actually break, and the prep doc never names it. The schema is .strict() (verified at `schemas.ts:389`). New keys must be .optional() with NO .default(): a required key makes every legacy export entry return 'Invalid entry shape', and a .default() injects the key into `schemaResult.data`, which `manifestChain` pushes and re-hashes, producing 'Entry hash mismatch' on every legacy export entry. #398 adds scope/`captureIds`; #399 adds `exportClass` only if a Working Copy appends an entry. One branch at a time.

### `src/main/services/export.ts`

Tickets: #398, #399, #401. Nature: serialize. Changed from prep doc: yes.

Three tickets, not two. #398 owns the capture-set filter at :260 and the manifest append at :387-396 (verified: the only `appendManifestEntry` export call site). #399 branches `generateReport` and `buildEvidenceZip` on export class. #401 adds pinned Wayback refs to the ExportData literal at :262-291, which the prep doc never assigned here. Order #398 then #399 then #401; #401 must not touch anything in 368-400, where the deliberate append-after-zip ordering comment lives.

## Revised merge order and track shape

Changed from the prep doc: yes.

#702 -> #398 -> #392 -> #695 -> #391 -> #399 -> #401 -> #393 -> #701 -> #704 -> `#803a` -> `#803b` -> `#803c` -> `#803d` -> #405

Changed. Four substantive edits to the prep doc's order. (1) #695 is added: it is a build, not paperwork, and the doc dropped it from every track and from the order. (2) #803 is added: ruling 7 created it the same day the order was written and the order was never amended, yet ruling 1 makes #803 the direct consumer of #701's registry. (3) The export chain deepens from three to four, #398 to #399 to #401 to #405, because #401 must now write `ExportDialog.tsx` after #399 rebuilds it and #405 consumes #399's v31 demo column. (4) #702 moves to the front: it is a single three-line deletion in `ConnectionStatus.tsx` (verified) and warms a slot at near-zero cost while the export chain's blocking question is answered. #704 moves behind #401 to avoid a rebase on the TABS array, and #803's menu split behind #701. #708 takes no slot and can land at any point.

### Track A, export and evidence, strictly serial

Tickets: #398, #399, #401, #405

The wave's critical path and four of its six evidence reviews. Every branch cut from main after the previous merges, never stacked. #398 cannot start until the trusted-captureIds question is answered; #399 cannot start until the Working Copy scope boundary is; #401 cannot start until the webview JavaScript question is. Answer all three before dispatching any of them, or the track stalls three separate times.

### Track B, extension and server, then the Data readers

Tickets: #392, #393, `#803a`, `#803b`, `#803c`

#392 then #393 is unchanged and correct, but #393 must gain the evidence-affecting label before dispatch or `pre-pass-gate.yml` keys wrong and an auto-merge rule that should not apply to it does. #803 splits: (a) the three main-process readers plus IPC, preload, types and their known-answer tests, (b) the screen shell plus the E2E and docs rewrite, (c) the remaining tabs. Every split branch keeps the evidence-affecting label.

### Track C, renderer chrome, cheapest first

Tickets: #702, #695, #391, #701, #704, `#803d`

All non-evidence and all auto-merge-eligible except #704, which stays non-evidence only if it never touches `MhtmlViewer.tsx`. #702 is one deletion plus one new test file. #701 is medium once it adopts the Radix ContextMenu already in `package.json` rather than hand-rolling roles, roving `tabindex`, typeahead and focus return. `#803d` adds the file, folder, part and ledger kinds to #701's registry.

What each still needs before it earns `ready-for-agent`:

**#398.** The trusted-captureIds ruling, without which AC 3 cannot be finished. Also an amended ADR-0009 or an explicit note that the 2026-08-23 ruling supersedes its 'scope always present' consequences bullet and its claim that Package Verification is untouched (`false`: `evidencePackage.ts:157-166` hard-FAILs for every unselected capture). And an acceptance criterion for the frozen pre-scope fixture package, since none exists in tests/fixtures today and 'run the verifier against a pre-scope package' currently has no package to run against. The starting-file list is missing two mandatory blocking-tier files: `src/shared/schemas.ts`, where the field is actually accepted or rejected, and `src/main/services/`verifyRunbook.ts:136`-137`, which tells a by-hand reviewer to sha256 pages/$id.mhtml for every capture line.

**#399.** Three scope answers: whether notes, extracted text and selector hits become real package content, whether a Working Copy is recorded in the manifest, and whether this ticket may touch the verifier the prep doc reserves to #398. The body still describes only a case-number field and must absorb the 2026-08-23 ruling's two-column v31 scope. It should also state explicitly that the mock's unconditional custody card and its Examiner and analyst vocabulary are to be diverged from. Separately worth re-reading: the CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION ruling leans on a wave-1 adjudication about a display-only column, and the demo flag is the 'silently discard' limb the constant's own comment describes.

**#401.** Three answers: JavaScript on or off in the replay webview, whether pinned refs render in a new report appendix or the existing per-exhibit corroboration block, and whether the left compare pane is the MHTML webview or the stored screenshot (which decides whether one guest or two are live at once). Beyond the questions, one acceptance criterion the issue lacks: the branch reconciles `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129`, which asserts today's full-bleed wayback layout and the wayback-lookup-btn `testid` the redesign removes. Without it an agent reports green locally and goes red in CI. The issue should also name `export.ts` and `ExportDialog.tsx`, both blocking tier and both owned by other wave-3 branches.

**#392.** Two contract decisions: where the auto-capture bytes come from, and whether the URL-lookup route is GET or POST (the guard only fires on POST, so a GET lookup is unauthenticated to any local process and leaks whether a case holds a given URL). It carries ready-for-agent today; pull the label or answer both before dispatch. One acceptance line should be added: the new shared URL canonicalizer must be added to the Acquisition include list in the evidence-paths assessment at blocking tier, matching the #227 and #400 amendments, or the module that decides which Capture a Tag or Note binds to ships off the backstop list entirely.

**#393.** Three gaps, and it is the worst-prepared ticket in the wave. It carries no evidence-affecting label despite three blocking-tier files, so `pre-pass-gate.yml` keys wrong and it could auto-merge under a rule that should not apply. It carries no ready-for-agent label. And it is the only wave-3 ticket with zero comments, so it has no grounding comment at all. Plus rulings on the Selector one-click-versus-popover contradiction and on whether the branch may extend `captureSuppression.ts` with the page-side latch it needs.

**#391.** Two rulings the ticket's own grounding comment flagged and nobody answered: the Backfill checkbox (the 'honour it' option silently makes the PR evidence-affecting at blocking tier and removes auto-merge eligibility) and the Tag action on an unanchored note. One correction the issue should carry regardless: the grounding comment's instruction to reuse CreateSelectorPopover 'including kind detection' is wrong on both counts (verified: 97 lines, two editable inputs, origin hardcoded 'capture' at :49, no kind detection anywhere in the renderer), and following it reaches `DataExplorer.tsx`, blocking tier.

**#695.** The ready-for-agent label, which the 2026-08-23 comment removed from ready-for-human without putting anything in its place. Acceptance criteria: the body predates the ruling and is written entirely as three open questions. The clear-control scope answer. And two concrete defects the change must fix that appear in neither the body nor the ruling: the empty-state branch keys on `filteredCaptureIds` or `activeFilterCount`, so a zero-match query renders 'No captures yet' on a case full of captures; and `countActiveFilters` feeds the Filter badge, so folding the query into it makes the badge count something absent from the Filter menu.

**#701.** The intersection-versus-expansion ruling, which is the difference between medium and large. Acceptance criteria: the 2026-08-21 comment said none could be written until points 1 and 2 were ruled, they were ruled on 2026-08-23, and nobody went back. The ready-for-agent label. Plus two explicit non-goals worth writing into the criteria: this ticket does not refactor CaptureMenu, CaptureDownloadMenu or ExportMenu onto the new primitive (`CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` is blocking tier and would change the PR's merge path), and it does not build the mock's 'Customise this menu' preference mode, which has no backing store anywhere in the app. Also settle whether the capture menu is multi-select aware, since it changes what Delete destroys.

**#702.** Acceptance criteria and the ready-for-agent label, both a five-minute paste. The body should be corrected to say the REC pill already exists at `TopBar.tsx:118-123` rather than implying it must be built. Write the scope explicitly as: delete the `sessionActive` read and early return at `ConnectionStatus.tsx:6` and :8-10, add `tests/components/ConnectionStatus.test.tsx`, and do not rename the Auto-Capture toggle (that naming collision is a separate issue). Note for the implementer: the edit is a pure deletion contributing zero changed lines, so adding a lone `data-testid` to TopBar without a test that renders TopBar scores 0% on the diff-coverage gate.

**#704.** Acceptance criteria (the body still says 'No acceptance criteria yet, which is why this is needs-triage', and the needs-triage label is not actually on it either) and the ready-for-agent label. Three copy questions: whether the banner appears on the legacy format '`html`' branch, whether it carries the mock's 'scripts and network disabled' claim, and whether it renders the truncated content hash. One instruction that is in neither the issue nor the ruling and that decides the merge gate: mount in `CaptureViewer.tsx` (excluded), do not edit `MhtmlViewer.tsx` (blocking).

**#405.** The ruling's acceptance criterion exists only in a comment; the body still carries the original five and none mentions the demo mark, the export dialog or the Certification. The two blocking questions (operator-name gate, fixture shipping) have no answer anywhere. No ready-for-agent label. #771 must be ruled or declared out of scope first, since both edit `useTourEngine.start()`. Two more facts the issue should absorb: the ten-step case chapter is already built and matches the mock step for step, so this is a fixture-and-import ticket rather than a tour ticket; and `deleteCase` removes only the DB row, so 'removes it cleanly' is not what the code does.

**#803.** A split decision with a merge order, the per-part `SHA-256` ruling, and a disposition for the extracted-data IOC browser this screen deletes (`DataExplorer.tsx` is the only renderer consumer of all five `extractedData` channels, so removing it makes the table and its FTS index write-only). The acceptance criteria need correcting on three points the mock does not support: the search filters the TABLE, not the tree; per-artifact integrity flags cannot be produced because verification is per capture and returns one prose reason; and the tree row height should read var(--d-tree) rather than the mock's self-contradictory 21px. The criteria should also state that the Headers and TLS tab must not present request headers the app never captured and must not label the TLS chain 'at capture time', which ADR-0002 already forbids.

**#708.** It carries needs-triage, not ready-for-agent, and has no acceptance criteria. Two answers needed: the deliverable's form and path, and whether all 37 wave-2 constraints are in scope or only the subset where design has a decision to make. The criteria should require the six missing ruled items (rulings 1, 2, 5, 6, 7 and the #704 candidate), require every template citation re-verified against the canonical unpack (the #702 ruling comment's are off by +393), and require the V2-bundle provenance item: three of the six branch-only prose documents also sit on main in the superseded V1 bundle with different blobs, so a reader who greps finds the older text with no signal it is stale.

## Phase 2 recommendation

**Effort.** Tiered, three tiers, and the tiering is now grounded in thirteen readers rather than in a guess, which is exactly the condition the prep doc set when it deferred this. max for #398, #399 and #803. high for #392, #393, #401, #405, #695, #391 and #701. medium for #702, #704 and #708.

The prep doc rejected tiering for phase 1 because it rested on the maintainer's guess about which tickets were easy, and #699 was that morning's evidence such a guess can be wrong in the expensive direction. Phase 1 has now measured it, and the measurement moved four tickets in both directions: #702 is one three-line deletion because the REC pill already ships; #701 drops from large to medium because radix-ui is already a production dependency; #399 and #803 both rose to very-large. The three max tickets each carry an unresolved mechanism plus a very-large or deep-serial diff, and all three are blocking-tier with human review, so an under-resourced implementer costs a full review round on the critical path. The three medium tickets are each a single file plus a test, fully ruled, with no open mechanism. The middle tier is where the readers found real but bounded difficulty: a security posture to write (#401), a page-gesture suppression latch (#393), a wire contract (#392), a bundled fixture and its import path (#405), and three renderer builds with one open decision each. Do not run phase 2 uniformly at max: it would spend the most on #702, which is a deletion, and buy nothing.

**Actually hard:** #398, #399, #803, #392, #401, #405, #393

**Actually cheap:** #702, #704, #708, #695, #391, #701

**Shape.** One agent per ticket in its own worktree, three concurrent (ADR-0014 WIP), every branch cut from main and never from another cycle's branch. Track A is strictly serial and is the wave's whole critical path: #398 then #399 then #401 then #405, each branch cut after the previous merges. Track B is #392 then #393 then #803's three main-side splits. Track C runs the cheap renderer work in order: #702, #695, #391, #701, #704, then #803's menu split. Serialization points a textual merge will not protect: `evidencePackage.ts` and `schemas.ts` between #398 and #399, `ExportDialog.tsx` between #399 and #401, `caseRepo.importCaseRow` between #399 and #405, and #701's registry before #803's four deferred kinds. Seven PRs carry the evidence gate once #393 is relabelled (#392, #393, #398, #399, #401, #405, #803): none auto-merge and all get human review. #702, #704 and #708 are each under an hour and should share one slot sequentially rather than each taking a cycle. Answer the three track-A blocking questions before dispatching any of #398, #399 or #401, or the track stalls three separate times.

## Defects filed

Found by the readers and covered by no wave-3 ticket. All filed on 2026-08-23, labels verified by a
direct label read rather than the lagging search index. Finding 11 turned out to be already tracked
and was not refiled.

| Finding | Issue |
| --- | --- |
| 1. `captureSuppression.ts` and `captureHygiene.ts` on neither tier | #807 |
| 2. `signals/**` and `CaptureItem.tsx` on neither list | #808 |
| 3. No blanket `src/main/services/**` or `src/shared/**` entry | #809 |
| 4. `mhtml-sandbox` partition has no permission handler | #810 |
| 5. `tagRepo.createTag` bare INSERT against a UNIQUE column | #811 |
| 6. `CaptureList` per-capture IPC and no windowing | #812 |
| 7. Top-bar Auto-Capture toggle sets `sessionActive` | #813 |
| 8. `SessionControls` hides the only stop control | #814 |
| 9. `sessionActive` never hydrated at renderer mount | #815 |
| 10. `deleteCase` leaves the case directory on disk | #816 |
| 11. Case tour first coach mark has no anchor | already tracked as #751 |
| 12. Design-handoff README points at a missing file | #818 |
| 13. Token guard covers only POSTs registered after the middleware | #817 |

### 1. Evidence path list: `extension/src/`captureSuppression`.ts` and `captureHygiene.ts` are on neither tier

Suggested labels: bug, evidence-affecting, documentation. Found by: #393 reader.

Both were created by #386, which closed 2026-08-13, after the 2026-07-31 survey, so nobody added them. They are the two files that decide whether injected UI reaches captured bytes, which makes them strictly more evidence-load-bearing than `extension/src/toast.ts`, which IS listed blocking. #393 will edit both. This does not change #393's tier (`content.ts` and `background.ts` already do that) but it is a hole that will not close itself.

### 2. Evidence path list: src/renderer/components/signals/** and `captures/CaptureItem.tsx` appear in neither the include nor the exclusion list

Suggested labels: documentation, evidence-affecting. Found by: #701 reader.

The signals directory was created by wave 2, after the 2026-07-31 survey. Absence from the include list means no backstop hit, so it does not change any ticket's tier, but the review record is silent where it should say 'renderer chrome, excluded'. #701 and #695 both edit these paths. Same root cause as the `captureSuppression` gap: the survey has no refresh trigger.

### 3. Evidence path list has no blanket src/main/services/** or src/shared/** entry, so new main-process services ship off the backstop

Suggested labels: enhancement, evidence-affecting. Found by: #392 and #803 readers.

The include list names individual files. #392's new shared URL canonicalizer decides which Capture a Tag or Note binds to and matches no glob; #803's three new main-process readers (artifact inventory, MHTML part enumeration, manifest ledger) match none either. The same argument added `urlPatterns.ts` in #227 and `exclusionPolicy.ts` in #400, each by hand. A structural answer, such as default-blocking for new files under src/main/services/ until triaged out, would stop the pattern repeating once per wave.

### 4. mhtml-sandbox webview partition has no permission or `webRequest` handler, so MHTML subresources can still be fetched

Suggested labels: bug, evidence-affecting. Found by: #704 reader.

MhtmlViewer sets javascript=no so scripts are genuinely off, and `index.ts` blocks post-initial-load will-navigate, but nothing configures the mhtml-sandbox partition anywhere in src/, and will-navigate does not fire for subresources. A non-inlined `img` or stylesheet in an MHTML would still be fetched from the network while rendering evidence. Found while checking whether #704 could ship the mock's 'scripts and network disabled' banner copy; it cannot, and the fix lands in `src/main/index.ts`, blocking tier, so it was deliberately kept out of that renderer-chrome ticket.

### 5. `tagRepo.createTag` is a bare INSERT against a UNIQUE column, so creating a tag whose name already exists throws

Suggested labels: bug. Found by: #391 reader.

tags.name is TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE and `tagRepo.createTag` has no ON CONFLICT. `useTagsMutations()`.create and `useCaptureTagEditor.createTag` both inherit the gap. Any create-tag-from-selection flow (#391's Tag action, and #393's, which slugifies the selected text) hits it the moment the derived name collides. Found while scoping #391; the workaround there is to look the name up first, but the repo function is the real defect.

### 6. CaptureList fires one captures:`getMatchingSelectors` IPC call per capture at mount and renders every row unvirtualized

Suggested labels: bug, performance. Found by: #695 reader.

The `useQueries` block is enabled unconditionally per capture id, and the row map has no windowing, under AnimatePresence `popLayout` with layout on every child. At a few thousand captures that is a few thousand concurrent IPC round trips at mount. Found while confirming #695's ruling premise (the list genuinely is fully loaded, so the client-side filter is correct); #695 strictly improves both costs once a query is typed and should not be widened to fix them.

### 7. Top-bar toggle labelled Auto-Capture actually toggles `sessionActive`, colliding with the Signals auto-capture setting

Suggested labels: bug, redesign. Found by: #702 reader.

SessionControls renders label and `aria-label` 'Auto-Capture' and CapturesGettingStarted tells the operator to 'Toggle Auto-Capture on in the header bar to start recording', but the control sets `sessionActive`; the actual `autoCaptureMode` setting is written by AutoCaptureCard on the Signals screen. After #702 the top bar reads Auto-Capture toggle, REC, Connected, and the #702 ruling's own sentence that auto-capture is a Signals concern reads as contradicted on screen. Kept out of #702 because the rename needs a copy decision (Record, Session, Capture Session) that no wave-3 ruling has answered.

### 8. SessionControls hides the only stop control when the extension heartbeat lapses mid-session

Suggested labels: bug. Found by: #702 reader.

SessionControls returns `null` when the extension is not connected, but `sessionActive` stays `true` in main, so the REC pill keeps rendering and the operator has no in-app way to stop the session. Pre-existing and out of #702's scope, but it is the strongest argument for #702's ruling: once the Connected chip stops hiding, the operator at least learns why the toggle vanished.

### 9. `sessionActive` is never hydrated at renderer mount, so the Dashboard reads Idle after a reload during an active session

Suggested labels: bug. Found by: #702 reader.

`sessionQueryOptions` has zero consumers anywhere in src/, tests/ or e2e/. After a renderer reload the store reads `false` regardless of main's state. It self-heals on entering any case route because `activateCase` notifies with the `true` value, so the uncovered window is the Dashboard. Found while confirming that #702's indicator is bound to a live field, which it is; the fix belongs in `useServerStatus` and not in `src/main/services/session.ts`, which is blocking tier.

### 10. `deleteCase` removes the DB row but no case directory, so a deleted case leaves its artifacts and manifest on disk

Suggested labels: bug, evidence-affecting. Found by: #405 reader.

`caseRepo.deleteCase` runs a single DELETE. No service removes the case directory. #405's Delete-demo ending is specified as 'removes it cleanly' and would leave the bundled fixture's `mhtml`, `png`, `txt` and manifest bytes behind. Also relevant to any operator who deletes a real case expecting the evidence to be gone.

### 11. Case tour's first coach mark has no anchor: CaseWorkspace clears `selectedCaptureId` on entry, so the `viewertabs` target never mounts

Suggested labels: bug. Found by: #405 reader.

CaptureViewer returns early before the tablist when no capture is selected, and CaseWorkspace clears `selectedCaptureId` on every case entry. The step therefore renders centred as an `anchorMissing` mark even on a fully populated case. Shipped by #404 and independent of #405's demo Case, so it is a live defect on any case tour today.

### 12. `docs/design-handoff/README.md` points at a round-trip protocol file the current bundle does not contain, and three V2 documents exist on main as stale V1 copies

Suggested labels: documentation. Found by: #708 reader.

README:14 sends readers to 'each bundle's `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`', but the 2026-08-21 standalone bundle contains only the HTML and a README. Separately, `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`, `github.md` and `style_sync_patch/SCREEN_NOTES.md` all exist both on origin/prototype/design-handoff-2026-08 and on main under the superseded 2026-08-10 bundle, with different blobs, so a reader who greps the repo finds the older text with no signal it is stale. Kept out of #708 to hold that diff to the corrections register itself.

### 13. Capture-server token guard covers only POSTs registered after the middleware, so a GET or PUT write route would be unauthenticated

Suggested labels: bug, evidence-affecting. Found by: #392 reader.

The middleware tests c.req.method === 'POST' and Hono only runs middleware registered before the matching route. Nothing in the code prevents a future route being registered above the guard or using a non-POST verb for a write; the failure would be an unauthenticated write endpoint on loopback with no test catching it. Not a live defect today, but the prep doc asserts unconditional coverage and #392 is about to add three routes on that assumption. Worth either a route-table test asserting every state-changing route answers 401 without the header, or broadening the guard.

## What phase 1 could not settle

Nothing was read at low confidence, and that uniformity is itself worth noting: all thirteen readers self-reported high confidence, which is not a distribution I would expect across tickets ranging from a three-line deletion to a screen rewrite. Treat the thin spots below as the real error bars rather than the confidence field. Nobody executed anything. Every finding in this synthesis is a static read plus, in one case, a probe of `node_modules`; no reader ran `pnpm` test, `pnpm` typecheck or `pnpm` build, so no size estimate has been calibrated against a real diff. The #803 range of 1500 to 2500 lines in particular has no comparable merged branch behind it. Three genuinely open technical questions could be closed by a spike rather than by the maintainer, and were not. Whether archive.org replays render usefully with JavaScript off is a twenty-minute experiment that would collapse #401's biggest question into an implementation detail. Whether Radix's default modal pointer-events lock behaves acceptably under Electron is untested, and it is the one thing that could turn #701's Radix recommendation back into a hand-roll. And whether the `jsdom` stubs Radix menus need (`hasPointerCapture`, `releasePointerCapture`, `scrollIntoView`) are sufficient is unknown, since the only Radix component in the tree today is Tabs, which needs none of them. Two evidence questions remain genuinely undecided rather than merely unanswered. The #398 trusted-scope source has a recommendation (`export-entry.json` outside `packageHash`) that nobody has prototyped, so its interaction with the existing `rollbackManifestEntry` ordering is asserted, not shown. And the CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION reasoning for #399's demo flag is contested: the ruling stands on a wave-1 adjudication about a display-only column, and the #399 reader argues the demo flag is the 'silently discard' limb the constant's own comment describes. The ruling can survive that, but it should survive on the demo flag's own merits. Finally, #708's 37 wave-2 constraints were not individually re-checked against main; exactly two were (C11, fixed in `selectorRepo`; C8, answered by ruling 4), and both turned out to be already closed, which suggests the remaining 35 hold more stale items than one. That check is mechanical and belongs inside #708's own work.

## Appendix: per-ticket reader notes

### #398 feat(export): selection-scoped export with scope Manifest fields and verifier reconciliation

**Verdict.** Backend-only export scoping whose real difficulty is not the manifest field design (that is three lines of conditional spread plus a Zod optional) but the fact that the package's own bundled `manifest.jsonl` deliberately does not contain the export entry that would carry `captureIds`, so the standalone verifier has no signed source for the scope it is asked to reconcile against.

Size: large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against origin/main at 0bf207e5 the work splits into four parts, only two of which the prep doc anticipates.

1) Field design (small, already patterned). `ManifestEntryInput`'s `export` variant is `src/main/services/manifest.ts:291-303` (the `type: 'export'` literal at :295, closing brace at :303). `appendManifestEntry` (:352) builds `{...entry, index, prevHash, schemaVersion}` at :357-362, canonicalizes at :363 and sha256s at :364 - the prep doc's line cite is exact. The house omission pattern is `CaptureEntryContext` at `manifest.ts:455-482` with conditional spreads at :502-512 and `reason` at :449. `canonicalStringify` drops only `undefined` (`src/shared/verify/`canonicalJson.ts`:33`); `null`, `''` and `[]` all survive as canonical bytes (:21-22, :24-29). So the "omit, never null/''/[]" rule is correct at the writer.

2) Zod, which the prep doc omits entirely and which is where legacy chains actually break. `ManifestExportEntrySchema` is `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389` and it is `.strict()`. Two mandatory constraints follow: the new keys must be `.optional()` with NO `.default()`, and they must be added to that schema or every new export entry fails `safeParse` and `verifyManifestChainText` returns 'Invalid entry shape'. A `.default()` would be the actual chain-breaker: `src/shared/verify/`manifestChain.ts`:123` destructures the Zod OUTPUT (`schemaResult.data`, pushed at :86), so a default-injected key lands in `body` and is hashed at :130, producing 'Entry hash mismatch' on every legacy export entry.

3) Artifact membership (medium). `generateReport` (`src/main/services/export.ts:243`) loads the whole case at :260 and threads `captures` into `ExportData` at :273; `buildEvidenceZip` (:413) maps `data.captures` at :482 to build pages/, screenshots/, timestamps/ and `evidence.json`. A capture-id scope filters at :260 and nowhere else, but `resolveUnreconciledChainCaptures` (:210-229) then reports every unselected capture as an unreconciled chain orphan into `evidence.json` warnings (:573-575) and `report.html` (`reportHtml.ts:678-693`). That is the #580 tamper/loss signal and it must not fire for a deliberate selection.

4) Verifier reconciliation (the hard part). `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage`.ts` derives the active-capture set from the chain at :119-126 and, for each one, FAILS when pages/{id}.mhtml is absent (:160-161); §7.5 coverage FAILs both directions between chain ids and `evidence.json` ids (:258-296). A selection package trips both for every unselected capture. `tests/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.test.ts:188`-214` already pins that behaviour as `pass === false`, so the change must scope §7.3/§7.5 without weakening real orphan detection - which needs a trusted source for the selection, and that is the blocking question below. `src/main/services/`verifyRunbook.ts:136`-137` tells a by-hand reviewer to sha256 `pages/$id.mhtml` for every capture line, so that recipe needs the same qualification.

Reusable as-is: the conditional-spread omission idiom, the `undefined`-filtering canonicalizer, `withManifestEntry`/`rollbackManifestEntry`, `EvidencePackageSchema` (`schemas.ts:486-505`, deliberately not `.strict()` per the comment at :462-469, so `evidence.json` can gain informational keys freely), and `selectedCaptureIds` in `src/renderer/stores/`appStore.ts`:13`. Must be written: the scoped §7.3/§7.5 logic, the report/runbook copy, and a frozen pre-scope fixture package (none exists; tests/fixtures holds only timestamp material).

`src/verifier/cli.ts` likely needs no edit at all - its GOLDEN_BODY (:22-51) is a capture entry and the behaviour change lives entirely in `evidencePackage.ts`, which `cli.ts` imports at :15.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Blocking, and by a wide margin. The assessment lists `manifest.ts` blocking at line 194, src/shared/verify/** at 202, src/verifier/** at 203, `export.ts` at 236, `reportHtml.ts` at 238 and `verifyRunbook.ts` at 240. `types.ts` (280), `schemas.ts` (281) and `ipcHandlers.ts` (277) are advisory and add nothing. The ticket also already carries the evidence-affecting label, which per the tiers section (spec lines 87-89) owes the full ADR-0004 obligations whatever the diff touches. So: Evidence impact section, known-answer test, preserved backward verification, human review, no auto-merge. Prep doc agrees, no correction needed. Worth flagging separately, though not a tier change: the single highest-consequence edit in this ticket is the Zod optional in `src/shared/schemas.ts`, and that file is tiered advisory. A hypothetical unlabelled PR that changed only that line could inject a .default() and break every legacy export entry's hash while the backstop produced a one-line disposition. The label saves this particular ticket; it does not save the next one.

Paths: `src/main/services/manifest.ts`, `src/main/services/export.ts`, `src/shared/verify/evidencePackage.ts`, `src/verifier/cli.ts`, `src/main/services/reportHtml.ts`, `src/main/services/verifyRunbook.ts`, `src/shared/types.ts`, `src/shared/schemas.ts`, `src/main/ipcHandlers.ts`

**Files.**

If a helper wraps the call, use the conditional-spread idiom at :502-512. |
