**Mock findings.**

- The export dialog carries a Scope row with an icon, a label and a monospace manifest note directly beneath it. Values are computed at 15100-15123: `expScopeLabel` is either 'N selected captures' or 'Whole case - 8 captures', and `expManifestNote` is "manifest entry -> scope: 'selection' - `captureIds`[N]" or `manifest entry -> scope: 'case'`. (template 12723-12728 (markup), 15100-15123 (values), 12942 and 15114 (`expScope` defaults to 'case')). The app's ExportDialog has no Scope row (`src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx:21-74` takes only `caseId`, `caseName`, `onClose`). The mock prints scope: 'case' explicitly for a case export; the 2026-08-23 ruling on #398 says omit the field entirely in that case. Follow the ruling for the on-disk entry. The visible copy is #399's ticket ('A note under the Scope row states what the manifest records for selections'), so #398 should not build this row.
- The mock's export content checklist offers a 'Ledger slice' item described as '16 entries covering the selection'. (template 15055 (the ITEMS row), 15045-15062 (surrounding list)). Directly contradicts ADR-0009, which rejects the slice by name and requires the complete chain regardless of scope. Diverge from the mock. ADR-0009 records that this constraint 'goes back to design per the handoff's own review contract', so it belongs on the #708 corrections list if it is not there already.
- The Captures screen selection toolbar has an 'Export selection' button that opens the export dialog pre-set to selection scope. (template 10045 (toolbar), 10053 (button), 13811 (`capExportSel` sets `expScope`: 'selection')). The app already ships this button at `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureSelectionBar.tsx:150-160`, but its tooltip reads 'Export case' with a comment at :152-155 stating there is no selection-scoped export yet. Follow the mock once the backend exists; the rewiring belongs with #399's dialog work, not #398.
- The mock's checklist names the manifest artifact '`manifest.json`'. (template 15056). The app ships `manifest.jsonl` (`export.ts:450`). Cosmetic mock error; keep the app's name.

**Conflicts.**

- #399 in `src/shared/verify/evidencePackage.ts` at 156-237 (7.3 per-capture binding) and 256-296 (7.5 coverage), serialize. #398 first. #399's AC 2 needs the verifier to report a Working Copy as 'not a verifiable object' rather than FAIL, which is a decision made in the same two blocks #398 rewrites to scope the capture set. The prep doc asserts #398 is alone in the verifier; it is not. Merge #398, then rebase #399.
- #399 in `src/main/services/export.ts` at 243-405 (`generateReport`) and 413-618 (`buildEvidenceZip`), serialize. #398 first, as the prep doc says. #399 adds the export classes and certification wiring on top of the scoped capture set, and the two-classes work has to know whether a Working Copy appends an export entry at all.
- #399 in `src/shared/types.ts` at ExportOptions at 618-628 (#398) vs the Case interface and `case_number` (#399), append-only. Different regions of the same file. Second to land resolves. Advisory tier.
- #401 in `src/main/services/reportHtml.ts` at #398 near the unreconciled disclosure at 678-693 and the ExportData field at 155-158; #401 adds pinned-wayback rendering, and the file contains zero occurrences of wayback today, append-only. Genuinely disjoint regions, as the prep doc says. Whichever lands second rebases. Both are blocking tier so both get human review regardless.
- #399 in `src/shared/schemas.ts` at ManifestExportEntrySchema at 373-389 (#398) vs any `case_number` archive schema (#399), disjoint. No overlap found. #399's `case_number` rides the v31 migration and `caseArchive`, not the manifest entry schemas.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) "`appendManifestEntry` canonicalizes {...entry, index, `prevHash`, `schemaVersion`} through `canonicalStringify` and hashes that (`manifest.ts:357-364`), so an always-present field rewrites every legacy entry's canonical body and breaks the chain on existing packages."
  - Correction: The line cite is exactly right and the conclusion (omit the fields) is right, but the stated mechanism is wrong and it points implementers at the wrong file. Adding a key to the TypeScript input type and always setting it at the call site cannot touch entries already on disk: verification re-canonicalizes each entry from its own parsed JSON (`manifestChain.ts:123` destructures, :130 hashes), so a legacy entry keeps its bytes and its hash. The two ways this change really can break legacy chains both live in `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389`, which the prep doc never names. (a) Marking the new keys required makes every legacy export entry fail ManifestExportEntrySchema, and `verifyManifestChainText` returns broken(i, 'Invalid entry shape'). (b) Giving them a Zod .default() injects the key into `schemaResult.data`, which is what `manifestChain.ts:86` pushes and :123 destructures, so `canonicalStringify` at :130 hashes a key the writer never wrote and every legacy export entry returns 'Entry hash mismatch'. The writer-side omission rule survives for a different reason: writing scope: 'case' produces a new on-disk shape that the .strict() at `schemas.ts:389` in already-distributed verifier binaries rejects, whereas omitting it keeps case exports byte-identical to today's.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/verify/`manifestChain.ts:82`-86` (`safeParse` then push data), :123 (destructure), :130 (hash); `src/shared/verify/`canonicalJson.ts`:33` (filters `undefined` only); `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389` (.strict(), no defaults)
- (changes-the-plan) "src/shared/verify/** and src/verifier/**. #398 alone. No other ticket touches the verifier."
  - Correction: False. #399's acceptance criterion 2 reads 'Working Copy output is visibly non-evidentiary and the standalone verifier says "not a verifiable object" rather than FAIL'. That is a behaviour change in `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage`.ts` and probably `src/verifier/cli.ts`, in the same region #398 rewrites (7.3/7.5). The two tickets contend on the verifier, not just on `export.ts`, which makes the #398-then-#399 serialization tighter than the prep doc represents.
  - Evidence: `gh` issue view 399 body, AC 2; the region is `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.ts:156`-237` and :256-296
- (changes-the-plan) The prep doc and the ruling comment's "Starting files" list `manifest.ts`, `export.ts`, `evidencePackage.ts`, `verifier/cli.ts` and `reportHtml.ts`.
  - Correction: Two blocking-tier files are missing and both are mandatory. `src/shared/schemas.ts:373-389` is where the field is actually accepted or rejected (see the first correction). `src/main/services/`verifyRunbook.ts:136`-137` instructs a by-hand reviewer to sha256 pages/$id.mhtml for every capture line in the manifest, which is guaranteed to fail on a selection package; that file is listed blocking at assessment line 240 and ships inside every package.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/schemas.ts:389`; `src/main/services/`verifyRunbook.ts`:67`, :136-137; `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:240`
- (changes-the-plan) ADR-0009: "Package Verification is untouched: one chain, one walk, existing semantics."
  - Correction: Not `true` against this tree. `verifyEvidencePackage` derives active captures from the chain (`evidencePackage.ts:119-126`) and emits a hard FAIL for each one whose pages/{id}.mhtml is absent (:160-161), and 7.5 coverage FAILs in both directions between chain ids and `evidence.json` ids (:258-296). A selection package trips both for every unselected capture. A test already asserts exactly this shape is pass === false. The ADR's own last bullet ('the unsigned index/report must reconcile against the selection') and #398's AC 3 ('standalone verifier reconciles artifacts against `captureIds`') also disagree with each other about whether the verifier or only the index changes.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.ts:157`-166`, :258-296; `tests/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.test.ts:188`-194` asserts result.pass === `false` for a chain capture missing from the package
- (cosmetic) "The file already documents this pattern four times for `screenshotHash`, headers, `tls` and method."
  - Correction: Undercount. `manifest.ts` carries five comment blocks covering six optional fields (`screenshotHash` and `textHash` at :461-464, headers at :465-467, `tls` at :468-470, method at :471-473, `consentSuppression` at :475-477), and the conditional-spread idiom appears seven times at :502-512 plus once for `reason` at :449. Immaterial to the design, but an implementer told 'four' may stop reading at method and miss the two-fields-one-comment case.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/manifest.ts:449`, :461-477, :502-512
- (cosmetic) "its call site at `src/main/services/export.ts:387-395`"
  - Correction: The object literal runs 387-396; `verificationResult` is at :395 and the closing `})` at :396. It is the only `appendManifestEntry`({type:'export'}) call site in the tree, which the prep doc gets right.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/export.ts:387-396`; grep for `appendManifestEntry` in `export.ts` returns only :16 (import) and :387

**Test plan.** Known-answer canonicalization, extending `tests/shared/verify/`canonicalJson.test`.ts`. That file already holds the frozen golden vector at :89-127 and, at :131, the exact precedent this ticket needs: `canonicalStringify({...GOLDEN_BODY, screenshotHash: undefined})` must equal GOLDEN_CANONICAL. Add the export-entry analogue - a frozen scoped export body with its expected canonical string, plus the assertion that the same body with scope and `captureIds` `undefined` canonicalizes to the unscoped bytes. That single pair is the ticket's AC 2.

Legacy-hash stability, in `tests/shared/verify/`manifestChain.test`.ts` (226 lines today). Hand `verifyManifestChainText` a hardcoded JSONL string containing a pre-scope export entry with its real `entryHash` and assert valid === `true` after the schema change. This is the test that catches a stray Zod .default(), which no other test in the suite would catch: the round-trip tests all write and read with the same code.

Backward verification proven, not asserted. The repository has no committed pre-scope package today - tests/fixtures holds only timestamp material (`content-hash.txt`, digicert-*.der/.tsr/.pem, request.tsq) and every package in `tests/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.test`.ts` is generated live through `generateReport`. So freeze one at 0bf207e5: `manifest.jsonl`, signing-public-key.pem, `evidence.json`, pages/, screenshots/, timestamps/, `VERIFY.md`. It is self-contained because `verifyEvidencePackage` reads the public key from the package itself (`evidencePackage.ts:86`, :98), so no test-key plumbing is needed. Assert PASS byte-for-byte unchanged, and run it through the built binary too - `tests/verifier/binary.test.ts:157` already has the 'exits 0 with a PASS report on a good package' shape to copy.

Verifier scoping, in `tests/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.test`.ts` (672 lines). Add: a selection package PASSes with unselected captures reported SKIP rather than FAIL; a selection package with a SELECTED capture's `mhtml` removed still FAILs. And keep the existing orphan test at :165-214 red - it asserts pass === `false` when the chain claims a capture the package lacks, and it must keep asserting that for a genuine orphan. That test is the regression proving the scoping did not blunt #580. Same for the deletion-coverage FAIL at :431.

End to end export, in `tests/main/services/export.test.ts` (1633 lines). Selection export of a case with deletions: the zip's `manifest.jsonl` is byte-identical to the full live manifest minus the one lagging entry, pages/ contains only the selection, the deletion entries are present and the deleted capture is visible as deleted, and `evidence.json`'s `unreconciledChainCaptureCount` is not inflated by the selection (the assertions at :275-320 are the pattern).

Manifest append, in `tests/main/services/manifest.test.ts` (1223 lines): scoped entry round-trips through append then read then verify.

Import hygiene: `tests/verifier/`importHygiene.test`.ts` and `tests/shared/verify/`importHygiene.test`.ts` both stay green - relevant only if anything new is imported into src/verifier or src/shared/verify.

E2E: there is no export spec in e2e/ today. #399's AC 6 asks for one; #398 does not, and adding one here would collide with #399. Skip it.

Full loop per the background-jobs carve-out: `pnpm` lint, `pnpm` typecheck (six projects), BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 `pnpm` test, `pnpm` build, `pnpm` build:verifier, then `pnpm` test:coverage and `pnpm` coverage:diff on the same tree state.

**Open questions.**

- The package's own bundled `manifest.jsonl` does not contain the export entry describing that package. `export.ts:346` snapshots the manifest, :450 adds that snapshot to the zip, and the export entry is only appended at :387 - after the zip is written, deliberately, so `packageHash` cannot cover the entry that quotes it (the comment at :374-379 spells this out and calls the one-entry lag acceptable). AC 3 says the standalone verifier reconciles artifacts against `captureIds`. Where does the verifier read a TRUSTED `captureIds` from?
  - Why blocking: It decides the package format, and every other part of the ticket sits downstream of it. An implementer who does not notice will write the manifest field, wire the artifact filter, then discover at the verifier step that the signed source is not in the package, and will most likely reach for `evidence.json` - which `evidencePackage.ts:132-133` and :14-17 declare untrusted, and which would let a tamperer add ids to `captureIds` to explain away a capture they removed. That is precisely the #580 detection the orphan test at `tests/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.test.ts:188`-194` exists to hold. Guessing here produces a package that verifies and should not.
  - Recommendation: Ship the signed export entry as its own package member. Restructure `generateReport` so `buildEvidenceZip` returns entries plus `packageHash` without writing, then append the manifest entry (rolling back via `rollbackManifestEntry` on failure, which already exists), then `unshift` the full signed line as `export-entry.json` exactly the way `evidence.json` is `unshifted` at `export.ts:593-596` so it stays outside artifacts and outside `packageHash`. The verifier then checks that file's signature against the bundled signing-public-key.pem, checks its `prevHash` equals the bundled chain head, recomputes its `entryHash`, and only then trusts its `captureIds`. That keeps the trust model intact, preserves the no-circularity ordering the existing comment protects, and needs `appendManifestEntry` to return the signature alongside the AppendResult it already returns at :372. The cheaper alternative - reconcile against `evidence.json` and label the scope untrusted - is not defensible for an evidence-affecting change and should be rejected explicitly rather than by default.
- ADR-0009 says The export Manifest Entry schema gains scope and (for selections) `captureIds`, that is scope always present. The 2026-08-23 ruling comment on #398 says the opposite: "A case-scoped export could reasonably carry scope: 'case' explicitly or omit it. Omit it." Which governs, and does ADR-0009 get amended in this PR?
  - Why blocking: They produce different bytes for every case-scoped export from now on, and the choice is unrecoverable once packages are in the world. It also decides whether the TypeScript type is `scope?: 'selection'` or `scope?: 'case' | 'selection'`, and whether the mock's `expManifestNote` copy (`template.html:15123`, which prints scope: 'case') describes something real.
  - Recommendation: Follow the ruling comment - it is dated later and it is right for the reason it gives: an omitted scope is the only shape where a new case-scoped package and a pre-#398 package produce identical entry bytes, which is what makes the frozen fixture pass without a special case. Type it `scope?: 'selection'`. Amend ADR-0009's consequences bullet in the same PR so the ADR and the code do not disagree in the record, and let #399's dialog copy keep saying "scope: case" as plain-language description of an omitted field, or reword it to `whole case`.
- ManifestExportEntrySchema is .strict() (`schemas.ts:389`), so a verifier binary built before this change returns 'Invalid entry shape' - a whole-chain FAIL - on any manifest containing a scoped export entry. Since a case's manifest accumulates export entries, the very next case-scoped export after one selection export ships a chain that older binaries cannot read. Is that accepted, and does MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION (currently 2, `src/shared/constants.ts:53`) bump?
  - Why blocking: It is a field-design decision, not a release-note decision. Bumping to 3 makes the failure explicit but not milder - `schemaVersionField` at `schemas.ts:250` caps at MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION, so an old binary rejects a v3 entry just as hard - while not bumping leaves the version number lying about the shape. Either way the answer has to be settled before the schema edit is written, and it feeds the Evidence impact section.
  - Recommendation: Do not bump. The entry stays `schemaVersion` 2 and .strict() is the honest forward-compat boundary; a bump buys a worse failure message for the same outcome and would additionally invalidate every unrelated v2 entry type against an old binary. Record the consequence plainly in the Evidence impact section and add a line to `verifyRunbook.ts` and the release notes: verify packages with the binary shipped in or after this release. The receiving side always has the runbook's jq/openssl path, which is version-agnostic.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: The issue is well specified on intent and the ruling comment is unusually good on the field design. What it does not contain is an answer to where the verifier gets a trusted `captureIds` from, and the ticket cannot be finished without one because AC 3 depends on it. AC 3 also contradicts ADR-0009's claim that package verification is untouched, and ADR-0009 contradicts the ruling comment on whether scope: 'case' is written. Add: (a) the maintainer's ruling on the signed-scope-source question, ideally naming `export-entry.json` or rejecting it; (b) an amended ADR-0009 or an explicit note that the ruling comment supersedes its consequences bullet; (c) an acceptance criterion for the frozen pre-scope fixture, since none exists in the tree today and 'run the verifier against a pre-scope package' currently has no package to run against.

### #399 feat(export): export dialog with two classes, presets, and extended Certification

**Verdict.** This is not a dialog restyle: it is a new export-class concept that reaches from a v31 migration through `certification.ts`, `export.ts` and `evidence.json` out to the standalone verifier, and the mock models none of the class distinction it asks for.

Size: very-large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Verified against 0bf207e5.

VERSION CONSTANTS (both prep-doc claims confirmed `true`). `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION = 30` at `src/main/services/db/core.ts:6`. The migrations tail IS the v30 block: `if (version < 30) {` at `migrations.ts:612`, closing at 636, file ends at 637. `CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION = 3` at `caseArchive.ts:64`. `tests/main/services/database.test.ts:448` asserts `user_version === LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`, so bumping the constant without a matching block fails fast.

THE v31 BLOCK. Insert at `migrations.ts:636`, styled on the v29 (605-610) and v30 (612-636) blocks: a `db.transaction(() => { ... db.pragma('user_version = 31') })()` wrapper, a comment naming the ticket and justifying nullability, one `db.exec` with both ALTERs, no index. Recommended DDL:
  ALTER TABLE cases ADD COLUMN `case_number` TEXT;
  ALTER TABLE cases ADD COLUMN `is_demo` INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;
`case_number TEXT` nullable with no default follows v29's `origin TEXT` and v30's `exclusions TEXT` exactly: NULL means the operator never set one, and '' would be a claim nobody made. `is_demo` is the opposite case and deserves the opposite treatment: the v29 comment rejects a backfill because 'manual' would be a `false` provenance claim, but backfilling every pre-v31 case with 0 is a TRUE claim (nothing before this migration was ever seeded as a demo), so `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` is warranted and matches the v23 precedent at `migrations.ts:457` (`method TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'extension'`). It also gives `rowToCase` a total field rather than a tri-state.

WHAT ALREADY EXISTS AND CAN BE REUSED. `certification.ts` already carries Operator name/role/organisation (106-111), tool name/version, hash algorithm, TSA identity, export timestamp, case name, and a signature/date block (288-294). `buildCertificationFields` / `renderCertificationHtml` are cleanly split, so the extension is additive fields plus new rendered rows, not a rewrite. The certification is added through `add()` (`export.ts:452-464`) so it is inside `artifacts` and therefore covered by `packageHash`; that only affects packages built after this ticket, never existing ones. `evidence.json` is deliberately NOT covered by `packageHash` (`export.ts:592-600`, `unshifted` rather than `add`ed), and `EvidencePackageSchema` (`schemas.ts:486-505`) is a non-strict `z.object`, so new keys there are stripped on parse and cannot break the verifier. Those two facts make `evidence.json` the cheap place to record class/case number/demo status.

WHAT MUST BE WRITTEN. (1) `Case` (`types.ts:7-15`) gains `caseNumber?: string` and `isDemo: boolean`; `UpdateCaseParams` (`ipc.ts:307-312`) gains `caseNumber`. Note the deliberate precedent at `types.ts:28-30`: exclusions were kept OFF `Case` because they are read by one screen. Case number and demo status are read on the dashboard and in export, so they belong ON `Case`. (2) `caseRepo.ts`: `rowToCase` (123-133) maps both; `updateCase` (32-48) writes `case_number`; `importCaseRow` (148-169) needs both added to its explicit column list with `?? null` / `?? 0` and a comment in the style of 163-166. `collectCaseRow` (140-146) is `SELECT *` and needs NO change. (3) `certification.ts` gains case number, contents summary, chain root hash, purpose-or-authority, and a demo-case statement. (4) `export.ts` must branch on export class in `buildEvidenceZip` (413-617) and in the manifest-append at 369-401. (5) `ExportDialog.tsx` (202 lines) is replaced wholesale. (6) `reportHtml.ts:462` renders `Investigator (self-asserted)` beside `Operator (self-asserted)` at 463 - the Investigator vestige is what 'Operator vocabulary throughout' means concretely, and `investigatorName` threads through ExportOptions (`types.ts:626`), `export.ts:271`, `export.ts:567` and `reportHtml.ts:111`. (7) The verifier must learn a third outcome; see `openQuestions`.

CASE NUMBER EDIT SITE. Neither the mock nor the issue says where. `CaseSubhead.tsx` (167 lines) already does inline edit-in-place for name (51-62) and description (67-77) through `update.mutateAsync`, so a third inline field there is the cheapest correct answer and is covered by `tests/components/CaseSubhead.test.tsx`. NewCaseWizard step 1 in the mock has name and description only (template 12552-12559), so do not add it there.

WHAT 'TWO CLASSES' MEANS CONCRETELY. An Evidence Package is today's zip: `manifest.jsonl`, `report.html`, `certification.html`, signing-public-key.pem, `VERIFY.md`, `evidence.json`, TSA material, page/screenshot artifacts, plus a signed `export` manifest entry. A Working Copy must be a zip that ships artifacts and operator work product with NO `certification.html`, NO signing key, and no `evidence.json` shaped like an Evidence Package's - and must be self-identifying so the verifier can say 'not a verifiable object'. Today `verifyEvidencePackage` (`evidencePackage.ts:85-95`) returns `{pass:false}` with '`manifest.jsonl` missing from package' for exactly that input, and `cli.ts:99` prints 'RESULT: FAIL'. Satisfying AC#2 therefore requires a new outcome on `PackageVerifyResult` plus new CLI text and exit code.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Every one of those is listed blocking in `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md`: db/** at line 216, verify/** at 202, verifier/** at 203, `export.ts` at 236, `reportHtml.ts` at 238, `certification.ts` at 239, `caseArchive.ts` at 241, `ExportDialog.tsx` at 245 (narrowed from the components/export/** glob on 2026-08-23, so this is a deliberate blocking entry, not a glob accident). `src/shared/types.ts`, `src/shared/ipc.ts` and `src/shared/schemas.ts` are advisory (the cross-process contracts block, lines 276-283) and do not change the verdict. The ticket already carries the evidence-affecting label. Full ADR-0004 obligations apply: Evidence impact section, known-answer test or written justification, preserved backward verification, human review, never auto-merges.

Paths: `src/main/services/db/migrations.ts`, `src/main/services/db/core.ts`, `src/main/services/db/caseRepo.ts`, `src/main/services/certification.ts`, `src/main/services/export.ts`, `src/main/services/reportHtml.ts`, `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx`, `src/shared/verify/evidencePackage.ts`, `src/verifier/cli.ts`, `src/main/services/caseArchive.ts (only if the demo-archive version option is adopted)`

**Mock findings.**

- The three presets and their exact copy. `full` = every item, `working` = artifacts + text + notes + index, `court` = everything except notes. Auto-detection is a set-equality check falling back to 'custom' (15065-15070), and the disclosure row's label flips between 'Customise contents' and 'Custom selection' (15119). (template 15060-15090, 15119-15124). The app has no presets at all - `ExportDialog.tsx:24-29` has four `booleans` (captures, screenshots, `auditTrail`, annotations). Follow the mock for the preset shape and the auto-detect behaviour; this is the part of the mock that is directly buildable.
- The mock models NO class distinction. The 'Chain of custody' card renders unconditionally (12762-12790) whatever preset is chosen, and every item including `manifest` and `custody` is a plain toggleable checkbox (12750-12760). Choosing 'Working copy' only unticks four boxes. (template 12762-12790, 12748-12761). The issue requires two semantically distinct classes with Evidence Package invariants that cannot be deselected. Diverge from the mock: the issue and its acceptance criteria outrank it. The custody card must hide (or restyle as a non-evidentiary notice) under Working copy, and manifest/custody rows must render always-on.
- The cover-sheet field grid: Case, Case number, Examiner, Organisation, Exported, Contents, Ledger root hash, Signing key, Tool, then a free-text 'Purpose or authority' input with placeholder 'for example Disclosure under CPS request 2026/114', then Signature and Date rules. (template 12771-12790). This is the extended Certification field list, and it is the mock's most useful contribution. One word must diverge: line 12773 labels the signer 'Examiner'. The issue says the signer is the Operator, never Examiner or analyst. Use Operator. The mock also says 'Ledger root hash' where the app's vocabulary is manifest/chain; the certification already uses manifest language throughout so keep the app's.
- The scope row and the manifest note beneath it: 'Whole case - 8 captures' / 'N selected captures', with a mono note reading manifest entry -> scope: 'selection' - `captureIds`[N]. (template 12724-12728, 15121-15123). This is #398's surface, not #399's, and it lands in the same file first. #399 builds around it. It is also the mock's own answer to the issue's 'A note under the Scope row states what the manifest records for selections'.
- Item metadata strings that describe content the app does not export: 'Ledger slice - 16 entries covering the selection', 'Extracted text - 6 text-bearing artifacts', 'Linked notes (Markdown) - 3 notes - analyst work product', 'Selector hits (CSV) - 5 selectors - 23 hits'. (template 15053-15062). Three of these have no app implementation: notes, extracted text and selector hits are not written into any package today (grep of `export.ts` finds no `noteRepo`/`listNotes` call). 'Ledger slice' actively contradicts #398's ruling that the manifest ships complete and never as a slice - do not build it, and record it for #708. 'analyst work product' is again the banned vocabulary.
- No demo-Case affordance anywhere in the export dialog, and no case-number edit field anywhere in the app screens. 'Case number' appears exactly once in the whole mock, as a read-only cover-sheet value (12772), and 'demo case' appears only in the tour's final card (12657) and its intro copy (13307). (template 12772, 12657, 13307). Both surfaces the 2026-08-23 ruling adds are undesigned. The demo notice in the dialog and the demo line in the Certification have no pixel truth to match, and neither does the case-number editor. Design them to the existing app conventions rather than inventing a new pattern.

Rebasing #399 onto merged #398 is mandatory - both edit the add() sequence and the manifest-append block.
- #398 in `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx` at whole file (202 lines today), serialize. #398 must add the scope row (mock 12724-12728) and #399 rebuilds the dialog around it. If #399 rebuilds first the scope row is lost. #398 lands first; #399 cuts its branch from main after #398 merges, per ADR-0014's no-stacking rule.

The prep doc reserves this file to #398, but #399's AC#2 needs a third outcome here. Either #398 lands the outcome enum as part of its verifier work and #399 only supplies the marker, or #399 adds it after #398 merges. Decide before dispatch - two branches independently widening PackageVerifyResult is a semantic conflict a textual merge will not catch.
- #398 in `src/shared/schemas.ts` at ManifestExportEntrySchema 373-389 (.strict()), serialize. Only live if #399 adds an `exportClass` field to the export manifest entry. #398 is already adding scope and `captureIds` here. Same field-omission discipline applies: declare optional and omit when the value is the legacy default, or every pre-existing entry's canonical body changes and old chains break.

No real overlap. Second-to-land rebases. Worth noting only because the prep doc's conflict map calls `reportHtml.ts` #401's region alone.
- #405 in `src/main/services/db/migrations.ts` at the v31 block, disjoint. #405 consumes cases.`is_demo` and does not migrate. It lands last by construction. No file overlap, but #405 is blocked until v31 merges.
- #391, #392, #398, #401 in `src/shared/types.ts and src/shared/ipc.ts` at Case interface 7-15, UpdateCaseParams 307-312, ExportOptions 618-628, append-only. Append-only, second-to-land resolves. All three are advisory tier since ADR-0014, so touching them does not pull anything into the evidence gate.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) "`src/shared/verify/**` and `src/verifier/**`. #398 alone. No other ticket touches the verifier."
  - Correction: #399's second acceptance criterion requires the standalone verifier to say 'not a verifiable object' rather than FAIL for a Working Copy. Today `verifyEvidencePackage` returns {pass:`false`} with reason '`manifest.jsonl` missing from package' and the CLI prints 'RESULT: FAIL'. The verifier has no third outcome or marker check. #399 therefore must touch both files, or the criterion cannot be met.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage.ts:85`-90` (early return on missing manifest); `src/verifier/cli.ts:96-100` (binary pass/FAIL report) and `cli.ts:130` (exit code is result.pass ? 0 : 1)
- (changes-the-plan) "per the wave-1 adjudication a plain additive column that an old app drops on import does not need a CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION bump, so it stays at 3" (repeated in the #399 ruling comment as 'both columns are plain additive fields')
  - Correction: The adjudication being invoked is about `selectors.origin`, and the wave-1 note justifies it as 'a display-only field, which is tolerable'. The in-code rule the constant carries is broader: 'Bump this whenever a Case Archive gains data an older release would silently discard or reject opaquely.' `case_number` is display-only and clears that bar. The demo flag does not: silently discarding it is the precise failure the #405 ruling exists to prevent, because a re-imported demo Case then presents as an ordinary Case and exports a Certification that says nothing. This is the 'silently discard' limb, and it is the same limb that justified version 2 for `anchor_kind`/`anchor_json`. I think the reasoning is wrong even though the ruling comment settles the outcome. A blanket bump to 4 is the wrong fix (it makes every older Birdbrain refuse every ordinary archive), but writing `schemaVersion` 4 in the header ONLY when the exported case is a demo Case uses the existing gate exactly as designed: an old app refuses precisely the archives it would mishandle and nothing else. The header field is a plain literal at `caseArchive.ts:185` and the gate is a single comparison at 248, so the change is about four lines.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`caseArchive.ts:60`-64` (the stated bump rule and the constant); `caseArchive.ts:185` (header.schemaVersion literal); `caseArchive.ts:248-252` (the refusal gate); `docs/plans/2026-08-19-wave1-implementation-notes.md:16` ('a display-only field, which is tolerable and needs no refusal')
- (changes-an-estimate) "#399 also round-trips the field through `caseArchive.ts`" and the ruling comment's "make sure the flag is in `collectCaseRows` / the import path alongside `case_number`"
  - Correction: The export half needs no code at all. `caseRepo.collectCaseRow` is `SELECT * FROM cases`, so both new columns travel into `data.json` automatically. Only the import half needs editing, and it lives in `caseRepo.ts`, not `caseArchive.ts`: `importCaseRow` has a hand-maintained INSERT column list. If the `schemaVersion`-4-for-demo-archives option above is declined, `caseArchive.ts` may end up with zero changes on this branch - which matters, because it is a blocking-tier file whose diff the reviewer will look for.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/db/`caseRepo.ts:140`-146` (`collectCaseRow`, SELECT *); `caseRepo.ts:148-169` (`importCaseRow`, explicit column list ending exclusions/`exclusion_mode`); `src/main/services/`caseArchive.ts`:108` (`collectCaseData` delegates to the repo)
- (cosmetic) Conflict map: "Only #399 needs schema, claiming v31 for the Case `case_number` column"
  - Correction: Stale within the same document. The rulings section two pages earlier already says '#399 grew by one column. v31 now carries `case_number` and the demo flag.' An implementer reading the conflict map alone writes a one-column migration and #405 is blocked.
  - Evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md`, 'Migration order' paragraph versus the 'What the rulings changed about the wave' bullet in the same file
- (changes-an-estimate) Prep doc conflict map lists #399's files as "`ExportDialog`, `certification.ts`, case number, migration v31"
  - Correction: Understated by six files. It omits `export.ts` (which the same document assigns to the #398/#399 serial chain elsewhere), `reportHtml.ts:462` (the Investigator-vs-Operator vocabulary criterion), `caseRepo.ts`, the two verifier files, and `schemas.ts` if a Working Copy appends a manifest entry. `reportHtml.ts` is also the file #401 writes, which the map treats as #401-only.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`reportHtml.ts`:462` (`${field('Investigator (self-asserted)', esc(data.investigatorName))}`); `src/main/services/export.ts:452-464` (certification wiring) and 369-401 (manifest append)

**Test plan.** Unit, extending existing files rather than adding new ones where possible.

`tests/main/services/database.test.ts` - add a v31 describe block beside the existing per-migration blocks; assert `case_number` and `is_demo` exist on `cases` via PRAGMA `table_info`, that `is_demo` is NOT NULL with default 0, and that an upgrade from a v30 snapshot leaves every pre-existing row at `is_demo` = 0. Line 448 already asserts `user_version` === LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION and starts passing again once the block lands.

`tests/main/services/certification.test.ts` (448 lines) - the extended fields. Assert every new field renders (case number, contents, chain root hash, purpose-or-authority), that a demo Case produces the demonstration statement and a non-demo Case does not, that the signer line says Operator and the rendered HTML contains neither 'Examiner' nor 'analyst', and that an absent case number renders 'not stated' rather than an empty cell (the file's existing convention at `certification.ts:266` and 270).

`tests/main/services/export.test.ts` (1633 lines) - the two classes. Known-answer coverage that an Evidence Package zip still contains `manifest.jsonl`, `certification.html`, signing-public-key.pem and `evidence.json`, and that a Working Copy zip contains none of them, carries the marker file, and produces the intended manifest outcome. Assert `packageHash` for a fixed input is byte-stable against a frozen vector so the certification extension's effect on `packageHash` is visible rather than incidental - this is the ADR-0004 known-answer test.

tests/shared/verify/ - a new case beside `canonicalJson.test.ts` and `manifestChain.test.ts` asserting `verifyEvidencePackage` returns the not-verifiable outcome for a Working Copy fixture directory and still returns FAIL (not not-verifiable) for a genuinely broken Evidence Package with the manifest deleted. That distinction is the whole point of AC#2 and it is the one an implementer is most likely to get wrong.

`tests/main/services/`caseArchiveRoundTrip.test`.ts` (407 lines) - export a case with a case number and `is_demo` = 1, import it into a fresh DB, assert both survive with id remapping intact. Also assert a synthetic pre-v31 `data.json` (no `case_number`, no `is_demo` key) imports to NULL and 0 rather than throwing - `importCaseRow`'s explicit column list is where that breaks.

`tests/components/ExportDialog.test.tsx` (214 lines) - preset-to-item mapping for all three presets, custom detection when an item is toggled off a preset, that the Evidence Package invariant rows render disabled and cannot be unticked, that the demo notice appears only for a demo Case, and that purpose-or-authority reaches the mutation payload. Line 142's `options.investigatorName` assertion must be updated, as must `tests/renderer/api/export.test.ts:48`.

E2E - a new `e2e/export.spec.ts` (none exists). Select each preset and assert the checklist state; run both classes to a temp path and assert the two zips' entry lists differ in the expected way. `e2e/case-archive.spec.ts` is the closest existing model for driving an export to disk. `e2e/readme-screenshots.spec.ts:594-597` fills the investigator input but guards on `isVisible`, so it degrades rather than fails; the captured screenshot changes, which is expected.

Full gate per the background-jobs carve-out: `pnpm` lint, `pnpm` typecheck, BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 `pnpm` test, `pnpm` build, `pnpm` test:coverage and `pnpm` coverage:diff re-run after every edit. No extension change, so `pnpm` build:extension is not required.

**Open questions.**

- Do the mock's Notes, Extracted text and Selector hits checklist items become real package content on this branch, or does the checklist map only onto the four include flags that exist today (captures, screenshots, `auditTrail`, annotations)?
  - Why blocking: Acceptance criterion 5 is 'Notes inclusion toggles correctly (Court exhibit excludes Notes)'. Nothing writes operator notes into any package today - `export.ts` contains no `noteRepo` or `listNotes` call, and `reportHtml.ts`'s only 'note' occurrences are a CSS callout class. If notes are not added, Court exhibit and Full evidence bundle produce byte-identical output and AC#5 is untestable; if all three are added, this branch also gains a notes serializer, a text export and a CSV writer and roughly doubles.
  - Recommendation: Notes yes, the other two no. Notes are named in an acceptance criterion and in the Court-exhibit definition, so they are in scope. Extracted text and selector hits are named only in mock metadata strings (template 15055, 15058) with no criterion behind them - file them as a follow-up and say in that issue that they were kept out to hold this branch reviewable. That also removes the mock's 'Ledger slice' item, which contradicts #398's complete-manifest ruling and should go to #708 instead.
- Does a Working Copy export append an `export` entry to the case manifest, and if so how is it distinguished?
  - Why blocking: The issue says a Working Copy has 'no export entry indistinguishable from an Evidence Package's', which reads equally as 'no entry' and as 'a distinguishable entry'. Guessing 'no entry' means an operator can extract every artifact from a case and leave no audit trace - a custody gap in an evidence tool. Guessing 'an entry' requires a field on ManifestExportEntrySchema, which is .strict() at `schemas.ts:389`, so an undeclared field makes `verifyManifestChainText` return 'Invalid entry shape' for the whole chain. Either guess is expensive to unwind after review.
  - Recommendation: Append an entry, with `exportClass: 'working-copy'` declared optional on the strict schema and OMITTED for evidence exports - never `null`, never 'evidence'. That is the same omit-when-absent discipline `manifest.ts` already applies four times (`screenshotHash`, headers, `tls`, method) and that #398 must follow for scope/`captureIds`, and it keeps every legacy entry's canonical body byte-identical. Coordinate the schema edit with #398 since both land in the same union member.
- May #399 change `src/shared/verify/`evidencePackage`.ts` and `src/verifier/cli.ts`, which the prep doc reserves to #398, and what marks a package as a Working Copy?
  - Why blocking: AC#2 cannot be met without a third verifier outcome, and the prep doc tells the implementer that file is someone else's. An implementer who obeys the prep doc ships without AC#2; one who ignores it may race #398's own widening of the same result type. The marker choice is coupled: the verifier must recognise a Working Copy BEFORE the manifest-present check at `evidencePackage.ts:85`, which is currently the first thing that runs.
  - Recommendation: Yes, after #398 merges - #399 already sits behind #398 in the merge order so the serialization costs nothing extra. Ship a `WORKING-COPY.json` marker at the package root carrying the class, case name, case number, export time and, when applicable, the demo-Case statement; have `verifyEvidencePackage` check for it first and return a distinct outcome with its own CLI wording and a non-zero-but-not-1 exit code so scripts can tell 'not verifiable' from 'failed verification'. Keep the marker human-readable: the person who finds a Working Copy in disclosure is the one who needs to know what it is not.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Three answers, all of them scope boundaries rather than preferences: whether notes/text/selector-hits become real package content, whether a Working Copy is recorded in the manifest, and whether this ticket may touch the verifier that the prep doc reserves to #398. Separately, the maintainer should re-read the CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION reasoning in the light of `caseArchive.ts:60-63` - the ruling can stand, but it should stand on the demo flag's own merits rather than on an adjudication that was about a display-only column. Everything else is decidable from the code and the mock. Two things the issue body should absorb before it is labelled ready: the v31 two-column scope from the 2026-08-23 ruling comment (the body still describes only a case-number field), and an explicit statement that the mock's unconditional custody card and its Examiner/analyst vocabulary are to be diverged from.

### #401 feat(wayback): slide-out panel with sandboxed side-by-side compare and export refs

**Verdict.** A large three-part ticket: a 436px archive.org snapshot panel replacing the current flat WaybackTab, a two-pane compare that renders live archive.org content in a webview the app has no policy hook for today, and a small precedented addition to `report.html` plus (per the mock, and the prep doc misses this) to the export dialog.

Size: large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Three separable pieces against 0bf207e5.

(1) UI rebuild. `src/renderer/components/captures/WaybackTab.tsx` (185 lines) is today a single scrolling list with a "Look up" button, a Pinned section and snapshot rows. The mock replaces it with a 436px non-resizable right aside carrying an archive.org pill, a summary line, refresh, close, a disclosure line, a text filter, a calendar popover with prev/next month, three range presets, a "Closest" jump, a paginated 6-per-page row list and a footer count (template 10565-10668). Rows carry date/time/closest chip/pin/open-external plus mime, HTTP status and a signed delta from capture time (10630-10650). The viewer body under the `wayback` tab becomes a two-pane compare (10486-10560). Layout plumbing already exists and is unit-testable: `captureColumns.ts:31-45,69-88` already models `waybackActive` and the route already computes it (`captures.tsx:159-174`); today `waybackActive` hides both list and details, so the new aside is a fourth output or a sibling of the panel group.

(2) The webview. This is new work with no in-app precedent for remote content in a `<webview>`. `src/main/index.ts:152-158` sets `webviewTag: true` on the main window with `sandbox`/`contextIsolation` on and `nodeIntegration` off. `index.ts:179-189` denies all window-open and hands http/https to `shell.openExternal`. `index.ts:234-247` is the only `<webview>` policy in the app: an `app.on('web-contents-created')` handler that, for `getType() === 'webview'`, allows one initial `file://` navigation and `preventDefault()`s every other. `src/` has **no** `will-attach-webview` handler anywhere (verified by grep across src/, tests/, e2e/), so no main-process code inspects or overrides a webview's `webPreferences` or its `src` before attach. No renderer-facing session has a `setPermissionRequestHandler` or `setPermissionCheckHandler`, and no `will-download` listener anywhere. The two places that do harden remote content are `pdfExport.ts:242-248` and `backgroundRenderer.ts:393,452-453`, both of which use a dedicated `partition` plus `setPermissionRequestHandler(false)` and `setWindowOpenHandler(deny)` on a hidden BrowserWindow, not on a webview. `MhtmlViewer.tsx:43-49` is the only `<webview>` in the renderer: `partition="mhtml-sandbox"`, `webpreferences="javascript=no,contextIsolation=yes,sandbox=yes"`, no permission handler on that partition (safe today only because JS is off there).

A Wayback webview therefore needs, at minimum: its own partition; `will-attach-webview` on the main window's `webContents` to delete `preload`, force `nodeIntegration:false`/`contextIsolation:true`/`sandbox:true` and `event.preventDefault()` unless `params.src` starts with `https://web.archive.org/web/` (Electron's own documented pattern, confirmed via Context7 against electron/electron `docs/tutorial/security.md`); `setPermissionRequestHandler` and `setPermissionCheckHandler` denying everything on that partition; a `will-download` `preventDefault` on that session; and `setWindowOpenHandler(deny)` on the guest.

It also **requires changing** `index.ts:239-245`. That handler's allow-list is literally `url.startsWith('file://')`; archive.org replay `302s` to the nearest snapshot and its toolbar navigates, so as written the guard blocks the Wayback pane. The handler must become partition- or src-aware. That is a modification of an existing evidence-viewer invariant, not an addition beside it, and it is the single riskiest line in the branch: a wrong discriminator could also loosen the MHTML viewer's guard.

(3) Export surfacing. `reportHtml.ts` has zero occurrences of `wayback` (confirmed, `grep -ic` = 0), but the rendering is precedented rather than novel: `renderExhibit` already builds a `corroboration` block for a refetched TLS chain at `reportHtml.ts:964-978`, titled `Corroboration only - not bound to the capture`, emitted at `:1085`, and the scope section already contains standing language about "a public web-archive record" at `:597-600`. So the report half is a `ReportData` field (`:106-166`), an `ExhibitView` field (`:220-250`), a line in `buildExhibits` (`:261-283`) and an extension of the existing corroboration block. `export.ts` must load the refs into the `ExportData` literal at `:262-291` - `waybackRefRepo.listWaybackRefs(captureId)` already exists (`waybackRefRepo.ts:50-57`), as does `collectWaybackRefsForCase` (`:81-89`) for a per-case read.

Reused unchanged: the whole CDX/pin/persist spine. `waybackMachine.ts` already ranks by proximity, computes `closestIndex`, returns `checkedAt`, and validates a snapshot before persist (`isPersistableSnapshot`, `:113-129`) without re-querying archive.org. The `capture_archive_refs` table (migration v21, `migrations.ts:401-414`) already stores timestamp, `snapshotUrl`, `originalUrl`, digest, `statusCode`, `mimeType`, `checkedAt`, `pinnedAt` - every field the mock's rows display. IPC `wayback:lookup/list/pin/unpin` exists (`ipc.ts:153-157`, `ipcHandlers.ts:577-614`). "Open at archive.org" needs no new channel: `CAPTURES_OPEN_EXTERNAL` (`ipcHandlers.ts:358-371`) already allows http/https. No migration: `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 30.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Blocking on five independent paths, any one of which would be sufficient, plus the triage label itself. Per assessment lines 87-89 a labelled PR owes the full obligations whatever the diff touches, so the label settles it before the path list is consulted. The prep doc's two stated reasons both hold: `reportHtml.ts` genuinely has zero wayback occurrences (grep -ic = 0), and AC 4 changes what ships inside a package. The prep doc's list of five evidence-affecting tickets is correct for #401. Two additions it does not name: the diff also lands on `export.ts` and `ExportDialog.tsx`, both blocking, and on `src/main/index.ts`, blocking specifically for the evidence-viewer webview invariants that this ticket has to modify rather than merely extend. So the tier is right and the surface is wider than recorded.

Paths: `src/main/services/reportHtml.ts (blocking - assessment:238)`, `src/main/services/export.ts (blocking - assessment:236)`, `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx (blocking - assessment:245)`, `src/main/index.ts (blocking - assessment:204)`, `src/renderer/components/captures/WaybackTab.tsx (blocking - assessment:263)`, `src/main/services/waybackMachine.ts (blocking - assessment:284, only if the CDX layer changes; the panel may not need it)`, `src/main/services/db/waybackRefRepo.ts (blocking via src/main/services/db/** - assessment:216, only if a per-case read is added)`, `src/shared/ipc.ts, src/shared/types.ts, src/main/ipcHandlers.ts (advisory - assessment:277-280)`
