**Mock findings.**

- The slide-out is a fixed 436px right aside (`width:436px;min-width:436px`, no resize handle), bound 1:1 to the viewer tab: `wbPanelOpen: s.viewerTab === 'wayback'` and `wbClose` sets `viewerTab: 'screenshot'`. It is not a separately toggled drawer. (template 10565-10566, 13802, 15030). The app has no aside in the wayback state at all: `captureColumns.ts:75,82` hides both list and details when `waybackActive`. Follow the mock; the details panel staying hidden is already correct.
- Panel header: amber archive.org pill, `wbSummary` (`N snapshots - closest <delta>`), refresh button titled `Look up again`, close button. Below it the standing disclosure `Independent record of this URL. Corroboration only - looking up discloses the URL to archive.org.` (template 10567-10580). `WaybackTab.tsx:41-44` already carries almost this exact sentence and a Look up / Look up again button at :46-55. Follow the mock; the copy survives, the layout changes.
- Filters: free-text input `Filter snapshots by date, status or type...`, a calendar range button, three presets (`All time`, `+/-30 days of capture`, 2026/year), and a `Closest` crosshair jump that pages to the closest snapshot. The calendar is a 7-column month grid with prev/next month and an Apply button. (template 10581-10627, 14948-14950, 15002-15007). None of this exists in the app. Filtering in the mock is purely client-side over the loaded snapshot set (`waybackVals` at 14939-14945), so it needs no CDX change.
- Row shape: date, time, `closest` chip, pin toggle (`aria-pressed`), open-external, then mime, HTTP status colour-coded by band (green <300, amber <400, red), and a signed delta phrased `Nd Nh before capture` / `after capture`. Pagination is 6 per page with a footer reading `N snapshots total - N in range - checked 2 min ago`. (template 10629-10667, 14916-14922, 15008-15027, 15035). The app shows only the formatted timestamp, a `closest to capture` chip and the mime (`WaybackTab.tsx:152-163`). `statusCode` and digest are already stored (`capture_archive_refs`, `waybackRefRepo.ts:64-77`) and already returned by the CDX parser (`waybackMachine.ts:92-99`), so the extra columns need no new data. The signed-delta formatter is new.
- The compare is INLINE IN THE VIEWER BODY, not in the slide-out. A full-width header strip carries the no-diff copy: `Side-by-side reference - archive.org's copy renders independently. Corroboration is your call; Birdbrain doesn't diff the two.` Under it, two equal min-300px columns: left header `Your capture` on accent-subtle with the capture timestamp; right header `archive.org snapshot` in amber (#f59e0b) with `<date> <time> - <delta>` and an `Open` button. (template 10486-10532). The app has no compare at all. Note the mock renders BOTH panes as static styled markup, so it gives no guidance on webview chrome. Diverge where the issue is stronger: the amber `archive.org snapshot` chip is the mock's only labelling of the replay pane, and the AC demands the replay pane be `prominently labelled as live remote non-evidence content`. The AC wins; add labelling the mock does not have and record it as a mock correction for #708.
- Pinned refs surface in the EXPORT DIALOG, not only the report: a `Pinned Wayback snapshots` block listing each pin with its delta, footed by `Included as archive.org references in the report appendix.`, gated on `expHasPins`. (template 12797-12811, 15048-15051). `ExportDialog.tsx` has zero wayback occurrences. The prep doc's conflict map never mentions #401 touching `ExportDialog.tsx`, which is both blocking tier and the file #399 rewrites. Follow the mock; serialize behind #399. Note also that the mock says `report appendix` and `report.html` has no appendix module (`ReportModuleId` at `reportHtml.ts:168-177`) - see open questions.
- Snapshot rows carry a right-click context menu (`sc-camel-on-context-menu`) of kind `snapshot`, offering pin/unpin and `compare`. Pinned rows in the export dialog carry one too. (template 10630, 15025, 12802, 15050). Ruling 1 in the prep doc scopes #701 to four kinds (capture, note, selector, tag) and defers file/folder/part/ledger to #803. `snapshot` appears in neither list. The menu is an accelerator only, and pin/compare are both reachable inline in the row, so #401 can ship without it. Worth reporting so the gap is tracked rather than silently dropped.

**Conflicts.**

- #399 in `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx` at the Include block, lines 24 and 121-150, serialize. #399 first, which the prep doc's merge order (#398 -> #392 -> #391 -> #399 -> #401) already gives. #399 rewrites this dialog for the two export classes and the case number; #401 adds the mock's `Pinned Wayback snapshots` block (template 12797-12811). Both are blocking tier. #401 must rebase onto merged #399 rather than guess at the post-#399 shape.
- #398 in `src/main/services/export.ts` at #401 at the ExportData literal 262-291; #398 at the `appendManifestEntry` export entry 387-395, serialize. #398 first (already the merge order). The line regions are genuinely disjoint - #401 adds a field to the data literal, #398 adds `scope`/`captureIds` to the manifest entry - so this is a textual rebase, not a semantic conflict. But the file is blocking tier and #398's omit-when-absent canonicalization rule is load-bearing, so #401 must not touch anything in 380-400.
- #399 in `src/main/services/reportHtml.ts` at ReportData 106-166 and the module registry 168-177, append-only. Unconfirmed whether #399's `case_number` reaches the report cover; if it does, both tickets append to the same ReportData interface. Append-only, second-to-land resolves. If #401 chooses a new appendix module rather than the existing corroboration block, it also touches `ReportModuleId` and `DEFAULT_REPORT_MODULES`, which raises the collision odds - another reason to prefer the corroboration block.
- #398 in `src/shared/ipc.ts, src/shared/types.ts, src/main/ipcHandlers.ts` at wayback channel block (`ipc.ts:153-157`, `ipcHandlers.ts:577-614`), disjoint. #401 most likely needs no new channel at all: lookup/list/pin/unpin already exist and `CAPTURES_OPEN_EXTERNAL` already permits `https`. If a per-case pinned-refs read is added it is one append. All three files are advisory tier since ADR-0014.
- #701 in `src/renderer/components/captures/WaybackTab.tsx (and its successor panel)` at snapshot row context menu, disjoint. The mock gives snapshot rows a context menu of kind `snapshot` (template 10630, 15025) offering pin/unpin and compare. Ruling 1 scopes #701 to capture/note/selector/tag and defers file/folder/part/ledger to #803; `snapshot` is in neither list. #401 ships without it - both actions are reachable inline in the row, which satisfies the ruling's accelerator-not-sole-route rule. Flagging so the gap is filed rather than lost.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (cosmetic) "#401 is evidence-affecting and carries no label." (prep doc line 74, and the heading "Two label corrections, to make before dispatch")
  - Correction: Stale, not wrong. The relabel was applied. `gh api repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/401/labels` returns enhancement, evidence-affecting, redesign, and the issue carries a maintainer comment headed `Wave 3 prep, 2026-08-23: relabelled evidence-affecting`. The triage miss was real; the correction has landed. Nothing is outstanding here before dispatch.
  - Evidence: `gh` `api` repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/401/labels --jq '.[].name' -> enhancement, evidence-affecting, redesign
- (changes-an-estimate) "`src/main/services/`reportHtml`.ts` contains zero occurrences of wayback today, so that is new rendering in a blocking-tier file, not a display tweak."
  - Correction: The grep is confirmed exactly (grep -ic wayback = 0). But it overstates the novelty. `renderExhibit` already builds a `corroboration` block for a refetched TLS certificate chain, headed `Corroboration only - not bound to the capture`, and the scope section already carries standing language about "a public web-archive record" being labelled as corroboration. The report half is an extension of an existing, wording-reviewed pattern, not a from-scratch section. That lowers the estimate for the report change while leaving the evidence obligations exactly where they are.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`reportHtml.ts:964`-978` (corroboration block), :1085 (emission), :597-600 (scope language); grep -ic wayback `src/main/services/`reportHtml`.ts` = 0
- (changes-the-plan) "#401 adds pinned-ref rendering to `reportHtml.ts`, which has no wayback code today, so its region is disjoint from #398's and #399's." (prep doc line 155)
  - Correction: Incomplete on two counts. First, `reportHtml.ts` cannot render refs the export never loaded, so #401 must also write `src/main/services/export.ts` - the ExportData literal at 262-291 - which is the same blocking-tier file #398 edits at 387-395. The regions are disjoint; the file is not, so #401 inherits the same serialize discipline the prep doc applies to #398 and #399. Second, the mock puts a pinned-refs block inside the export dialog, so #401 also writes `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx`, which is blocking tier and is the file #399 rewrites. Neither file appears in the prep doc's #401 row.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/export.ts:262-291` (ExportData literal), :387-395 (#398's `appendManifestEntry` region), :451 (`report.html` into artifacts); /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html:12797-12811; `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:236`,245
- (changes-the-plan) "`src/main/index.ts` already enforces evidence-viewer invariants and is blocking tier; whatever the panel needs belongs there rather than in a second place." (prep doc line 228-231)
  - Correction: Right about the destination, but it reads as if the work is additive. What `index.ts:239-245` actually enforces is a single hard-coded rule - allow one initial navigation whose URL starts with `file://`, block everything else, for every webview in the app. A Wayback webview must load `https`://web.archive.org and must tolerate archive.org's redirects, so #401 has to MODIFY that invariant into a partition- or src-aware policy, not add beside it. That is a stronger claim than the prep doc makes and is the branch's highest-risk edit, because the same handler is the MHTML evidence viewer's only navigation guard.
  - Evidence: `src/main/index.ts:234-247`; `src/renderer/components/captures/MhtmlViewer.tsx:43-49`
- (changes-an-estimate) "The acceptance criteria ask for the posture to be verified by tests where testable and documented where not, which is the right split." (prep doc line 228-229)
  - Correction: Agreed, and the testable half is larger than the phrasing implies. `index.ts` has an established extraction pattern for exactly this: `resolveWindowSize` was pulled out to `src/main/windowSize.ts` and `revealWhenReady` to `src/main/windowReveal.ts`, each with its own unit test. The whole webview decision surface (which partition, which `src` prefixes, which `webPreferences` are forced, which navigations are allowed) can be extracted the same way and covered by known-answer tests. Only Chromium actually honouring the resulting preferences is untestable in unit scope, and that belongs in e2e or in the Evidence impact section.
  - Evidence: `src/main/`windowSize.ts`:41`, `src/main/`windowReveal`.ts`, `tests/main/`windowSize.test`.ts`, `tests/main/`windowReveal.test`.ts`, `src/main/index.ts:15`,140,165
- (changes-an-estimate) The prep doc's #401 row lists only `WaybackTab` to panel, sandboxed webview, refs in the report.
  - Correction: Understates the renderer footprint. The wayback layout state is already modelled in `captureColumns.ts` and computed in the captures route, both of which change; and `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129` already asserts the current behaviour, including that `wayback-lookup-btn` is visible - a `testid` the redesigned panel header replaces with an icon button. That spec breaks unless the branch carries it.
  - Evidence: `src/renderer/components/captures/`captureColumns.ts:31`-45`,69-88; src/renderer/routes/cases/$`caseId/captures.tsx:159-174`; `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129`

**Test plan.** Unit, node project:
- New `tests/main/webviewPolicy.test.ts` over an extracted `src/main/webviewPolicy.ts`, mirroring the shape of `tests/main/windowSize.test.ts`. Known-answer cases: a `https://web.archive.org/web/...` `src` is allowed only on the wayback partition; any other host is refused; a `file://` `src` is allowed only on the `mhtml` partition (proving the existing evidence-viewer invariant is not loosened); a `webPreferences` object carrying `preload`, `nodeIntegration:true` or `contextIsolation:false` comes back sanitized; every permission string is denied; a download decision is always refuse.
- Extend `tests/main/services/reportCitationInvariants.test.ts` (319 lines, the invariant harness whose property 1 is "every artefact path the document cites exists in the package"). Three cases: a case with pinned refs renders the corroboration wording and the archive.org URL is emitted as an external reference, not as a package path, so property 1 neither trips nor is evaded; a case with zero pinned refs renders `report.html` byte-identically to the pre-change output for that fixture (the backward-verification criterion in prose form); and the report still renders for a capture with a ref whose digest/`statusCode`/`mimeType` are all `null`, since `waybackRefRepo` returns `undefined` for each.
- Extend `tests/main/services/export.test.ts` for the ExportData load, and assert the refs come from `listWaybackRefs` per capture rather than being inferred.
- `tests/main/services/waybackRefs.test.ts` (83 lines) and `tests/main/services/waybackMachine.test.ts` (169 lines) need extension only if the CDX layer or the repo changes; if the panel is pure presentation over the existing result they stay as-is, and saying so in the PR is the right answer.

Unit, web project:
- Extend `tests/components/WaybackTab.test.tsx` (168 lines) for the panel: filter narrows rows, each preset changes the range and resets to page 1, calendar Apply closes and re-pages, Closest jumps to the page holding the closest snapshot, pin toggles `aria-pressed`, pagination bounds at both ends, and the empty state renders `No snapshots in this range.`
- Extend `tests/renderer/captureColumns.test.ts` (126 lines) for the wayback aside: with `waybackActive` the list is hidden, the details panel is hidden and the aside is present at 436px.

E2E:
- New `e2e/wayback-panel.spec.ts` for the AC-mandated open-panel / pin / compare flow. The CDX lookup must be stubbed or the spec must run against a pinned fixture; do not let CI reach archive.org.
- The webview posture is partly e2e-testable and that is where the "verified by tests" half should reach furthest: assert the guest's `webContents` reports `nodeIntegration` `false` and no preload, assert a navigation to a non-archive.org URL is refused, and assert a `window.open` from the guest opens nothing. Whether Chromium honours `sandbox:true` internally is the genuinely untestable part.
- Reconcile `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129`, which asserts the current full-bleed layout and the visibility of `wayback-lookup-btn`. Either carry that `testid` onto the panel-header refresh button or update the spec in the same PR; it will otherwise go red.

Testable vs documented, stated plainly for AC 2:
Testable, and therefore owed as tests - the policy decisions (`src` allow-list, forced `webPreferences`, permission denial, download refusal, navigation refusal), and the observable guest configuration in e2e. Documented, and therefore owed as an Evidence impact section plus comments in the policy module - that Chromium's process sandbox actually isolates the guest, that archive.org's own toolbar JS cannot reach the host, and the reasoning for why rendering third-party remote content inside an evidence tool does not contaminate the evidence path (the replay is never hashed, never manifest-anchored, and never becomes a Capture).

Backward verification, proven not asserted: `report.html` enters the artifact index through `export.ts:451` and therefore into `packageHash`, so new exports get a new package hash while every previously produced package verifies unchanged. Run the standalone verifier against a pre-change fixture package and record the result rather than asserting it.

**Open questions.**

- Is JavaScript enabled in the Wayback replay webview?
  - Why blocking: It decides both whether the feature works and how much hardening is load-bearing, and the AC does not say. The AC lists no node/preload/IPC/filesystem, intercepted navigation and popups, denied permissions and blocked downloads - it never mentions JS. The app's only existing webview runs `javascript=no` (`MhtmlViewer.tsx:47`), and that is precisely why nobody ever set a permission handler on the `mhtml-sandbox` partition. With JS off, archive.org replays of most modern pages render broken or blank and the compare pane is worthless; with JS on, the permission handlers, download blocking and navigation policy stop being defence-in-depth and become the only thing between third-party code and the evidence tool. An implementer who guesses ships either a useless pane or a materially larger attack surface, and on a never-auto-merge PR that is a full review round either way.
  - Recommendation: JavaScript ON, and treat every item in the hardening list as mandatory rather than defence-in-depth: dedicated partition, `will-attach-webview` `src` allow-list restricted to `https://web.archive.org/web/`, permission request and check handlers denying everything, `will-download` prevented, `setWindowOpenHandler` denying, and the guest's navigations confined to that same prefix. JS off defeats the stated purpose of the feature. If the maintainer is not willing to run third-party JS in the app, the honest alternative is to drop the in-app replay and keep only `Open at archive.org`, which is a scope decision, not an implementation one.
- Where in `report.html` do pinned refs render: a new appendix module, or the existing per-exhibit corroboration block?
  - Why blocking: The mock's export-dialog copy says `Included as archive.org references in the report appendix.` (template 12809), and `report.html` has no appendix. Its module set is fixed at `cover, contents, scope, methodology, custody, exhibitIndex, exhibits, verification, signature` (`reportHtml.ts:168-177`). Adding a tenth module changes `ReportModuleId`, `DEFAULT_REPORT_MODULES`, the contents index and the surface `tests/main/services/reportLayoutInvariants.test.ts` checks, and it raises the collision odds with #399. Using the existing block does none of that. Both are defensible readings of AC 4, which says only `appear in the Evidence Package report`, and getting it wrong means redoing the evidence-affecting half of the diff.
  - Recommendation: The existing per-exhibit corroboration block at `reportHtml.ts:964-978`. Pinned refs are per-capture (`capture_archive_refs.capture_id`), they are corroboration in exactly the sense that block already defines, and the scope section already carries the standing language about a public web-archive record (:597-600). A reviewer reads the ref beside the exhibit it corroborates rather than flipping to an appendix. Send `report appendix` back to the design side as a #708 correction rather than building an appendix to match a caption.
- In the compare view, is the left `Your capture` pane the stored screenshot or the MHTML webview?
  - Why blocking: It decides whether one webview or two are live at once, which is the whole blast radius of the security work. If it is MhtmlViewer, the `web-contents-created` handler at `index.ts:234-247` must simultaneously serve a `file://`-only guest and an archive.org guest, so the per-webview discriminator stops being optional and a mistake in it silently loosens the evidence viewer. If it is the screenshot, the wayback guest is the only remote webview and the policy is much simpler. The mock does not settle it: the pane header reads `Your capture` with the capture timestamp (template 10495-10496) over static markup that could be either.
  - Recommendation: The MHTML webview via the existing MhtmlViewer, because the mock renders an interactive-looking page and a screenshot beside a live replay is not a like-for-like comparison. Accept the two-guest policy and write the discriminator as partition-keyed in the extracted policy module, with a unit test asserting the `mhtml` partition still refuses everything but its one `file://` load.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: The label is already correct, so nothing is outstanding there. Three answers are: JavaScript on or off in the replay webview; where in `report.html` the pinned refs render; and whether the left compare pane is the MHTML webview or the screenshot. Beyond those, one thing the issue should gain before dispatch that is not a question: an acceptance criterion saying the branch reconciles `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts:124-129`, which asserts today's full-bleed wayback layout and the `wayback-lookup-btn` `testid` the redesign removes. Without it an agent will report green locally and go red in CI. It is also worth adding to the issue that the diff lands on `export.ts` and `ExportDialog.tsx`, since neither is named anywhere in the ticket and both are blocking tier and owned by other wave-3 branches.

### #392 feat(server): extension write endpoints with auto-capture-then-attach

**Verdict.** Three new token-guarded capture-server routes plus a brand-new shared URL canonicalizer, blocked on one undecided mechanism question: nothing in the tree can produce an operator-witnessed capture on the server's initiative, so the wire contract for "auto-capture" has to be settled before a branch starts.

Size: large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against 0bf207e5 the ticket is four pieces of new code plus one contract decision.

1. Routes. `createApp` in `src/main/services/`captureServer`.ts` holds every route between lines 159 and 592. Three new ones go beside them: tag-apply, note-create, URL-lookup. The Zod-validated route pattern to copy is POST /api/selectors at `captureServer.ts:451-495` (`zValidator` with a formatter, active-case check, archived-case check, server-stamped origin, `satisfies` on the response DTO). The wire schema pattern is `src/shared/schemas.ts:190-218` (SelectorCreateSchema plus `formatSelectorCreateError`) and the response-DTO convention is documented at `schemas.ts:106-115`.

2. URL canonicalization. Verified: it does not exist. `grep -rniE "canonicaliz|normalizeurl|canonicalurl|normalizedUrl" src/` returns four hits, all about JSON canonicalization (`src/verifier/cli.ts:19` and `src/main/services/`verifyRunbook.ts`:3`,98,102). This is a new module. `src/shared/`urlPatterns`.ts` is the precedent for placing it in `src/shared/` and it is a real precedent, not just a stylistic one: `extension/src/background.ts:11` imports `matchIgnoredUrl` from `@shared/urlPatterns` as a runtime value, so #393 can run the identical canonicalizer without #392 editing the extension.

3. Duplicate-URL resolution. `captureRepo` has no URL lookup at all (see the export list in `src/main/services/db/`captureRepo`.ts`; there is `searchCaptures` at :244, FTS-backed, and nothing keyed on URL). A new repo function is required. The `captures(url)` table also has no index - `migrations.ts` declares only `idx_captures_case_id` (:152), `idx_captures_format` and `idx_captures_manifest_index` (:230-231), `idx_captures_trusted_time` (:360). Recommend canonicalizing at read time over the case's rows rather than adding a `canonical_url` column, because a column means a migration and #399 has claimed v31; keeping this migration-free is what keeps the prep doc's "nothing else in the wave migrates" true.

4. Attach. Both attach targets already exist and need no new persistence: `tagRepo.findTagIdByNameInsensitive` (`tagRepo.ts:150`) plus `tagRepo.addTagToCapture` (:34), and `noteRepo.createNote` (`noteRepo.ts:148`) taking CreateNoteParams (`src/shared/ipc.ts:359-369`) which already carries `captureId`, `bodyDoc` and `sourceUrl`. Criterion 4 is satisfied by building the doc with `plainTextToNoteDoc` (`src/shared/`noteDoc.ts`:263`) and serialising it into `bodyDoc`, not by writing a bare `body` string.

5. Exclusion. Any route that can cause a capture must call `matchCaseExclusion` (`src/main/services/`exclusionPolicy.ts`:47`) or the pair used at `captureServer.ts:329`. The self-test route at :499 is the only documented exception and the assessment doc's `exclusionPolicy` entry says to read call sites rather than assume coverage.

The contract decision is in `openQuestions`: the server has no way to make an operator-witnessed capture happen. The only server-side acquisition path is `renderPageInHiddenWindow` via `createRecaptureService` (`src/main/index.ts:364-365`), which ingests with `method: 'background'` (`recapture.ts:167`), and `src/shared/types.ts:38-40` defines the vocabulary explicitly - `'extension' = operator-witnessed via the Chrome extension; 'background' = silent hidden-window recapture`. So the hidden-window path cannot satisfy the words the ticket uses. Separately, `recaptureService.enqueue` returns `{accepted, rejected}` only (`recapture.ts:218-247`) - no capture id, no promise - so the server could not await a specific capture through it without extending that interface.

Confirmed on the extension question: #392 need not touch extension/src at all. `extension/tsconfig.json` maps `@shared/*` to `../src/shared/*`, and `extension/src/utils/api.ts:1-13` imports the wire types from `@shared/schemas` type-only. New schemas and a new shared canonicalizer land in src/shared and #393 consumes them without a diff under extension/.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** `captureServer.ts`, `exclusionPolicy.ts` and `recapture.ts` are blocking entries in the Acquisition block (assessment lines 167, 173, 175); `captureLifecycle.ts` and db/** are blocking in the capture-lifecycle block; `noteDoc.ts` is blocking in the notes-to-evidence block. The ticket already carries the evidence-affecting label, so full ADR-0004 obligations apply whatever the diff touches. Two additions. First, the new shared URL canonicalizer will not match any include-list glob when it lands, and it decides which Capture a Tag or Note binds to - the same argument that got `urlPatterns.ts` added in #227 and `exclusionPolicy.ts` in #400. The PR should amend `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md` to list it at blocking tier. Second, the advisory-tier files this ticket touches (`shared/schemas.ts` line 281, `shared/types.ts` line 280, `shared/ipc.ts` line 279) owe only a one-line reviewer disposition and do not add obligations.

Paths: `src/main/services/captureServer.ts`, `src/main/services/captureLifecycle.ts`, `src/main/services/exclusionPolicy.ts`, `src/main/services/db/**`, `src/shared/noteDoc.ts`, `src/main/index.ts`, `src/main/services/recapture.ts`

**Files.**

| Path | Lines | Risk | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `src/main/services/captureServer.ts` | 49-56 (CaptureServerDeps), 147-156 (token guard), 451-495 (route pattern to copy), new routes appended after 576 | high | Three new POST routes registered inside `createApp` after the guard at line 156. Possibly a new dep on CaptureServerDeps if the capture mechanism ends up server-side. |
| `src/shared/urlCanonical.ts` | new file | high | The canonicalization rules (redirect target, fragment, trailing slash, case/host, query handling). New evidence surface: it decides which Capture a Tag or Note attaches to. |
| `src/main/services/db/captureRepo.ts` | new function beside `listCaptures` (14) and `searchCaptures` (244) | high | Lookup of captures in a case whose canonicalized URL matches, plus the deterministic tiebreak when several match. |
| `src/shared/schemas.ts` | 190-218 (SelectorCreateSchema and its error formatter), 106-115 (response DTO convention) | medium | Wire schemas and error formatters for the three payloads, plus response DTOs the extension imports type-only. |
| `src/shared/types.ts` | 906-922 (CaptureEvent) | medium | Only if progress/failure is reported over the existing capture-activity event stream; CaptureEvent has no attach-related variant today. Append-only. |
| `src/main/index.ts` | 417 (`startCaptureServer` call), 364-376 (`recaptureService` construction) | medium | Only if the resolved mechanism gives the capture server a render or recapture dependency. Disjoint from the webview region at 234-236. |

The harness starts a real server on a fresh port per test with injected deps, so a failing `captureLifecycle` stub is how the capture-failure case is driven. |

Without it the module sits off the backstop list entirely. |

**Mock findings.**

- The in-page selection bar this ticket's endpoints back: three buttons, Selector / Tag / Quote, on the Chrome Extension screen. (template 12306-12321 (markup), 12176 (screen root)). The app has no in-page bar; that is #393. What matters for #392 is that the mock defines exactly three actions and only two of them (Tag, Quote) hit new endpoints. Selector already has a route (POST /api/selectors, `captureServer.ts:451`).
- Only the Selector action opens a confirm popover (Watch for new hits, Backfill). Tag and Quote fire immediately with no confirmation and no capture step. (template 12322-12349 (confirm popover, gated on `selConfirmExt`), 15891 (`openSelConfirm` is wired to the Selector button only)). No app equivalent exists. Consequence for #392: the mock provides no UI in which an operator could disambiguate between several existing Captures of one URL, which means the duplicate-URL resolution must be deterministic server-side rather than a prompt. That agrees with the ticket wording and should be built that way.
- The post-action toast is success-only: a green check, a label, a value and a meta string. The extension selection flow has no pending, in-progress, capturing or failed state anywhere. (template 12351-12366 (toast markup), 15931-15932 (`selToastLabel` only ever reads Selector created / Tag applied / Quote saved / Copied)). This is the significant gap. #392 must report capture progress and failure to the caller, and #393's criteria say to show them inline, but the mock designs neither. The Tag toast meta string is literally 'applied to this page' (15919), asserting the attach as instantaneous. Follow the ticket, not the mock: progress and failure states have to be invented, and #708 should carry the note that the mock has no design for them.
- The mock is internally inconsistent about what Quote creates. `selAsNote` `unshifts` a new Note titled 'Quote - cracked-forum thread 88213' with a `sourceUrl`, but the toast it sets says value 'Case log' and meta 'quote appended'. (template 15921-15927). Load-bearing for #392's note-create route: a new Note per quote and an append to a running case-log Note are different endpoints. The data written (a new Note carrying title, body and `sourceUrl`) matches `noteRepo.createNote` and CreateNoteParams, so build a new Note per quote and treat the toast wording as a mock defect for #708.
- The in-app twin of the same bar on the Captures screen, for comparison. (template 10422-10437, prompt text at 10420). Not #392's scope (that surface belongs to #391 and the capture viewer), but it shows the same three actions, which supports keeping one shared canonicalization and attach vocabulary across both callers.

**Conflicts.**

- #393 in `src/shared/schemas.ts` at new wire schemas and response DTOs appended after line 218, serialize. #392 lands first, as the merge order already has it. #393 consumes the types type-only and must not redefine them. The real coupling is not the file, it is that #392 fixes the request/response shape #393 renders progress and failure from, which is why the `openQuestion` below has to be answered in #392.
- #398, #399, #401, #391 in `src/shared/types.ts` at CaptureEvent at 906-922, and end-of-file appends, append-only. Second to land resolves. Advisory tier per the assessment list line 280, so no evidence obligation flows from touching it.
- #401 in `src/main/index.ts` at #392 would touch line 417 (`startCaptureServer` deps); #401's webview work sits at 234-236 and 152-157, disjoint. Different regions of one blocking-tier file. Either order works; whoever is second rebases on main per ADR-0014.
- #399 in `src/main/services/db/migrations.ts` at v31 block and LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION, disjoint. Only if #392 adds a `canonical_url` column, which it should not. Canonicalize at read time in `captureRepo` instead; a case's capture set is small and `idx_captures_case_id` (`migrations.ts:152`) already covers the scan. That keeps #399 the sole owner of v31.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) the guard at `captureServer.ts:147-158` already covers every state-changing endpoint, so new routes inherit it rather than re-implementing it
  - Correction: Half right, and the unqualified form is dangerous. The guard only tests `c.req.method === 'POST'` (`captureServer.ts:149`), and Hono only runs middleware registered before the matching route. I probed this with the repo's own `hono` build (`node_modules/hono/dist/index.js`): a POST route registered above the `app.use` returns 200 with no token; the same route registered below returns 401; a PUT route and a GET route return 200 regardless of position. So a new route inherits the guard if and only if it is a POST registered after `captureServer.ts:156`. Registering a route above line 148, or using GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE for a write, produces an unauthenticated write endpoint on loopback. Nothing in the code prevents either.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`captureServer.ts:148`-156`; probe output: POST /early 200, POST /late 401, PUT /put 200, GET /getwrite 200
- (cosmetic) the guard at `captureServer.ts:147-158`
  - Correction: The middleware is lines 147-156 (comment at 147, `app.use` at 148, closing `})` at 156). Line 157 is blank and 158 is the comment above `app.get('/api/status')` at 159.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`captureServer.ts:147`-159`
- (changes-the-plan) Neither the prep doc nor the grounding comment says where the bytes for an auto-capture come from.
  - Correction: This is the ticket's central unknown and it is unstated in both. The server cannot initiate an operator-witnessed capture: the only server-side acquisition path is `renderPageInHiddenWindow` (`src/main/index.ts:365`), which ingests with `method: 'background'`, and `src/shared/types.ts:38-40` defines 'background' as the silent path and 'extension' as the operator-witnessed one. The prep doc's starting-file list names `captureLifecycle.ts` as 'the ingest pipeline auto-capture-then-attach drives', which is `true` of the attach half and silent on the acquisition half.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/types.ts:38-40`; `src/main/services/recapture.ts:167` (method: 'background'); `src/main/index.ts:364-365`, 417
- (cosmetic) (grounding comment) `grep -rn "canonicaliz|normalizeUrl|canonicalUrl" src/shared src/main/services` returns three hits, all inside `src/main/services/verifyRunbook.ts`
  - Correction: Four hits over src/: `verifyRunbook.ts:3`, :98, :102 and `src/verifier/cli.ts:19`. The conclusion is unchanged - all four are JSON canonicalization and no URL normalization exists anywhere in the tree.
  - Evidence: grep -rniE "canonicaliz|normalizeurl|canonicalurl|`normalizedUrl`" src/
- (cosmetic) `extension/src/`. #393 owns `content.ts` and the selection bar; #392 does not touch the extension.
  - Correction: Confirmed, and the mechanism is worth recording so nobody breaks it: `extension/tsconfig.json` maps `@shared/*` to `../src/shared/*`, `extension/src/utils/api.ts:1-13` imports wire types from `@shared/schemas` type-only, and `extension/src/background.ts:11` already imports a shared runtime value (`matchIgnoredUrl`). So both the new schemas and a shared canonicalizer are reachable from #393 with zero #392 edits under extension/.
  - Evidence: `extension/tsconfig.json:15-18`; `extension/src/utils/api.ts:1-13`; `extension/src/background.ts:11`

**Test plan.** HTTP-seam tests extend `tests/main/services/`captureServer.test`.ts`. The harness there is real: `beforeEach` starts a live server on a fresh port with an in-memory DB, a temp storage root and injected `selectorLifecycle`/`captureLifecycle`/`sessionService` (lines 47-66), so the four required cases are all reachable without mocking Hono.

- Happy path: active case, a URL the case already holds, tag applies and note is created; assert the tag row via `tagRepo.getTagsForCapture` and the note's `bodyDoc` parses through `parseNoteDoc` (criterion 4 is only proved by asserting the stored doc, not the response).
- Auth failure: mirror the existing 401 assertions at lines 163 and 754 - one request with no X-Birdbrain-Token, one with a wrong token. Add a route-table test that asserts every new route answers 401 without the header, so the guard-inheritance rule is pinned rather than assumed. This is the single most valuable new test given the middleware-ordering finding below.
- Capture failure: inject a `captureLifecycle` whose ingest rejects, then assert the response is a useful error AND that no tag row and no note row exists afterwards. That negative assertion is the whole of the "no orphan" criterion; a status-code assertion alone does not test it.
- Duplicate URL: seed two captures of the same URL in one case (`postCapture` at line 82 can do it, or insert through the repo) and assert the deterministic winner, then assert the same request twice is idempotent for the tag case.
- Exclusion: a URL matching the case's exclusion list must be refused before anything is written. Not in the ticket's list but `perCaseExclusions.test.ts` exists and #400's rule applies to every acquisition route.

`CLAUDE.md` warning that applies here: the file funnels every response read through `type JsonBody = Record<string, any>` with an ESLint-disable (lines 38-44). `CLAUDE.md`'s Testing section records that this helper discards the ~80 TS18046 errors the node typecheck project would otherwise surface in this file. Use `readJson` for new assertions rather than adding fresh casts or a second helper, and do not "improve" the helper - the disable comment is deliberate.

Canonicalization gets its own file, `tests/shared/`urlCanonical.test`.ts`, modelled on `tests/shared/`urlPatterns.test`.ts`. Table-driven known-answer cases: trailing slash on root vs path, fragment stripping, scheme and host case, default port, percent-encoding normalisation, query parameter ordering and whether tracking params are dropped, plus idempotence (canon(canon(u)) === canon(u)) and the non-http schemes CaptureUploadSchema already admits (`schemas.ts:76-90` allows birdbrain://). This file is the ADR-0004 known-answer test for the ticket.

Full loop before handoff: `pnpm` lint, `pnpm` typecheck, BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 `pnpm` test, `pnpm` build, then `pnpm` test:coverage and `pnpm` coverage:diff. `pnpm` build:extension is not needed if extension/ stays untouched, which it should.

**Open questions.**

- Where do the bytes for an auto-capture come from: does the extension capture and hand the server MHTML in the same request, or does the server render the page itself in a hidden window?
  - Why blocking: It decides the entire wire contract, which is what #393 is blocked on, and it decides what the resulting Capture claims about itself. `src/shared/types.ts:38-40` defines 'extension' as operator-witnessed and 'background' as the silent hidden-window path, so a server-side render cannot honestly be called operator-witnessed - the ticket's own wording. An implementer who guesses the hidden-window route also finds that `recaptureService.enqueue` returns only {accepted, rejected} (`recapture.ts:218-247`), with no capture id and no promise, so there is nothing to await or attach to; they would have to extend that interface or call `renderPage` plus `captureLifecycle.ingest` directly and re-implement the exclusion enforcement `exclusionPolicy.ts` exists to centralise. Either guess is several hundred lines in the wrong direction.
  - Recommendation: Extension supplies the bytes. Make the tag and note routes accept an optional multipart MHTML payload in the same shape POST /api/captures already takes (`captureServer.ts:259-296`), and make the flow: canonicalize, look up an existing Capture, and if none exists ingest the supplied payload first and attach only after ingest returns. That keeps method 'extension', makes the no-orphan criterion structurally `true` rather than defensively `true`, holds the HTTP request open so #393's 'inline progress' is a spinner on one awaited fetch, and needs no polling route. It also explains why the URL-lookup route exists at all: it lets the extension skip capturing when the case already holds the page.
- Is the URL-lookup route a GET or a POST?
  - Why blocking: The ticket body says 'All new routes sit behind the existing state-changing-endpoint token guard', but that guard only fires on POST (`captureServer.ts:149`). A GET lookup would be unauthenticated to any local process, and would leak whether a given case holds a given URL. This is the exact failure mode the reader brief names as the worst outcome for this ticket, and it is decided at route-table design time.
  - Recommendation: POST, with the URL in a JSON body. It is semantically a read, but the guard as written protects nothing else, and the alternative - broadening the middleware to cover GET - would change the response of the existing unauthenticated GET routes (/api/status at :159, /api/cases at :218, /api/selectors/active at :441) that the extension depends on. If a GET is chosen anyway, say so explicitly in the PR and record why the disclosure is acceptable.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: The two questions above, both of which are contract decisions rather than preferences. Everything else is present: the ticket has grounded starting files, the guard behaviour is now measured rather than assumed, the attach targets already exist, and the test harness supports all four required seam cases. Also worth adding to the issue before dispatch: an acceptance line requiring the new shared canonicalizer to be added to the Acquisition include list in `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md` at blocking tier, matching the #227 and #400 amendments, or the module ships off the backstop list.

### #393 feat(extension): in-page selection bar (Selector / Tag / Quote)

**Verdict.** A persistent, page-gesture-driven injected element on three blocking-tier evidence paths, where the #386 suppression protocol is a stateless one-way strip built for background-driven transient UI, so the ticket carries hidden work (a page-side suppression latch plus a `captureHygiene` entry) and is evidence-affecting at the blocking tier, not the non-evidence auto-merge candidate the prep doc calls it.

Size: large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: yes).

**What it requires.** WHAT EXISTS AND IS REUSABLE.
- The page-side protocol is exactly one thing: `registerCaptureUiTeardown` at `extension/src/`captureSuppression.ts:32`-37` over a module Set at :26, drained by `suppressCaptureUi` at :46-57 and `suppressCaptureUiOrThrow` at :69-72 (throws CaptureUiSuppressionError, :58-63). Registration precedents: `toast.ts:165` registers `removeToastImmediately`; `content.ts:263` registers `removeHighlights`.
- The background bracket `createCaptureSuppression` at `captureSuppression.ts:102`, instantiated at `background.ts:190-198`, wrapped around the whole MHTML + screenshot + text collection at `background.ts:713-724`.
- The fallback strip `removeInjectedBirdbrainUi` at `captureHygiene.ts:3-17`, injected by chrome.scripting.executeScript at `background.ts:246-249` when the content script is unreachable or answers ok:false.
- The connection/case gate already exists as connected + `activeCaseId`, used to enable/disable context menus at `background.ts:412-414`. The pre-filter `blockedCaptureReason` is at `background.ts:590-594`; `ignoreRulesLoaded` is surfaced as `rulesLoaded` in `messages.ts:48`.
- The Selector path the AC says to match is the context-menu handler at `background.ts:473-530`, calling `createSelector`({`caseId`, pattern, label}) (`utils/api.ts:238-247`), then re-fetching selectors and re-highlighting.
- Toast plumbing already respects the bracket: `sendToastWhenCaptureIdle` (`background.ts:203-206`) drops, `sendCaptureOutcomeToast` (:214-223) holds, `flushHeldToast` (:226-233) releases.

HOW THE BAR REGISTERS. It calls `registerCaptureUiTeardown` with a synchronous remove-my-host function, exactly like `toast.ts:165`, AND its host id/prefix must be added to `removeInjectedBirdbrainUi` in `captureHygiene.ts:3-17`. The header comment at `captureSuppression.ts:9-16` states this explicitly: the `executeScript` copy cannot reach the registry, and the two are kept in step by hand. Miss the second half and an orphaned content script (extension reload, `background.ts:240-243`) leaves the bar in the MHTML and in every screenshot slice, with `prepareTabForCapture` reporting success.

WHERE THE PROTOCOL DOES NOT COVER THIS. The registry is strip-only and stateless. The content script has no page-side restore hook or page-side "suppression is in force" flag. Restore is background-only (`background.ts:191-197`): `restoreSelectorHighlights` plus `flushHeldToast`. Nothing tells the content script a bracket has opened or closed. That is sufficient for the two existing widgets because both are background-driven and the background gates them (`checkSelectorsOnTab` bails at `background.ts:830`; toasts are dropped or held at :203-233). The selection bar is raised by a page `mouseup`, which the background cannot gate. Failure mode: operator selects text mid-capture, or the scrolling path's up to 120 second phase (`content.ts:472-486`) moves the page under a live selection, and the bar is injected after `suppressCaptureUiOrThrow` has already run for that slice (`content.ts:378`). It lands in the next frame and in the `saveAsMHTML` serialisation, which is the #386 defect class reopened. `content.ts`'s `captureInProgress` flag (:265) is not a substitute: it is set only inside the two screenshot functions and is `false` during an MHTML-only capture, which is the whole bracket for a capture with screenshots disabled (`background.ts:717-722`).

WHAT MUST BE WRITTEN. (1) A latch on the suppression protocol: `suppressCaptureUi` sets suppressed, an explicit release clears it, and the bar's show path consults it. The release needs a new content-script message from the background restore effect (`background.ts:191-197`), since nothing is sent today. (2) The bar itself: selection geometry, positioning, three actions, dismissal. (3) `captureHygiene.ts` entry. (4) `background.ts` message handlers relaying the three actions, plus new `utils/api.ts` client functions for #392's Tag / Note / URL-lookup routes. The content script cannot call the server itself and there is no precedent for it doing so: `content.ts` imports nothing from utils/api (its only fetch, :432, is a data: URL), and utils/api is imported only by `background.ts:6`, `PopupApp.tsx:2` and `OptionsApp.tsx:2`.

SHADOW DOM VS LIGHT DOM. Both existing patterns put a host div in the light DOM: `toast.ts:16-20` (mode 'open', fixed id) and `content.ts:39-46` (mode 'closed', randomized id STYLE_CONTAINER_ID at :21, matched by prefix at `captureHygiene.ts:15`). Shadow content is not what leaks; the host element is, because `saveAsMHTML` serialises the light DOM. Either mode works provided the host is removable by id or id prefix from a function that closes over no module state (`captureHygiene.ts:1-2`). Prefer a fixed id like the toast's; a randomized one needs its own prefix rule.

MHTML TIMING. `captureMhtml` (`background.ts:15-24`) runs inside `collectFrames` alongside the screenshots (`background.ts:714-724`), so one suppress call at :191 covers both. The screenshot path re-suppresses per slice (`content.ts:378`) but the MHTML does not, which is precisely why the latch matters more for MHTML than for the frames.

BUILD. `pnpm` build:extension runs two Vite passes; the content script is a separate BUILD_TARGET=content IIFE config (`extension/vite.config.ts:9`,15-39) with `emptyOutDir`:false. Two consequences: `pnpm` dev:extension does not watch `content.ts` (`CLAUDE.md`), so an agent iterating on the bar must re-run the content build by hand; and the content config has no react/tailwind plugins, so the bar must be plain DOM plus a style string, like `toast.ts:23-78`. It resolves @extension and @shared (`vite.config.ts:33-38`), so importing `captureSuppression` and shared types is fine.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:161-164` scores `extension/src/background.ts`, `extension/src/content.ts`, `extension/src/toast.ts` and `extension/src/utils/api.ts` all blocking. #393 cannot avoid `content.ts` (the bar mounts in the content script) or `background.ts` and `utils/api.ts` (`content.ts` imports nothing from utils/api and its only fetch at :432 is a data: URL, so every server call is relayed through the service worker; utils/api is imported only at `background.ts:6`, `PopupApp.tsx:2`, `OptionsApp.tsx:2`). Three blocking hits. ADR-0014 section 'The exit bar keys on the blocking tier' therefore gives this the full ADR-0004 obligations: Evidence impact section, human review, no auto-merge, evidence-affecting label. Substantively as well as mechanically: the change injects a persistent element into the page that `saveAsMHTML` serialises and every screenshot slice renders, which is the exact defect class #386 was opened for. Separately, `captureSuppression.ts` and `captureHygiene.ts` are on no tier at all despite being the files that decide whether injected UI reaches captured bytes - a hole in the list, not a reason to score this ticket lower.

Paths: `extension/src/content.ts`, `extension/src/background.ts`, `extension/src/utils/api.ts`, `extension/src/captureSuppression.ts`, `extension/src/captureHygiene.ts`

**Files.**

| Path | Lines | Risk | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `extension/src/content.ts` | 263, 265, 378, 554-560, 584 (new sibling module preferred) | high | Mount point for the bar: register its teardown next to `removeHighlights` at :263, and wire the mouseup/selectionchange listener. The bar body belongs in a new sibling (`extension/src/`selectionBar`.ts`) rather than growing this 584-line file, following `toast.ts` as precedent. |
| `extension/src/captureSuppression.ts` | 26, 32-37, 46-57, 102-165 | high | Add the page-side suppressed latch: `suppressCaptureUi` sets it, a new release entry point clears it. This is extending the one protocol, not adding a second mechanism. |
| `extension/src/captureHygiene.ts` | 3-17 | high | Add the bar's host id (or id prefix) to the `executeScript` fallback strip. Mandated by the comment at `captureSuppression.ts:9-16`. |
| `extension/src/background.ts` | 190-198, 203-233, 246-249, 473-530, 590-594 | high | New message handlers for the three bar actions; relay Tag/Quote to #392's endpoints and Selector to the existing `createSelector` path; send a suppression-release message from the restore effect at :191-197; gate the bar on connected + `activeCaseId` the way the context menus are gated at :412-414. |
| `extension/src/utils/api.ts` | 238-247 (new functions appended) | high | Client functions for #392's Tag-apply, Note-create and URL-lookup routes. Shape is fixed by #392, which lands first per the merge order. |
| `extension/src/selectionBar.ts` | new file | medium | The bar: selection geometry, shadow host, three buttons, dismissal, disabled state, and its teardown registration. |
| `tests/extension/prepareForCapture.test.ts` | 1-60 (existing known-answer harness), extend | medium | Extend the DOM known-answer test to raise the bar before PREPARE_FOR_CAPTURE and assert the serialisable DOM matches the pre-injection baseline. This is the #386 regression guard and the highest-value test in the ticket. |
| `tests/extension/captureSuppression.test.ts` | 39-48 (registry describe block), extend | low | Cover the latch: set by `suppressCaptureUi`, cleared by release, and that a show attempt while latched is refused. |
| `tests/extension/selectionBar.test.ts` | new file | low | `jsdom` test (// @vitest-environment `jsdom`, as `tests/extension/`prepareForCapture.test.ts`:1` does) for state gating (no connection, no active case, selection too short/long) and inline failure display. |

**Mock findings.**

- The bar itself: three buttons Selector / Tag / Quote, 28px tall, single row, dividers between buttons (border-left on buttons 2 and 3), Selector rendered in accent colour at weight 600 and the other two in text-secondary at weight 500. Icons #i-crosshair, #i-tag, #i-note at 13px. position:absolute at left/top `selX`/`selY`, z-index 60, class `pop`, animation `bbrise` .`12s` ease-out, `data-selui`=`1`. (template 12306-12321). Nothing like it exists in extension/src today. The app's CaptureSelectionBar (from #396) is the multi-row toolbar at template 10045-10071, a different surface. Follow the mock.
- Selection geometry. The bar is raised on `mouseup` (sc-camel-on-mouse-up, an undocumented fourth packer rewrite) over the page container. It bails if the `mouseup` target is inside [`data-selui`]; requires a non-collapsed range whose whitespace-collapsed text is 3..160 chars; takes `getRangeAt`(0).getBoundingClientRect(); x = max(8, min(rect.left - `hostLeft` + `scrollLeft`, `hostWidth` - 308)); y = rect.bottom - `hostTop` + `scrollTop` + 8. (template 12220 (the `mouseup` binding), 15835-15851 (`startSel`)). No equivalent in the app or extension. Note the 308px right clamp is the confirm popover's 296px width plus 12: it exists so the popover fits, not the bar. Document-flow absolute positioning (not fixed) means no scroll listener is needed, which is the safer choice here because a reposition-on-scroll listener would re-touch the DOM during the scrolling capture phase (`content.ts:472-486`).
- Selector is a two-step flow, not a one-click create. It opens a 296px confirm popover with a kind badge, the derived value, an optional advisory note, a 'Watch for new hits' checkbox (default on), a 'Backfill - scan 42 existing captures' checkbox (default off), 'Esc to cancel', and a Create button with an Enter affordance. (template 12322-12350; defaults at 15848-15850; Enter binding at 13115-13118). Directly contradicts AC 2 ("Selector action matches the existing context-menu path"), which is a one-click create (`background.ts:473-530`). Neither toggle has a backend: POST /api/selectors takes only `caseId`/pattern/label (`utils/api.ts:238-247`), and auto-capture is HOTFIX-disabled (`background.ts:157`, 321-322, 630-631, 857-881). This is the ticket's biggest unresolved gap - see open questions.
- Kind classification for the confirm popover: domain (URL path stripped, with the note 'Path stripped - matches any URL on this host.'), email, `btc` address, ipv4, hash, handle, filename, domain, text; plus a >42-char note. (template 15820-15833 (`selKindOf`)). Nothing equivalent exists anywhere in src/. `CreateSelectorPopover.tsx` is 97 lines of Label + Value inputs with Cancel/Create and has no kind detection at all. The only IOC-kind vocabulary in the tree is `src/main/services/extraction/`iocAdapter`.ts`, which is a main-process extraction adapter, not a classifier the extension can call.
- Tag acts immediately, no picker. The tag name is derived from the selected value: lowercase, non-alphanumerics collapsed to hyphens, trimmed, truncated to 22 chars, falling back to 'tagged'. Created if absent, then applied. (template 15913-15918 (`selAsTag`)). This answers a question the AC leaves open. It also diverges from #665, which asks for a designed batch-tag picker for the app-side bar - the mock gives the extension bar no picker at all.
- Quote acts immediately: prepends a note titled 'Quote - <page context>' whose body is the selected text in curly quotes, carrying `sourceUrl`. (template 15919-15927 (`selAsNote`)). Maps onto #392's Note-create endpoint. The mock does not model the auto-capture-then-attach progress the AC requires, so progress/failure display has no pixel source (see below).
- Outcome toast: bottom-LEFT (left:24px, bottom:20px), 36px tall, light card surface, green check, label + monospace value + meta, a `6s` progress bar, and a dismiss button. (template 12351-12366; `6s` timer at 15815-15819; labels at 15931-15933). Distinct in position and styling from the existing capture toast, which is bottom-RIGHT and dark (`toast.ts:29-31`, and the mock's own dark capture toast at template 12407). So this is a second toast surface, not a reuse of `toast.ts`. The mock shows only success states - there is no failure variant, which AC 3 ("a failed auto-capture shows the error and creates nothing") requires.
- The bar renders in all six simulated extension states, including '`nocase`' and 'disconnected'. `selectionVals()` reads no connection state. (template 15862-15935 (`selectionVals`), 16014-16019 (the six states), 16046). AC 1 requires the bar be `disabled without connection + active Case`. The mock has no disabled variant of the bar in any state. That styling has no pixel source and must be invented or ruled.
- Escape behaviour is internally inconsistent in the mock. The global `keydown` steps confirm -> bar and bar -> dismissed. The button inside the confirm popover, labelled 'Esc to cancel', calls `cancelSel`, which clears the native selection and dismisses entirely. (template 13102-13103 (global Escape), 12344 (the button), 15892 (`cancelSel`)). A mock defect rather than an app divergence. Belongs on the #708 corrections list.
- A third instance of the same bar exists on the Captures screen over the Text tab of the capture viewer (`selBarApp`), and a second on the Notes screen (`selBarNote`). (template 10413 and 10423-10437 (Captures/Text); 11411-11422 (Notes)). Notes is #391. The Captures/Text instance is in no wave-3 ticket I can find. Worth naming so it is not lost - it is the same component in a third runtime.

**Conflicts.**

- #392 in `extension/src/utils/api.ts` at appended client functions after `createSelector` (:238-247), disjoint. #392's grounding comment says explicitly "Standard loop plus `pnpm` build:extension only if you end up touching extension/. You should not; #393 owns that side." No file overlap. What exists is a hard contract dependency: #393 cannot write its client functions until #392 fixes the route names and payloads, and the merge order already puts #392 first.
- #391 in `(no shared file today) the selection-kind classifier` at the mock's `selKindOf`, template 15820-15833, append-only. #391 is renderer-only and #393 is extension-only, so the file sets are disjoint as long as neither puts the classifier in src/shared/. If one does, the other should import it. #391 merges first per the merge order, so #393 resolves: reuse whatever #391 produced if it landed in src/shared/, otherwise write a private copy under extension/src/. Do not create a second src/shared/ module for the same thing.
- #391 in `docs/adr and PR-body evidence sections` at n/a, disjoint. No overlap. Flagged only because both tickets build visually identical bars and a reviewer may expect one shared implementation; the runtime boundary (React renderer vs plain-DOM content-script IIFE with no react/tailwind plugins, `extension/vite.config.ts:15-39`) makes that impossible.

**Test plan.** The load-bearing test is an extension of the existing known-answer harness at `tests/extension/`prepareForCapture.test`.ts` (`jsdom` via the // @vitest-environment `jsdom` directive at :1; it already asserts the serialisable DOM after PREPARE_FOR_CAPTURE is identical to the pre-injection page, and already carries a page-owned decoy at PAGE_HTML to prove the strip is not over-broad). Add: raise the selection bar, run PREPARE_FOR_CAPTURE, assert the baseline DOM; then repeat with the content script simulated unreachable so the `removeInjectedBirdbrainUi` path (`captureHygiene.ts:3-17`) is what does the stripping, which is the half that is kept in step by hand and the half most likely to be forgotten.

`tests/extension/`captureSuppression.test`.ts` extends for the latch: `suppressCaptureUi` sets it, the release entry point clears it, a show attempt while latched is refused, and the latch survives a teardown that throws (the existing block at :39-48 is the pattern).

A new `tests/extension/`selectionBar.test`.ts` (`jsdom`) covers AC 5: bar shows for a 3..160-char selection, does not show below/above that, does not show when the `mouseup` originates inside the bar, and is disabled with no connection or no active case. Failure display for the Tag/Quote auto-capture path belongs here too, driven by a mocked chrome.runtime.sendMessage rejection - assert the bar surfaces the error and issues no follow-up call, which is AC 3's `creates nothing`.

Background side extends `tests/extension/`backgroundScreenshotSuppression.test`.ts` or adds a sibling: the new message handlers gate on connected + `activeCaseId` the way the context menus do at `background.ts:412-414`, respect `blockedCaptureReason` (:590-594) and `ignoreRulesLoaded`, and the restore effect (:191-197) emits the release message.

Two constraints to know. First, tests/extension/** typechecks under `tsconfig.test.web.json` (its include lists tests/extension/**/*.ts explicitly) but RUNS in the node vitest project, whose include is tests/**/*`.test.ts` (`vitest.config.ts:114`) - hence the per-file @vitest-environment `jsdom` directive, which is the established pattern here, not an override to avoid. Second, coverage does not reach any of this: `vitest.config.ts:25` includes only src/** and :35 excludes extension/**, and `scripts/diff-coverage.mjs:148-172` lists changed extension files as `unscored` and passes (`passed = !scored || ...`). So `pnpm` coverage:diff will report PASS on an extension-only PR with the files named. The tests are enforced by the pre-pass reviewer, not by the gate. No E2E is possible: playwright drives Electron and e2e/ contains no extension harness.

Manual verification, per the #386 precedent (its AC 4): full-page and scrolling captures of a page with the selection bar raised, confirming it appears in neither the screenshot nor the MHTML.

**Open questions.**

- Is the Selector action one click (matching the context-menu path at `background.ts:473-530`) or the mock's two-step confirm popover with the 'Watch for new hits' and 'Backfill - scan N existing captures' toggles (template 12322-12350)?
  - Why blocking: AC 2 and the mock give opposite answers, and the gap is not cosmetic. Neither toggle has a backend: POST /api/selectors takes only `caseId`/pattern/label (`extension/src/utils/api.ts:238-247`), auto-capture is HOTFIX-disabled throughout `background.ts` (:157, :321-322, :630-631, :857-881), and no backfill/rescan capability exists anywhere. An implementer who follows the mock either invents server surface (a second server ticket inside an extension ticket) or ships two checkboxes wired to nothing. One who follows the AC ships a bar that does not match the design source, which the wave's definition of done rejects.
  - Recommendation: Follow the AC: one click, matching the context-menu path, and no confirm popover in #393. File the typed confirm popover with watch and backfill as its own ticket blocked on the server work those two toggles need. Rationale: the mock is the pixel source, but its confirm popover depends on capabilities that do not exist and that #392 does not add, so building it here means building them here. #391 faces the same popover question in the renderer and is the natural place for the classifier to land first.
- May #393 modify `extension/src/`captureSuppression`.ts` to add a page-side 'suppression in force' latch and a release message, or is 'route through the existing protocol' meant as 'register a teardown and change nothing else'?
  - Why blocking: An implementer reading the prep doc's constraint literally will register a teardown and stop. That is insufficient and the failure is silent: the bar is a page-gesture-driven element, so nothing prevents it being injected between the bracket's suppress call (`background.ts:191`) and a frame or the `saveAsMHTML` serialisation. `content.ts`'s `captureInProgress` (:265) is not a substitute - it is set only inside the two screenshot functions, so it is `false` for the whole bracket when screenshots are disabled (`background.ts:717-722`). Guessing wrong reopens #386 with the same shape and no test would catch it unless the latch is what is being tested.
  - Recommendation: Extend `captureSuppression.ts`. A latch set by `suppressCaptureUi` and cleared by an explicit release, with the release sent from the existing restore effect at `background.ts:191-197`, is one mechanism extended, not the second mechanism the constraint forbids. It also keeps the extension's one new seam (the module's own header comment at :1-24) as the single place the rule lives, and it is unit-testable without a browser, which was #386's stated design property.

**Ready for agent.** no. Missing: Three things. (1) The evidence-affecting label, which the issue does not carry and which the tiered path list says it needs - three blocking-tier files, assessment :161, :162, :164. Without it `pre-pass-gate.yml` keys wrong and the PR can auto-merge under a rule that should not apply to it. (2) The ready-for-agent label, absent today (labels are enhancement, redesign only), and a wave-3 grounding comment, which this ticket alone among the wave-3 set does not have - zero comments, against two on #392 and two on #391. (3) Rulings on the two open questions above; both are the kind an unattended agent will silently guess. Blocker #392 is still open, so the branch cannot start regardless, but that is expected sequencing rather than a readiness defect.

### #391 feat(notes): selection-to-Selector/Tag action flow

**Verdict.** A from-scratch text-selection overlay for the Tiptap note editor (nothing in the tree calls window.getSelection today), whose only genuinely hard parts are two mock affordances the app's data model does not support: the "Backfill" checkbox, which `selectorLifecycle` already does unconditionally, and the Tag action, which the mock applies to the note while the app has no note-tag relation.

Size: medium. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against 0bf207e5 the plumbing genuinely exists and the UI genuinely does not.

Exists and reusable: `SELECTOR_ORIGINS` including 'note' at `src/shared/types.ts:660`; `CreateSelectorParams.origin?: SelectorOrigin` at `src/shared/ipc.ts:337-343`; the handler at `src/main/`ipcHandlers.ts:442`-444` delegating to `selectorLifecycle.createSelector`, which persists via `selectorRepo.ts:22-40` and coerces an unknown origin to NULL at `selectorRepo.ts:45-47`; `useSelectorsMutations(caseId).create/.update` at `src/renderer/lib/api/selectors.ts:87-97` with cache invalidation; `useCaptureTagEditor(captureId).createTag` at `src/renderer/components/captures/`useCaptureTagEditor.ts:32`-38`, which is exactly create-tag-then-apply to-capture; sonner toasts through `notify.success` (`src/renderer/lib/notify.ts:96-98`) with the Toaster mounted at `src/renderer/routes/__root.tsx:149`; `ORIGIN_LABEL`/`ORIGIN_ICON` already covering 'note' at `src/renderer/components/selectors/`selectorOrigin.ts:10`-22`. I confirmed the whole renderer-to-selectorRepo path accepts 'note' with no migration.

Does not exist and must be written: any text-selection handling at all. `rg "getSelection" src/ extension/src` returns nothing. The renderer also has no kind-detection function - the only IOC classification in the tree is main-process (`src/main/services/extraction/`iocAdapter.ts:9`-20`), so the mock's `selKindOf` regexes (template 15820-15832) have to be ported as a new pure module. Since #393 builds the same bar inside `extension/src/content.ts`, and the extension already imports `@shared` (`extension/tsconfig.json:17`, `extension/src/content.ts:15-17`), that module belongs in `src/shared/` so it is written once. A new file there matches no evidence glob.

Selection extraction detail: the mock reads `window.getSelection().toString()`. Inside this editor that is wrong across a Mention chip, because MentionChip renders `maskMention` (`MentionChip.tsx:84`), a truncated label. `useNoteEditor.ts:48-55` already has `mentionLeafText`, the leaf-text walker used for clipboard; reading the selection through `editor.state.doc.textBetween(from, to, ' ', mentionLeafText)` reuses it and gets the untruncated form.

Two integration hazards in the existing editor. First, buttons steal focus and collapse the selection they act on - `NoteEditor.tsx:99-101` already documents this and guards with `onMouseDown={(e) => e.preventDefault()}`; the new bar and popover need the same. Second, the CaptureDetailsPanel mount passes `onBlur={() => void inline.flush()}` and an Escape handler that reverts the draft (`CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:458-467`, guarded by `if (e.defaultPrevented) return`), so the popover's own Escape-to-cancel must call `preventDefault()` or cancelling the popover throws the note draft away.

Mention system interaction is otherwise nil: nothing here writes note body text, so `src/shared/noteDoc.ts` (blocking tier) and `noteReferenceRepo.ts` are untouched. That is a guard rail worth stating in the PR, not work.

No new dependency needed. `@tiptap/extension-bubble-menu` and `@floating-ui/dom` are present only as transitive deps in the `pnpm` store (not hoisted into `node_modules`/@Tiptap, not in `package.json:129-134`), and the mock hand-rolls its positioning anyway (template 15843-15850: range rect, clamp x to `host.clientWidth - 308`, floor 8, y = rect.bottom + 8). Hand-rolling matches the pixel spec and adds nothing.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Neither tier, matching the prep doc's "no" at line 47 and the grounding comment's Gate section. But the classification is conditional on three files staying out of the diff, and two of them are exactly where a careless implementation lands. `src/renderer/components/dashboard/cases/DataExplorer.tsx` is blocking (:268) and is the only call site of the popover the grounding comment tells the implementer to reuse. `src/main/services/`selectorLifecycle`.ts` is blocking (:217) and is where a Backfill parameter would have to go. `src/shared/`noteDoc`.ts` is blocking (:228) and is untouched only because this flow writes no note body. Naming all three as out-of-bounds in the PR body is cheap; discovering one of them at review costs the auto-merge.

Paths: `src/renderer/components/notes/** - explicitly on the exclusion list (evidence-paths assessment:331), "Note-editing UI; the evidence binding lives in the included src/shared/noteAnchor.ts / src/main/services/noteAnchorResolver.ts"`, `src/renderer/components/selectors/** - explicitly on the exclusion list (:332), "Selector-management UI; matching lives in main"`, `src/shared/selectionKind.ts (new) - matches no include glob; there is no blanket src/shared/** entry, only the named files at :172, :202, :228-229, :279-282`, `e2e/**, tests/** - not on the list`

**Files.**

| Path | Lines | Risk | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `src/shared/selectionKind.ts` | new file | low | Pure kind detection ported from the mock's `selKindOf` (template 15820-15832): domain-from-URL with path stripped, email, `btc` address, ipv4, hash, handle, filename, bare domain, else text; plus the two advisory notes. In src/shared/ so #393's extension bar reuses it rather than duplicating the regexes. Matches no evidence glob. |
| `src/renderer/components/notes/useNoteSelection.ts` | new file | low | Selection detection and flow state: `mouseup` handler bailing on [`data-selui`] hits, min 3 / max 160 chars after whitespace collapse, step 'bar' \| 'confirm' \| `null`, watch default `true`, backfill default `false`, plus the geometry clamp as an exported pure function so it is unit-testable (`jsdom` returns zeros from `Range.getBoundingClientRect`). |
| `src/renderer/components/notes/NoteSelectionBar.tsx` | new file | low | The two-action bar (Selector, Tag) and the typed confirm popover, per template 11407-11454. `onMouseDown` `preventDefault` on every button, per the precedent at `NoteEditor.tsx:99-101`. Escape handler must call `preventDefault` (see `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:458-467`). |

Keep `data-tour`="`noteeditor`" on the element `tourSteps.ts:113` targets. |

| `tests/components/NoteSelectionBar.test.tsx` | new file | low | `jsdom` component test for the bar and popover: two actions not three, kind chip and value rendering, watch default on / backfill default off, Escape calls `preventDefault`, buttons `preventDefault` on `mousedown`. |
