**Mock findings.**

- The note-scope selection bar: exactly two buttons, Selector (crosshair, accent, opens the confirm step) and Tag (tag icon, fires immediately with no confirm). 28px tall, absolutely positioned at `selX`/`selY`, marked `data-selui`="1" so a `mouseup` inside it does not restart the flow. (template 11407-11418). The app has nothing. The two-action shape confirms the ticket and the grounding comment: no Quote action in the note editor. Build as designed.
- The typed confirm popover: 296px wide, an uppercase mono kind chip, the value in mono with ellipsis, an optional advisory note line, a "Watch for new hits" checkbox, a "Backfill - scan 128 existing captures" checkbox, and a footer of "Esc to cancel" plus "Create" with a return-key glyph. It has no editable inputs at all. (template 11419-11454). `CreateSelectorPopover.tsx` is a different component with a different job: two editable inputs (Label, Value), no kind chip, no toggles, origin hardcoded to 'capture' at line 49. It is not this popover, and reusing it means rewriting it. Follow the mock and build the new one.
- Flow state and defaults: `startSel` gates on a non-collapsed selection whose whitespace-collapsed text is 3 to 160 chars, positions x clamped to host.clientWidth - 308 with a floor of 8 and y at rect.bottom + 8, and sets `selWatch` `true` / `selBackfill` false. Enter confirms while `selStep` === 'confirm'. (template 15835-15851, 13116-13119). All new. The 308 clamp is the bar's max width, the popover is 296 - use 308 as the mock does.
- `createSelFromSelection` records origin 'note' for note scope and sets `autoCap` from the watch toggle. `selAsTag` slugifies to [a-z0-9-], strips leading/trailing dashes, truncates to 22 chars, falls back to 'tagged', and its toast meta reads `applied to this note`. (template 15896-15918). origin 'note' maps cleanly. `autoCap` does not: the app has no per-selector auto-capture, only case-level settings.autoCaptureMode (`src/shared/types.ts:215`, `AutoCaptureCard.tsx:52-57`). The closest app concept is selectors.enabled (`migrations.ts:92`, DEFAULT 1). "applied to this note" has no app equivalent at all - there is no `note_tags` table (grep of `migrations.ts` returns 0). See open question 2.
- Success toast: label ("Selector created" / "Tag applied"), an icon, the value in mono accent, a meta string, a 6-second progress bar and a dismiss button, positioned bottom-left inside the editor pane. (template 11455-11472). The app's toasts are sonner, bottom-right, mounted once at `__root.tsx:149-159` with token `classNames` already applied. Use notify.success rather than building a second in-pane toast; that is the app-wide convention and the visual delta is position only.
- The mock's Notes screen is a left list plus a full-height editor pane, with the selection host being the scrolling editor wrapper. (template 11219-11221, 11406). The app's Notes screen is a card list (`NotesOverview.tsx:104-121` -> NoteCard -> NoteEditor mounted only while editing, `NoteCard.tsx:41-44`). That structural divergence is not this ticket's scope; attach the bar to whatever editor container exists.

**Conflicts.**

- #803 in `src/renderer/components/selectors/CreateSelectorPopover.tsx` at whole file, 1-97, disjoint. Disjoint only if #391 leaves this file alone and builds the mock's confirm popover as a new component. If #391 instead follows the grounding comment and parameterises this one, its sole call site `DataExplorer.tsx:156-161` must change too, and #803 rebuilds that entire screen - that would become serialize, #391 first per the wave merge order, #803 rebasing. Recommendation: build new, stay disjoint.
- #803 in `src/renderer/components/dashboard/cases/DataExplorer.tsx` at 156-161, the CreateSelectorPopover mount, disjoint. #391 has no reason to touch this file and must not - it is blocking tier (evidence-paths assessment:268) and touching it drags #391 into the evidence gate. #803 owns it.
- #393 in `src/shared/selectionKind.ts (new)` at whole file, append-only. #391 lands the classifier first (merge order puts #391 third, #393 sixth). #393's in-page bar imports it - the extension build already resolves @shared (`extension/vite.config.ts:36`, `extension/tsconfig.json:17`). If #391 instead buries the regexes under src/renderer/components/notes/, #393 duplicates them, which is the outcome to avoid.
- #393 in `extension/src/content.ts` at the in-page selection bar, disjoint. Different bundle, different files. Shared concept only.
- #701 in `src/renderer/components/notes/NoteEditor.tsx` at editor container, 117-125, append-only. #701 adds a note-kind context menu; if it attaches to the note editor rather than to NoteCard rows, both tickets add an overlay to the same container. #391 merges first (wave order puts the ruled extras after it); second-to-land resolves. Low likelihood - #701's four kinds attach to rows, not to editor bodies.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) "`src/renderer/components/selectors/CreateSelectorPopover.tsx` - the existing typed confirmation path, including kind detection. Reuse it rather than writing a second popover" (grounding comment, Starting files)
  - Correction: CreateSelectorPopover has no kind detection, no watch or backfill options, and hardcodes origin 'capture' with a comment explaining why. It is a two-input edit form, not the mock's read-only typed confirm. Nor does kind detection exist anywhere else in the renderer - `selectorUtils.ts` is `regex` syntax highlighting and pattern testing only, and the sole IOC classification in the tree is main-process. Reusing it means rewriting it into a different component.
  - Evidence: `src/renderer/components/selectors/CreateSelectorPopover.tsx:6-11` (props: `caseId`, `defaultValue`, `defaultLabel`, `onClose`), :41-50 ("'capture', not 'manual'" plus origin: 'capture'); `src/renderer/components/selectors/`selectorUtils.ts:20`-140`; `src/main/services/extraction/`iocAdapter.ts:9`-20`
- (changes-the-plan) "Not evidence-affecting, and the diff should stay renderer-side to keep it that way" (grounding comment, Gate)
  - Correction: True as stated, but the reason is narrower and more fragile than the comment implies, and following the comment's own reuse advice breaks it. Modifying CreateSelectorPopover's props forces an edit to its only call site, `src/renderer/components/dashboard/cases/DataExplorer.tsx:156-161`, which is on the include list at blocking tier. Separately, honouring the mock's Backfill checkbox requires a parameter that reaches `src/main/services/`selectorLifecycle`.ts`, also blocking tier. Either move flips the ticket to full ADR-0004 obligations, human review and no auto-merge.
  - Evidence: `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:268` (`DataExplorer.tsx`, blocking), :217 (`selectorLifecycle.ts`, blocking), :331-332 (notes/** and selectors/** both excluded)
- (changes-an-estimate) "Shared IPC spine. #391, #392, #398, #399, #401 all append to `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/types.ts` and `src/main/`ipcHandlers`.ts`" (prep doc, Conflict map, lines 177-178)
  - Correction: #391 appends to none of the three. Every channel and payload field it needs already exists: selectors:create carries origin, tags:create and tags:`addToCapture` are unchanged, and SELECTOR_ORIGINS already contains 'note'. The prep doc's own grounding comment contradicts this line by calling the ticket renderer-only.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/ipc.ts:337-343` (`CreateSelectorParams.origin`), :321-324 (CreateTagParams), :655-662 (tag channels), :672 (selectors:create); `src/shared/types.ts:660`
- (changes-an-estimate) "`NoteEditor.tsx` is 128 lines and `useNoteEditor.ts` 126, both small enough that the selection bar belongs in a new sibling rather than inside either" (prep doc lines 169-171, repeated in the grounding comment)
  - Correction: Both line counts are exact and the placement conclusion is right, but it understates the blast radius. `NoteEditor.tsx` is mounted from four call sites, none of which passes `caseId` today, so a new sibling still means prop additions in `NoteCard.tsx`, `CreateNoteCard.tsx`, `AddNoteModal.tsx` and `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx` - and the last of those has a blur-flush and an Escape-revert that the popover has to avoid tripping.
  - Evidence: `wc` -l gives 128 and 126; mounts at `NoteCard.tsx:73`, `CreateNoteCard.tsx:65`, `AddNoteModal.tsx:85`, `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:455`; blur/Escape at `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:458-467`
- (changes-an-estimate) Ticket AC2: "Created Selectors persist origin 'note' and enter the Selector Lifecycle normally (matches recompute asynchronously)"
  - Correction: Satisfied by construction with zero code - `selectorLifecycle.createSelector` already calls `scheduleRetroactiveMatch` unconditionally over the most recent 500 captures via `setImmediate`. Nothing to build; the risk is the opposite one, that an implementer reads the mock's Backfill toggle as a feature to add.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`selectorLifecycle.ts:96`-100`, :16 (RETRO_MAX_CAPTURES = 500), :92 (`setImmediate`)
- (changes-the-plan) Implied by "reuse the mutation hooks with cache invalidation" (grounding, Starting files, naming `src/renderer/lib/api/tags.ts`)
  - Correction: `useTagsMutations()`.create wraps a plain INSERT with no ON CONFLICT, against a tags.name column declared NOT NULL UNIQUE. A Tag action on selected text that already matches an existing tag name throws a SQLITE_CONSTRAINT error rather than reusing the tag. `useCaptureTagEditor.createTag` has the same gap. The note bar must look the name up in the tags list first and only create when absent.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/db/`tagRepo.ts:10`-16`; `src/main/services/db/migrations.ts:32-36` (name TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE); `src/renderer/components/captures/`useCaptureTagEditor.ts:32`-38`

**Test plan.** Unit, `jsdom` project (tests/components/**/*`.test.tsx` runs under the `jsdom` Vitest project, per `CLAUDE.md`):

- New `tests/shared/selectionKind.test.ts` - table-driven known answers over every branch of the classifier, one case per `regex` in template 15820-15832 plus the URL path-stripping result and the >42-char advisory note. Lands in the node typecheck project (`tsconfig.test.node.json`), which is correct for a pure shared module. This is the cheapest coverage in the ticket and the one that carries diff-coverage.
- New `tests/components/NoteSelectionBar.test.tsx` - alongside the existing `NoteCard.test.tsx` and `noteEditorFidelity.test.tsx`. Assert: two actions and not three; the kind chip and value render from the classifier; Watch defaults checked and Backfill defaults unchecked; every button calls `preventDefault` on `mousedown` (the `NoteEditor.tsx:99-101` rule); Escape calls `preventDefault` on the event so `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:464` does not revert the draft. Note `jsdom` returns zeros from `Range.getBoundingClientRect`, so do not assert pixel positions here - export the clamp maths as a pure function and test it directly against the template 15843-15850 numbers (308 width, floor 8, +8 y offset).
- Extend `tests/components/useCaptureTagEditor.test.tsx` or add a sibling for whatever the Tag path becomes, specifically the existing-tag-name case: tags.name is UNIQUE and `createTag` is a bare INSERT (`tagRepo.ts:10-16`), so the test that matters is `selected text matching an existing tag reuses it and does not throw`.

E2E, the acceptance criterion:

- Extend `e2e/notes.spec.ts` (197 lines, already has the create-a-note flow at lines 45-70 and a `reloadToNotes` helper at 36-43) or add `e2e/note-selection.spec.ts` following the same fixture. Round trip: create a note whose body contains a recognisable value, enter edit mode, double-click the word to select it (Playwright's `dblclick` fires a real `mouseup`, which is what the bar listens for), click Selector, confirm, then poll `window.birdbrain.selectors.list(caseId)` with expect.poll until the row appears and assert `origin === 'note'`. Poll rather than assert once: `selectorLifecycle.ts:92` defers the retroactive pass through `setImmediate`, and asserting immediately after the mutation resolves is the flake this ticket's own AC2 is warning about. If the test also asserts matches recomputed, poll `selectors.matchCounts` rather than reading the row.
- The existing `seedSelector` helper at `e2e/notes.spec.ts:16-30` is the negative control: it proves the UI is what created the row, since a seeded selector carries no origin.

Do not add a known-answer fixture under tests/shared/verify/ - nothing here touches the manifest chain or the verifier.

Per the background-jobs carve-out: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` (six projects), `BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 pnpm test`, `pnpm build`, then `pnpm test:coverage` and `pnpm coverage:diff` re-run after any edit. No `pnpm build:extension` - nothing under extension/ changes unless the classifier lands in src/shared/ and #393 has not started, in which case nothing imports it from the extension yet and the extension build is still untouched.

**Open questions.**

- What does the "Backfill - scan N existing captures" checkbox do? The app already backfills unconditionally: `selectorLifecycle.createSelector` calls `scheduleRetroactiveMatch` over the 500 most recent captures on every create (`selectorLifecycle.ts:96-100`, :16). The mock renders the checkbox unchecked by default (template 15869, `selBackfill`: `false`) with the label at 15920.
  - Why blocking: The three answers have different costs and one of them changes the ticket's gate. (a) Omit the checkbox: renderer-only, non-evidence, auto-merge stays available, but it is a visible divergence from the mock that needs recording on #708. (b) Render it checked and disabled with copy saying backfill always runs: same gate, honest, one extra line of copy. (c) Honour it: needs a new field on CreateSelectorParams reaching `src/main/services/`selectorLifecycle`.ts`, which is blocking tier (evidence-paths assessment:217) - the PR then owes an Evidence impact section, gets human review, and never auto-merges. An implementer who guesses (c) silently converts a non-evidence ticket into an evidence one after the fact.
  - Recommendation: (b). It matches the mock's pixels, tells the truth about behaviour, keeps the diff renderer-side, and costs nothing. Record the divergence on #708. The related "Watch for new hits" toggle needs no ruling: default-on already matches the app (selectors.enabled DEFAULT 1, `migrations.ts:92`), and an unchecked box can be honoured renderer-side by following create with `useSelectorsMutations()`.update({id, enabled: `false`}) (`selectors.ts:93-97`), so it never reaches main.
- What does the Tag action do when the note has no capture? The mock applies the tag to the note itself - `selAsTag`'s toast meta reads "applied to this note" for note scope (template 15918). The app has no note-tag relation: grep for `note_tags` in `migrations.ts` returns zero, and tags attach only through `capture_tags` (`migrations.ts:38-44`). A Note carries an optional `captureId` and an optional anchor, and all four anchor kinds carry a `captureId` (`types.ts:705-723`, `noteAnchor.ts:13-77`), but two of the four NoteEditor mounts - `CreateNoteCard.tsx:65` and `AddNoteModal.tsx:85` - have no note at all yet, so no capture either.
  - Why blocking: The grounding comment names this as the vaguest line in the ticket and says to settle it before building the bar and to send the constraint back if the mock does not cover it. The mock does not cover it - it assumes a relation the app does not have. An implementer who guesses "apply to note.captureId" ships an action that throws or silently no-ops in two of four mounts; one who guesses "create the tag but apply it to nothing" ships a success toast for a write that did nothing.
  - Recommendation: Create-or-reuse the tag by name (looking it up in the tags list first, because tags.name is UNIQUE and `tagRepo.createTag` is a bare INSERT that throws on a duplicate - `tagRepo.ts:10-16`, `migrations.ts:34`); apply it to note.captureId ?? note.anchor.captureId when one exists, reusing the exact create-then-apply shape of `useCaptureTagEditor.ts:32-38`, with the toast meta naming the capture. When no capture exists, render the Tag button disabled with a title explaining why, per the grounding comment's own instruction to say so in the UI rather than fail on click. Send the divergence from the mock's "applied to this note" back through #708.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Two rulings, both flagged by the ticket's own grounding comment as things to settle before building. (1) The Backfill checkbox, because option (c) silently makes the PR evidence-affecting at blocking tier and removes its auto-merge eligibility. (2) The Tag action's behaviour on an unanchored note, because the mock's answer assumes a note-tag relation the schema does not have. Everything else is ready: the origin value, the IPC surface, the mutation hooks, the toast infrastructure and the E2E fixture all exist and were verified. One correction the issue should carry regardless of the rulings: the grounding comment's instruction to reuse CreateSelectorPopover "including kind detection" is wrong on both counts, and following it reaches a blocking-tier file.

### #701 Right-click context menus: one shared primitive plus a per-kind registry, four kinds

**Verdict.** This is a small-to-medium renderer ticket that the ruling makes sound larger than it is: Radix is already a production dependency exporting a fully accessible ContextMenu, so the real work is a thin wrapper, a four-kind action registry restricted to actions the app already exposes inline, and wiring on five row/chip components.

Size: medium. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against origin/main at 0bf207e5 the app has zero context-menu handlers: `grep -rn onContextMenu src/` returns one comment at `src/renderer/components/signals/SignalRow.tsx:25` plus a synthetic event in `tests/components/SignalRow.test.tsx:197-206`. That much of the ruling is correct.

WHAT ALREADY EXISTS AND CAN BE REUSED.

1. Radix. `radix-ui` ^1.6.1 is a production dependency (`package.json:145`), installed, and re-exports `ContextMenu` from `@radix-ui/react-context-menu@2.3.2` (`node_modules/radix-ui/src/index.ts:8`). The repo already consumes it: `src/renderer/components/ui/tabs.tsx:3` does `import { Tabs as TabsPrimitive } from "radix-ui"`. shadcn is configured at `components.json` with `ui` aliased to `@renderer/components/ui` and `iconLibrary: lucide`. The verified Radix menu implementation renders `role="menu"`, `role="menuitem"`, `role="menuitemcheckbox"`, `role="menuitemradio"` (`node_modules`/.pnpm/@radix-ui+react-menu@`2.1.19_.../dist/index.js`) and ships roving `tabindex`, typeahead, focus return, Escape, outside-click dismissal, portal and collision-aware positioning. So the roles/roving-tabindex/typeahead/focus-return/portal/positioning list in my brief is not work that has to be written; it is work that has to be adopted. Adding `context-menu.tsx` costs zero new dependencies.

2. The Escape precedence mechanism. `src/renderer/components/captures/`useCaptureSelection.ts`:85` reads `document.querySelector('[role="dialog"], [data-selection-escape-guard]')`. Radix menu content is `role="menu"`, never `role="dialog"`, so it is NOT caught by the first selector; the menu must opt in via `data-selection-escape-guard` on the Content, exactly as `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureSelectionBar.tsx:173` and :233 already do, or call `preventDefault()` from Radix's `onEscapeKeyDown` to satisfy the `defaultPrevented` early return at `useCaptureSelection.ts:71`. The attribute path is already covered by `tests/components/`useCaptureSelection.test.tsx`:192`. This mechanism exists on main today and needs neither #686 nor #687.

3. Semantic tokens for the mock's chrome. `--color-border-strong` (`globals.css:25`/134), `--shadow-overlay` (:146/153), `--color-elevated` (:10/125) and the `.neu-overlay` composite (:283-286) already exist, so the 252px/6px-radius/overlay-shadow panel is expressible in tokens.

WHAT MUST BE WRITTEN.

- `src/renderer/components/ui/context-menu.tsx`: a token-styled wrapper over Radix ContextMenu (Root/Trigger/Portal/Content/Item/Separator/Label), plus the header row the mock draws (icon, title, kind subtitle) which Radix has no slot for and which is just markup inside Content.
- A per-kind registry mapping `capture | note | selector | tag` to an ordered action list `{ icon, label, kbd?, danger?, separatorBefore?, run }`, taking the target entity plus the mutation hooks as inputs. No natural home exists; `src/renderer/components/contextMenu/` or `src/renderer/lib/contextMenuActions.ts` are both consistent with the tree.
- Wiring on five call sites: `CaptureItem.tsx` (both row treatments share one component), `NoteCard.tsx`, `SignalRow.tsx` (selector and tag variants share it), `TagBadge.tsx` (used at `CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:428` and `TagEditorPopover.tsx:62`).
- One line on `useCaptureSelection.ts` if the attribute is set on the shared Content rather than per call site.

THE ACCELERATOR RULE IS THE REAL SCOPE LEVER. The ruling forbids menu-only actions. Mapping the mock's action sets onto what the app has inline today: selector (mock `template.html:15676-15684`) offers edit pattern, toggle watching, duplicate, backfill, show matches, copy pattern, export, delete; `SignalRow.tsx` already has rename (`dblclick` at :90, Enter at :68-70), toggle enabled (:98-101 switch), delete (:196-206 and Backspace/Delete at :71-73) and `regex` toggle, but has no duplicate, no copy-pattern, no backfill and no per-selector export. tag (:15686-15693) offers filter, rename, duplicate, colour, merge, export, delete; the app has colour editing at `SignalDetailRail.tsx:199-210`, selector filters in `appStore.ts:154-168`, rename and delete on SignalRow, and nothing else. capture (:15639-15653) offers thirteen items; the app has star, open-source-URL, recapture and delete in the CaptureDetailsPanel menu (:252-302), download at `CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx:105-124`, add-tag via TagEditorPopover, add-note inline, and multi-select toggle, but no copy-URL, no copy-`SHA-256` (the renderer has `navigator.clipboard.writeText` only at `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx:259` and `AnalysisTab.tsx:105`), no rename and no duplicate. note (:15666-15674) offers eight; NoteCard has open, edit/rename and delete only.

Read strictly, the accelerator rule collapses the four menus to roughly 5, 4, 4 and 3 items respectively. Read loosely, it makes #701 also build copy-URL, copy-`SHA-256`, duplicate, merge, backfill and per-entity export as inline affordances first. That is the single biggest sizing variable and it is not settled anywhere.

GAP WORTH NAMING: `src/renderer/components/signals/**` and `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureItem.tsx` appear in NEITHER the include nor the exclusion list of `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md`. The signals directory was created after the 2026-07-31 survey and nobody added it. Absence from the include list means no backstop hit, so it does not change this ticket's tier, but the review record is silent where it should say `renderer chrome, excluded`.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Every file this ticket needs to touch is either explicitly excluded or absent from the include list, and the assessment is explicit at lines 63-64 that only the include list feeds the matcher. So the mechanical backstop does not fire and the PR carries no evidence obligations, matching the ruling's own "Not evidence-affecting" and the prep doc's five-ticket evidence set. One trap is worth spelling out in the issue before dispatch: `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` is BLOCKING tier (assessment:264, "Per-capture evidence export surface"). An implementer who reasonably decides to migrate the three existing hand-rolled menus onto the new primitive would pull that file into the diff and convert an auto-mergeable non-evidence PR into one owing an Evidence impact section, a known-answer test or written justification, human review and no auto-merge. `src/renderer/components/export/ExportMenu.tsx` was narrowed OUT to the exclusion list on 2026-08-23 (assessment:346), so migrating that one is safe; `CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` is not. Recommend an explicit non-goal: this ticket does not refactor existing menus.

Paths: `src/renderer/components/ui/** ,  exclusion list, assessment:330, "Shared UI primitives"`, `src/renderer/components/tags/** ,  exclusion list, assessment:327, "Renderer chrome"`, `src/renderer/components/notes/** ,  exclusion list, assessment:331`, `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx ,  exclusion list, assessment:322`, `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx ,  exclusion list, assessment:323`, `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureItem.tsx ,  in NEITHER list; absence from the include list means no backstop hit`, `src/renderer/components/signals/** ,  in NEITHER list; the directory postdates the 2026-07-31 survey`, `src/renderer/components/captures/useCaptureSelection.ts ,  in neither list`, `AVOID: src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx ,  include list, assessment:264, BLOCKING tier`

**Mock findings.**

- The shared `openCtx(kind, id, label, e, actions)` helper the ruling cites is real and sets `ctx` state plus a viewport-clamped position (`Math.max(8, Math.min(clientX, w - 262))`), resetting `ctxView` to 'root' and closing any other open menu. It calls `e.preventDefault(); e.stopPropagation()`. (template 15600-15608). No app equivalent. Radix does the clamping (collision detection) for free, so the app should not reimplement the arithmetic. Follow the mock's behaviour, not its code.
- The menu chrome: 252px wide, 6px radius, `--color-border-strong` border, `--color-card` background, `--shadow-overlay`, a header row with kind icon plus title plus subtitle, item rows with icon / label / right-aligned `kbd` hint / optional chevron, and a full-bleed dismiss scrim at z-index 200 with the panel at 201. (template 12875-12915). Every token exists in `src/renderer/styles/globals.css` (border-strong :25/:134, shadow-overlay :146/:153). Follow the mock. Radix Portal replaces the hand-rolled scrim.
- The mock's menu has NO accessibility semantics at all: the panel is a bare `div`, the items are bare `button`s, there is no `role="menu"`, no `role="menuitem"`, no `aria-*`, no arrow-key handling and no focus management. The scrim is the only dismissal path besides an item click. (template 12876-12912). Ruling point 4 says diverge: the app must add role=menu, focus management and Escape. The mock supplies zero guidance here, so this is invention, not conformance. Radix supplies all of it.
- Capture action set: Open capture (`kbd` ↵), Add/Remove from selection (⌘click), Open source URL, Copy URL (⌘C), Copy `SHA-256`, Add tag..., Quote into note (⌘J), Create selector from..., Favourite, Export... (drill-down), Rename (F2), Duplicate, Delete (⌫, danger). (template 15639-15653). Only open, selection toggle, open-source-URL, add-tag, favourite and delete have an inline route in the app today. Copy URL, Copy `SHA-256`, Rename and Duplicate have none, so under ruling point 3 they must either be dropped or built inline as well. Unsettled.
- Note action set: Open note (↵), Copy as markdown (⌘C), Copy [[wiki link]], Duplicate, Pin to case, Export..., Rename (F2), Delete. (template 15666-15674). `NoteCard.tsx` supports open, title edit and delete only. Five of the eight have no inline route.
- Selector action set: Edit pattern (↵), Toggle watching, Duplicate, Backfill existing captures, Show matches, Copy pattern (⌘C), Export..., Delete. Tag action set: Filter captures by tag (↵), Rename (F2), Duplicate, Change colour..., Merge into..., Export..., Delete. (template 15676-15693). `SignalRow.tsx:60-79` and :90-206 already give rename, toggle enabled and delete with keyboard equivalents; `SignalDetailRail.tsx:199-210` gives tag colour; `appStore.ts:154-168` gives selector filters. Duplicate, backfill, copy pattern, merge and per-entity export have no inline route.
- The mock's menu is multi-select aware. `ctxVals` computes `n = multiCount(kind) > 1 && isMulti(kind,id) ? multiCount : 1`, pluralizes the noun through `ctxNoun`, retitles the header to '3 captures selected' with subtitle '⌘-click to change selection', and swaps 'Add to selection' for 'Clear selection'. (template 15766-15768, 15806-15809). The app has exactly this multi-select model for captures (`appStore` `selectedCaptureIds`, `useCaptureSelection.ts:17-67`, CaptureSelectionBar). The ruling does not mention multi-select at all. Recommend following the mock for the capture kind only; the other three kinds have no multi-select in the app.
- A 'Customise this menu...' edit mode. `toggleCtxEdit` flips `ctxEditing`, every item grows a checkbox, and `ctxPrefs` persists per-`kind|label` visibility, with hidden actions said to live in Settings. (template 15764, 15774-15791, 15816-15818). The app has no per-menu preference store and Settings has no such panel. This is designed but unbuildable inside this ticket's scope. Recommend explicitly excluding it and recording the divergence on #708.
- An 'Export...' drill-down submenu, shared by every kind: Originals to folder, ZIP + manifest, PDF report, CSV of extracted data, Copy as markdown, Reveal in Finder. (template 15614-15627). The app's export is case-scoped (`ExportMenu.tsx`, `ExportDialog.tsx`) plus per-capture download (`CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx`). Per-note, per-selector and per-tag export does not exist. Wiring this drill-down would also drag `CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx`, a blocking-tier path (assessment:264), into the diff. Recommend dropping the drill-down.
- 23 `sc-camel-on-context-menu` attributes exist, but only 21 are entity bindings. Line 12876 is the dismiss scrim's own `closeCtx`, and line 12220 is the Chrome Extension screen's simulated web page, whose handler `onExtCtx` (16097-16110) opens the in-page selection popover and never calls `openCtx`. (template 9270, 9383, 10090, 10123, 10630, 10755, 10915, 10963, 11174, 11324, 11349, 11374, 11529, 11559, 11579, 11628, 11697, 11740, 11823, 11905, 12220, 12802, 12876). Mapping by screen: 9270 TopBar capture-pipeline dropdown (event); 9383 Dashboard case card (case); 10090/10123 Captures rows, detailed and compact (capture); 10630 Wayback snapshot row (snapshot); 10755 capture details tag chip (tag); 10915/10963 Signals selector and tag rows (selector, tag); 11174 Selectors screen table row (selector); 11324/11349 Notes rows (note); 11374 note tag chip (tag); 11529 suggested-selector row (suggestion); 11559/11579 backlink out/in rows (link); 11628 Tags screen row (tag); 11697/11740/11823/11905 Data screen tree node, artifact row, MHTML part row, ledger row (folder, file, part, ledger); 12802 export dialog pinned-Wayback row (snapshot).
- Ten of the 21 entity bindings fall inside the four ruled kinds, but two of those ten sit on the Selectors (screen label at 11071) and Tags (11599) screens, which the default `overviewVariant: consolidated` filters out of the sidebar. (template 10090, 10123, 10755, 10915, 10963, 11174, 11324, 11349, 11374, 11628). Under the default variant the reachable in-scope set is EIGHT bindings, not ten: 11174 and 11628 are classic-only. The app has no Selectors or Tags screen either (SignalsOverview consolidated them in wave 2), so the two classic-only bindings have no app surface to land on and should be ignored rather than counted as missing work.

**Conflicts.**

- #391 notes selection-to-Selector/Tag in `src/renderer/components/notes/` at #391 works `NoteEditor.tsx` (128 lines) plus a new sibling; #701 wraps `NoteCard.tsx`'s read-only card root, disjoint. Different files inside one directory. No serialization needed. If both are in flight, #701's `NoteCard.tsx` edit is a wrapper around the existing card root and rebases trivially.
- #695 per-list capture search in `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx` at #695 adds a client-side title/URL filter over the loaded list and intersects it with selector filters; #701 may touch the same file if the trigger is wired at the list rather than inside `CaptureItem.tsx`, append-only. Wire the trigger inside `CaptureItem.tsx`, not `CaptureList.tsx`, and the two never meet. If `CaptureList.tsx` must change, second-to-land resolves; the regions are the row map versus the filter chain.
- #686 dialog role persists through exit animation in `src/renderer/components/captures/useCaptureSelection.ts` at lines 81-92, the Escape guard, serialize. #686 lands first if both hold a slot, because #686 owns this region and its acceptance criteria allow replacing the `[data-selection-escape-guard]` DOM query with a state signal. #701 needs no edit here at all if it sets the existing attribute, so this is an ordering preference, not a dependency. Do not block #701's dispatch on it.
- #687 CommandPalette has no role=dialog in `src/renderer/components/layout/CommandPalette.tsx` at the palette panel element, plus the store check at `useCaptureSelection.ts:90`, disjoint. No overlap. #701 touches neither `CommandPalette.tsx` nor the store-check line. The claimed dependency does not exist.
- #803 Data screen rebuild in `src/renderer/components/contextMenu/registry.ts (new)` at the registry's kind union and action-list shape, serialize. #701 lands first and defines the registry; #803 appends the file, folder, part and ledger kinds when it builds the rows they attach to, per ruling 1. If #803 lands first it must ship those rows without menus. The prep doc's merge order omits #803 entirely, so this ordering has to be stated somewhere before dispatch.
- #702 Recording indicator, #704 Page-tab archived-copy banner in `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx and the capture viewer Page tab` at n/a, disjoint. No shared files. The prep doc groups all three as a trailing batch; they can run in any order or concurrently.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) Ruling 1: "`ui/` has no menu primitive today, so this ticket writes the accessible one" (prep doc line ~150; the same wording is in the 2026-08-23 ruling comment on #701, point 4). My briefing sharpened this to `the repo has no Radix or Headless UI`.
  - Correction: `ui/` genuinely has no menu primitive, but the accessible one does not have to be written. `radix-ui` ^1.6.1 is already a PRODUCTION dependency and already consumed by the tree; it re-exports a complete, accessible ContextMenu. shadcn is configured for this repo. Adopting it is a zero-new-dependency change that supplies roles, roving `tabindex`, typeahead, focus return, Escape, outside-click, portal and collision-aware positioning. Hand-rolling those is roughly 250 avoidable lines that the coverage gate then has to cover.
  - Evidence: `package.json:145` (`"radix-ui": "^1.6.1"`); `node_modules/radix-ui/src/index.ts:8` (`export * as ContextMenu from '@radix-ui/react-context-menu'`); `src/renderer/components/ui/tabs.tsx:3` (`import { Tabs as TabsPrimitive } from "radix-ui"`); `components.json` (shadcn config, `ui` alias); `node_modules`/.pnpm/@radix-ui+react-menu@`2.1.19_.../dist/index.js` emits role: "menu" / "`menuitem`" / "`menuitemcheckbox`" / "`menuitemradio`"
- (changes-an-estimate) Prep doc open items, final section: "#686 and #687 should precede #701." Ruling 1 states it harder: "#686/#687 land first." My brief calls it a sequencing claim nothing has tested.

A context menu is role=`menu`, never role="dialog" (verified in the installed Radix menu build), so neither fix reaches its code path. The Escape-precedence mechanism #701 actually needs already exists and is already used and tested: the `[data-selection-escape-guard]` opt-in at `useCaptureSelection.ts:85`, set at `CaptureSelectionBar.tsx:173` and :233, asserted at `tests/components/`useCaptureSelection.test.tsx`:192`. The one real coupling is that #686's accepted acceptance criteria explicitly permit `giving the guard a state signal`, which would replace that attribute; if #686 takes that shape after #701 lands, #686's author updates one call site. Recommendation: drop the hard blocker, keep a soft ordering rule that #686 lands first if both are in a slot at once, because #686 owns `useCaptureSelection.ts`.
  - Evidence: `src/renderer/components/captures/`useCaptureSelection.ts`:71` (`defaultPrevented` early return), :85 (the two-selector guard), :90 (the palette store check #687 removes); `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureSelectionBar.tsx:173`,233; `tests/components/`useCaptureSelection.test.tsx`:192`; `gh` issue view 686/687 acceptance criteria
- (changes-the-plan) Prep doc line 200: "Merge order: #398 -> #392 -> #391 -> #399 -> #401 -> #393 -> #701/#702/#704 -> #405."

The order was written before #803 existed and was never amended. Either #701 precedes #803 so the registry exists to extend, or #803 ships Data rows with no menus and a follow-up adds the four kinds. That needs stating, not inferring.
  - Evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md:200` contains no #803; the same document's ruling 7 introduces #803, and `gh` confirms #803 is OPEN and labelled enhancement,evidence-affecting,redesign
- (changes-an-estimate) Ruling 1 / #701 ruling comment point 4: "`CaptureMenu.tsx`, `CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` and `ExportMenu.tsx` are three hand-rolled menus, so this ticket writes the accessible one."
  - Correction: All three are already partly accessible: each carries `role="menu"` on the panel, `role="menuitem"` on every item, `aria-haspopup="menu"` and `aria-expanded` on the trigger, an Escape handler and an outside-click handler. What all three lack is focus management: no focus moves into the menu on open, no roving `tabindex`, no arrow-key navigation, no typeahead and no focus return. Stating the gap precisely matters because it is exactly the part Radix supplies and the part a hand-roll would have to get right.

No ArrowDown/ArrowUp handling in any of the three.

Template line 12876 is the dismiss scrim binding `closeCtx` to itself, and line 12220 is the Chrome Extension screen's simulated page, whose handler `onExtCtx` (16097) opens the in-page selection popover and never calls `openCtx`. Both are shallow, but the conflict map does not mention either. **Test plan.** Unit / component, `jsdom` Vitest project (`vitest.config.ts:118-131`, include `tests/components/**/*.test.tsx`):

1. The primitive. Extend `tests/components/`uiPrimitives.test`.tsx` (118 lines today, already `// @vitest-environment jsdom` at :1 and already covers SectionLabel, Badge, CardPanel, CardTitle, Dialog), or add `tests/components/ContextMenu.test.tsx` beside it. Pin: `contextmenu` on the trigger opens a `role="menu"` with the expected `role="menuitem"` children; ArrowDown/ArrowUp move focus between items; Escape closes and returns focus to the trigger; an outside `pointerdown` closes; the Content carries `data-selection-escape-guard`. If Radix is adopted, these assertions verify the wrapper wired the primitive correctly rather than re-testing Radix.

   KNOWN FRICTION, PLAN FOR IT: the `jsdom` project declares NO `setupFiles`. `tests/components/`resizeObserverStub`.ts` exists precisely because of that and says so at :3-5. Radix menus additionally need `Element.prototype.hasPointerCapture`, `releasePointerCapture` and `scrollIntoView`, none of which `jsdom` implements. The repository has no precedent: the only Radix component in the tree is Tabs (`src/renderer/components/ui/tabs.tsx:3`), used only by `SettingsView.tsx` and `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`, and Tabs is a much simpler primitive. Budget a stub helper next to `resizeObserverStub.ts` and expect a round of `jsdom` errors.

2. The registry. A pure unit test over the four kinds asserting shape (label, run, ordering, separator placement) and, critically, a known-answer test for the accelerator rule: every registry entry declares an `inlineRoute` identifier, and the test asserts the set of declared routes is non-empty for every action. That turns ruling point 3 from prose into something CI enforces.

3. Per-surface wiring, extending files that already exist: `tests/components/CaptureItem.test.tsx`, `tests/components/NoteCard.test.tsx`, `tests/components/SignalRow.test.tsx` (which already fires a synthetic `contextmenu` at :197-206 and asserts it propagates ,  that test will need updating once the row consumes the event), `tests/components/CaptureSelectionBar.test.tsx` if multi-select-aware capture items are in scope.

4. Escape precedence. Extend `tests/components/`useCaptureSelection.test`.tsx`, which already builds a `data-selection-escape-guard` element at :192. Add the case that matters: with captures selected AND a context menu open, Escape closes the menu and leaves the selection intact; a second Escape clears the selection. That is the regression this ticket can actually introduce.

E2E, Playwright (e2e/, `30s` timeout, 1 worker, `pnpm build` runs first). One new spec, `e2e/context-menu.spec.ts`, doing a real right-click on a capture row and on a signal row, asserting the menu opens, an item runs and the DB-visible effect lands, and Escape closes without clearing the multi-selection. `e2e/capture-multiselect.spec.ts` already exercises the selection model and is the closest sibling.

Coverage. `pnpm test:coverage` then `pnpm coverage:diff`, both re-run after any edit (#508). The 90%-of-changed-lines gate is the real risk on this ticket: an action registry is dozens of tiny `run` closures, and an uninvoked closure is an uncovered line. Either invoke every closure from the registry unit test with mocked mutation hooks, or keep the closures thin and put the logic in already-covered hooks.

**Open questions.**

- Does the accelerator rule mean the menus ship ONLY actions the app already exposes inline or by keyboard, or does this ticket also build the missing inline routes (copy URL, copy `SHA-256`, duplicate, per-entity export, merge tags, backfill selector) so the mock's fuller action sets can ship?
  - Why blocking: It is the difference between a ~400-line branch and a multi-day one, and it changes what the menus contain, which is the whole deliverable. Verified gap: of the mock's 13 capture actions the app has 6, of 8 note actions it has 3, of 8 selector actions it has 3, of 7 tag actions it has 4. An implementer who guesses "build the missing ones" produces a branch nobody scoped; one who guesses "intersection only" ships menus a reviewer may call incomplete against the mock. Neither guess is recoverable at review.
  - Recommendation: Intersection only. Ruling point 3's own wording is "reachable inline or by an EXISTING keyboard path" and "a missing menu is never a missing capability" ,  both read as a mirror of current capability, not an expansion. File the missing capabilities as separate issues (they are per-kind features with their own acceptance criteria) and let the registry absorb them later at near-zero cost. This is also the only reading under which "ONE ticket, not a shared component plus staged per-surface adoption" is a sane call.
- Build the primitive on the Radix ContextMenu already in `package.json`, or hand-roll it as the ruling's wording implies?
  - Why blocking: The ruling says `this ticket writes the accessible one`, and my own briefing asserted the repo has no Radix. Both point an unattended agent at hand-rolling roving `tabindex`, typeahead, focus return, portal and collision-aware positioning ,  roughly 250 lines of subtle code that the 90%-changed-line coverage gate then has to cover, and that this repo has already got wrong three times (CaptureMenu, CaptureDownloadMenu, ExportMenu all have roles and Escape but no focus management at all). Meanwhile `radix-ui` ^1.6.1 is a production dependency at `package.json:145`, already imported at `src/renderer/components/ui/tabs.tsx:3`, and its ContextMenu supplies every one of those behaviours. An agent that hand-rolls because it believed the framing has done avoidable work that a reviewer will then ask it to undo.
  - Recommendation: Adopt Radix. Zero new dependencies, shadcn is already configured at `components.json` with the right aliases, and it makes the four-kind registry the only real invention. Accept two costs explicitly: the `jsdom` test stubs described in the test plan, and Radix's default `modal` behaviour (pointer-events lock on body) which should be evaluated under Electron. If the maintainer wants the hand-roll for control, say so on the issue, because the effort estimate roughly doubles and the ticket moves from medium to large.
- Is the capture menu multi-select aware, per the mock, and if so does a right-click on a row OUTSIDE the current selection replace the selection or act on the single row?
  - Why blocking: The app has a real multi-select model (`appStore.selectedCaptureIds`, `useCaptureSelection.ts:17-67`, CaptureSelectionBar) and the mock is explicitly selection-aware (template 15766-15768, 15806-15809: pluralized noun, '3 captures selected' header, 'Clear selection' replacing 'Add to selection'). The two possible behaviours produce different destructive outcomes for the Delete item ,  one capture or twelve ,  and there is no way to add this later without changing what an already-shipped menu item does. The ruling does not mention multi-select at all.
  - Recommendation: Follow the mock for the capture kind and only the capture kind, since the other three have no multi-select. Right-click on a row inside the selection acts on the set; right-click on a row outside it acts on that row alone and leaves the selection untouched (the mock's `isMulti(kind,id)` check at 15767 is exactly this). Add the explicit acceptance criterion, because Delete is destructive.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Three things, in order of cost. (1) The three blocking questions above, chiefly the intersection-versus-expansion call, which is the difference between a medium branch and a large one. (2) Acceptance criteria. The issue has none. The 2026-08-21 comment said plainly "no acceptance criteria can be written until points 1 and 2 are ruled"; points 1 through 5 were ruled on 2026-08-23 and nobody went back and wrote them. Every other wave-3 ticket I can see carries a checkbox list ending in the verify loop; #701 ends at scope. An unattended agent has no definition of done and no list to self-check against. (3) The label. `gh api repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/701/labels` returns exactly `enhancement` and `redesign` ,  no `ready-for-agent`, so the dispatch routine will not pick it up regardless. Two things worth adding to the criteria while writing them: an explicit non-goal that this ticket does not refactor `CaptureMenu.tsx`, `CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` or `ExportMenu.tsx` onto the new primitive (`CaptureDownloadMenu.tsx` is blocking-tier and would change the PR's merge path), and an explicit non-goal for the mock's 'Customise this menu...' preference mode, which has no backing store anywhere in the app.

### #702 top-bar Recording indicator

**Verdict.** The Recording indicator already exists on main and is already bound to `sessionActive` exactly as ruled, so the only code this ticket owes is deleting the three-line early return in `ConnectionStatus.tsx` that hides the Connected chip during a session.

Size: small. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against origin/main at 0bf207e5 the ticket is almost entirely already built.

Already exists and is reusable as-is: the REC pill at `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx:118-123`, gated on `sessionActive` read from the store at :20. Its markup is a structural match for the mock pill at `template.html:9244-9249` (rounded-full, 6px dot, pulse, 10px/500 "REC"; Tailwind red-500 is #ef4444 and red-400 is #f87171, the mock's exact values). Nothing about the indicator itself needs writing.

What must be written: remove the `sessionActive` read and early return at `src/renderer/components/status/ConnectionStatus.tsx:6` and :8-10. That early return is the app hiding its own Connected chip while a session runs, and the ruling reverses it. Everything else in that file already does the right thing.

What `sessionActive` actually means today, since the ruling binds the indicator to it:
- Renderer store field, `src/renderer/stores/`appStore.ts`:10` and :67, initialised false.
- It mirrors the main-process session state machine's recording flag. `src/main/services/session.ts:60` holds it; only `start()` (:101-105) and `stop()` (:107-110) move it, and both call `notifyChange()` (:72-74), which emits SessionStateEvent.
- That event reaches the renderer through `src/main/index.ts:394-399` -> IPC_CHANNELS.SESSION_STATE_CHANGED -> `src/renderer/hooks/`useServerStatus.ts:14`-15` -> `setSessionActive`. `useServerStatus` is mounted once at `src/renderer/main.tsx:11`.
- Two real drivers reach `start()`/`stop()`: the renderer IPC handlers at `src/main/`ipcHandlers.ts:257`-269`, driven by the top-bar toggle in `SessionControls.tsx:24-44`, and the extension's HTTP routes at `src/main/services/`captureServer.ts:244`-256`. Both hit one shared instance: `index.ts:394` constructs it, :413 passes it to `registerIpcHandlers` and :417 to `startCaptureServer`, and `captureServer.ts:603` assigns it over the module-level default at :76.

So the flag is genuinely driven, from both the app and the extension, through a single instance. The indicator is not bound to a dead field. Three caveats, all verified:

1. No mount-time hydration. `sessionQueryOptions` (`src/renderer/lib/api/session.ts:8-12`) has zero consumers anywhere in src/, tests/ or e2e/ - grep returns only the definition. After a renderer reload the store reads `false` regardless of main's state. This self-heals on entering any case route, because `CaseWorkspace.tsx:29-35` fires `activateCase.mutate` on mount and `activateCase` calls `notifyChange()` (`session.ts:96-99`) carrying the `true` `sessionActive`. The uncovered window is the Dashboard route after a reload, where the pill renders and would read Idle.
2. `countCapture()` deliberately does not notify (`session.ts:112-117`). Irrelevant to `sessionActive`, noted so nobody "fixes" it.
3. `SessionControls.tsx:20` returns `null` when `!connectedToExtension`. If the extension heartbeat lapses (`10s`, `session.ts:13`) mid-session, `sessionActive` stays `true` in main, the REC pill keeps rendering, and the only in-app control to stop it has vanished. Pre-existing, out of scope, but it is the strongest argument for the ruling: after this ticket the Connected chip stays visible and tells the operator exactly why the toggle went away. Worth filing separately.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** All three files the branch should touch sit on the explicit exclusion list in `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md`: `src/renderer/components/status/**` at line 329, `src/renderer/components/layout/**` at line 325, `src/renderer/hooks/**` at line 333, each reasoned as `Renderer chrome`. Neither blocking nor advisory: the advisory tier per ADR-0014 is the cross-process contracts block and the software distribution block only, and none of these are in it. So no `evidence-affecting` label, no Evidence impact section, no reviewer disposition line, and per ADR-0014 the PR is eligible to auto-merge on green checks plus an agent/pre-pass success verdict. This agrees with the prep doc and with the ruling comment's own `Not evidence-affecting`. One trap: `src/main/services/session.ts` is BLOCKING tier (assessment line 175, "Active-case state deciding which case a capture is filed under"). An implementer who decides to fix the mount-time hydration gap by changing the session service rather than the renderer drags this ticket into the full evidence gate for no behavioural gain. Fix hydration in `useServerStatus.ts` or not at all.

Paths: `src/renderer/components/status/ConnectionStatus.tsx`, `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx`, `src/renderer/hooks/useServerStatus.ts`, `src/main/services/session.ts`

**Files.**

| Path | Lines | Risk | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `src/renderer/components/status/ConnectionStatus.tsx` | 6, 8-10 | low | Delete the `sessionActive` store read at :6 and the `if (sessionActive) return null` block at :8-10. This is the entire behavioural change the ruling asks for. Note this is a pure deletion, so it contributes nothing to diff coverage. |
| `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx` | 20, 118-123 | low | No functional change required - the REC pill is already here and already bound to `sessionActive`. Only touch it to add a `data-testid`, and only if a test that renders TopBar lands with it (see the coverage trap in `testPlan`). |
| `tests/components/ConnectionStatus.test.tsx` | new file | low | New `jsdom` component test proving the chip renders while `sessionActive` is true. No test covers ConnectionStatus, SessionControls or TopBar today. |
| `src/renderer/hooks/useServerStatus.ts` | 9-15 | medium | Optional and probably out of scope: hydrate the store from window.birdbrain.session.snapshot() on mount so the Dashboard route is not stale after a reload. Only worth doing if the maintainer wants caveat 1 closed inside this ticket. |

**Mock findings.**

- The REC pill markup, gated on `recActive`. Rounded-full, 1px rgba(239,68,68,.2) border, rgba(239,68,68,.1) fill, 2px/8px padding, 6px gap, 6px #ef4444 dot animated with `bbpulse 2s ease-in-out infinite` (`keyframes` at 9079), 10px/500 #f87171 text reading REC. (template 9244-9249). None. `TopBar.tsx:118-123` is already a structural and colour match. No divergence to resolve and nothing to build.
- `const sessionOn = s.autoCapture` - the mock derives recording from the auto-capture switch alone. (template 13468). Ruled divergence 1. The app binds to `sessionActive` (`appStore.ts:10`) and keeps `autoCaptureMode` (`schemas.ts:530`) a Signals concern. Do not rewire this toward the mock.
- `recActive: sessionOn` and `recDotColor`/`recLabel: sessionOn ? 'Recording' : 'Idle'`, the latter consumed only by the health popover row at 9265. (template 13538, 13569, 9265). None on labels. `CaptureHealth.tsx:155-157` already renders the same Recording/Idle dot inside its popover, and the top-bar pill already says REC. The visible strings are already right.
- `showConnected: extConnected && !sessionOn` is computed but NEVER referenced by any markup in the template. grep for `showConnected` returns exactly two hits: this definition and nothing else. The mock's actual Connected control is the always-rendered green button at 9250-9258 reading 'Connected . 12', which is the Connected chip and the Capture Pipeline health button merged into one, opening the pipeline popover. (template 13537, 9250-9258). This inverts the ticket's framing. The mock does not hide the Connected chip - that state is dead. The APP hides it, at `ConnectionStatus.tsx:8-10`, code that predates the redesign (introduced in bf43c7af). So 'the Connected chip never hides' is a fix to the app that moves it TOWARD the mock, not a divergence from it. The genuine unbuilt divergence is that the mock merges Connected into the health button; the app keeps ConnectionStatus and CaptureHealth as two separate controls at `TopBar.tsx:125-126`. That merge is not in this ticket's scope and no ruling covers it.
- `showSessionControls: inCase && extConnected` is also computed and never referenced by markup. The mock's top-bar right cluster is, in order: REC pill (9244), Connected/health button (9250), Export menu gated on `inCase` (9287), Browser (9306), Settings (9310), Theme (9313). The mock.s top bar has no Auto-Capture toggle at all. (template 13536, 9244-9315). The app renders SessionControls (`TopBar.tsx:116`) with a visible label and `aria-label` of 'Auto-Capture' (`SessionControls.tsx:49`, :54) which in fact toggles `sessionActive`. After this ticket the top bar reads [Auto-Capture toggle][REC][Connected], and the label contradicts the ruling's own sentence that auto-capture is a Signals concern. See `openQuestions`. The Browser button at 9306 is already ruled out by wave-3 ruling 5.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) Ruling comment on #702: "The mock hides it while recording (`showConnected`: `extConnected` && !`sessionOn`, line 13930). During recording is exactly when the operator most needs to know the extension is connected... Divergence from the design."
  - Correction: `showConnected` is computed at template line 13537 and consumed by nothing - it is dead state in the mock. The mock's Connected control (template 9250-9258) is unconditional markup with no sc-if wrapper. So the mock never hides it either. The component that hides it is the app's own `ConnectionStatus.tsx:8-10`, from commit bf43c7af, long before the redesign. The ruling's outcome is right and should not change; its rationale is aimed at the wrong artifact, and the change is a bug fix that converges on the mock rather than a divergence from it. Consequence: only one of the two ruled items (the `sessionActive`-vs-autoCapture binding) is a real divergence owing an entry on #708.
  - Evidence: grep -n '`showConnected`' /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html returns only line 13537; `template.html:9250-9258` has no sc-if guard; git log -S 'if (`sessionActive`) {' -- `src/renderer/components/status/ConnectionStatus.tsx` -> bf43c7af
- (cosmetic) Ruling comment on #702 and the issue body cite template lines 13861 (`sessionOn`), 13930 (`showConnected`) and 13962 (`recLabel`/`recDotColor`).
  - Correction: Those line numbers do not match /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html, the unpack this wave was told to cite. The real lines are 13468, 13537 and 13569 - a constant offset of roughly 393. Line 13861 in the current unpack is annotation pin-popover code. An implementer following the ruling's numbers reads the wrong thing.
  - Evidence: grep -n '`sessionOn`|`recLabel`|`recDotColor`|`showConnected`' /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html -> 13468, 13537, 13538, 13569; `sed` -n '13861p' shows `pinPopClick`/`cancelPin`
- (changes-an-estimate) Ruling comment on #702: "Divergence from the design, both parts, recorded on #708."
  - Correction: Not recorded as of 0bf207e5. #708's body and all its comments contain zero occurrences of `702`, "Recording" or `Connected chip`. Its divergence list runs items 9 through 14 and covers #397, #402, #400, #406 and the tour, not this. Per the repo rule about not asserting something is filed until it is, appending the entry to #708 is a task this ticket still owes.
  - Evidence: `gh` issue view 708 --comments | grep -c '702|Recording|Connected chip' -> 0
- (changes-the-plan) Ruling comment on #702: "`TopBar.tsx` already renders both signals: SessionControls at :116 (gated on `activeCaseId`) and ConnectionStatus at :125."
  - Correction: True but incomplete in the way that matters. `TopBar.tsx:118-123` already renders the Recording pill itself, gated on `sessionActive` read at :20 - that is the indicator this ticket exists to add is already on main, already bound to the ruled flag, and already a pixel match for the mock. The ticket is not "build an indicator"; it is `stop hiding the Connected chip`, which is one deletion. Nobody reading the issue body, the ruling or the prep doc would learn this.
  - Evidence: `src/renderer/components/layout/TopBar.tsx:20`, 118-123
- (cosmetic) Prep doc, wave-3 ticket set: `#702 top-bar Recording indicator | split out of #400 | needs a ruling: yes`, and merge order `#398 -> #392 -> #391 -> #399 -> #401 -> #393 -> #701/#702/#704 -> #405`.
  - Correction: Sequencing is fine but understates independence. #702 touches no file any other wave-3 ticket touches (verified: #701, #704, #695, #405, #803, #391 bodies contain no reference to TopBar, ConnectionStatus, SessionControls or components/status). It can land at any point, including first, and does not need to queue behind #391.
  - Evidence: `gh` issue view for 701/704/695/405/803/391 grepped for TopBar|ConnectionStatus|SessionControls|status/ -> no hits

**Test plan.** No existing test covers `TopBar.tsx`, `ConnectionStatus.tsx` or `SessionControls.tsx` anywhere in tests/ or e2e/. `tests/components/CaptureHealth.test.tsx` is the closest sibling and the right template.

New file: `tests/components/ConnectionStatus.test.tsx`. Copy the harness from `tests/components/CaptureHealth.test.tsx:1-28` - the `// @vitest-environment jsdom` directive on line 1, `fakeBridge()` from ../renderer/`fakeBridge` in `beforeEach`, `cleanup()` in `afterEach`. ConnectionStatus needs no QueryClientProvider (it reads only the Zustand store), so the wrapper can be dropped. Reset the store the way `tests/renderer/stores/`appStore.test.ts:6`-9` does. Three cases:
1. `connectedToExtension` `true`, `sessionActive` `true` -> the Connected chip renders. This is the regression the ticket exists to prevent and it fails on main today.
2. `connectedToExtension` `true`, `sessionActive` `false` -> renders.
3. `connectedToExtension` `false` -> renders nothing, whatever `sessionActive` is (guards the HOTFIX comment at `ConnectionStatus.tsx:32-33`).

Coverage-gate trap, name it in the PR body. `scripts/diff-coverage.mjs` scores only the `+` side of the -U0 diff (`changedLinesByFile` at :63-78, min 90 at :29). The ConnectionStatus edit is a pure deletion and contributes zero changed lines. So if the branch also adds a single `data-testid` to `TopBar.tsx:119` with no test that renders TopBar, that lone added line is the whole denominator and scores 0%, and CI's `test` job fails a PR that `pnpm test` called green. Either skip the `testid` or land a TopBar render test with it - and TopBar needs `useMatchRoute`, `useNavigate`, `useRouter`, `useTheme` and `useUpdateStatus` stubbed, which is a real cost for a testid.

E2E: none required, and no existing spec is affected. `e2e/cases.spec.ts`, `e2e/empty-captures-state.spec.ts` and `e2e/screenshot-zoom-bar.spec.ts` key on [`data-testid`="topbar-case-name"] only. Driving a session from E2E is possible via page.evaluate on window.birdbrain.session.start() after activating a case, but not by clicking, because `SessionControls.tsx:20` hides the toggle without a connected extension. No spec does this today; treat it as optional and not worth the setup for a chip-visibility change.

Non-code deliverable: append the surviving divergence (indicator binds to `sessionActive`, not `autoCapture`) to #708's "where the app wins" list. The ruling asserts this is already recorded and it is not.

**Open questions.**

- The top-bar control that sets `sessionActive` is labelled and `aria-labelled` "Auto-Capture" (`SessionControls.tsx:49` and :54), and `CapturesGettingStarted.tsx:27-28` instructs the operator to `Toggle Auto-Capture on in the header bar to start recording`. The ruling's stated consequence is "auto-capture becomes a Signals-screen concern only, shown where it is set" - but `AutoCaptureCard.tsx:52-57` is where `autoCaptureMode` is actually set, and it is a different control with the same name. After this ticket the top bar reads [Auto-Capture toggle][REC][Connected], where the toggle has nothing to do with the `autoCaptureMode` setting. Does #702 relabel that toggle, or leave the collision?
  - Why blocking: An implementer who guesses "rename it" changes a visible label, an `aria-label`, onboarding copy in a second component, and breaks `tests/components/CapturesGettingStarted.test.tsx:24` which asserts the string "Enable Auto-Capture" - turning a 15-line ticket into a four-file rename with copy decisions, on a PR that is eligible to auto-merge. An implementer who guesses "leave it" ships a top bar whose own ruling comment reads as contradicted on screen, and a reviewer will send it back. Either guess costs a round.
  - Recommendation: Leave it. Keep #702 to the one deletion plus its test, and file the naming collision as its own issue covering `SessionControls.tsx:49`/:54, `CapturesGettingStarted.tsx:27-28` and its test. The rename needs a decision on what the session toggle is actually called (`Record`, `Session`, "Capture Session") which is a copy question no wave-3 ruling has answered, and it is not what #702 was split out of #400 to do.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Acceptance criteria, which the issue explicitly says cannot be written until the ruling lands - the ruling landed 2026-08-23 and the body was never updated, so the issue still reads as an open three-way decision. It also needs the Auto-Capture label question above answered, and its body corrected to say that the REC pill already exists at `TopBar.tsx:118-123` rather than implying it must be built. Everything else is settled: the ruling is unambiguous, the evidence tier is none, and there are no conflicts.

### #704 Page-tab archived-copy banner

**Verdict.** A ~45-line presentational banner mounted in `CaptureViewer.tsx` above the Page-tab branch, reading capture.timestamp, whose only real difficulty is copy: the mock's banner text asserts `network disabled`, which the app does not enforce on the mhtml-sandbox partition.

Size: small. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against origin/main at 0bf207e5 there is no "archived copy" string anywhere in src/, e2e/ or tests/ (grep -rni "archived copy" returns nothing), so this is entirely new UI.

Mechanically it is one presentational component plus one mount. The Page tab is rendered in `CaptureViewer.tsx:243-258`, which has TWO branches: `capture.format === 'mhtml'` renders `<MhtmlViewer captureId>` inside `div.h-full.w-full.overflow-hidden` (243-246), and the legacy `format === 'html'` path renders an `<iframe sandbox="" srcDoc={content}>` (247-258). The mock puts the banner in a flex-column wrapper with the banner `flex-shrink:0` and the rendered page `flex:1;min-height:0` (`template.html:10366-10375`), so the app's `div.h-full.w-full.overflow-hidden` at 244 becomes `flex flex-col` with the webview at `flex-1 min-h-0`.

WHERE IT MOUNTS DECIDES THE MERGE GATE. `src/renderer/components/captures/MhtmlViewer.tsx` is blocking tier (`docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:262`, rationale "Evidence rendering invariants"). `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureViewer.tsx` is on the exclusion list of the same document, line 321, rationale `Renderer chrome`. Mount the banner in `CaptureViewer.tsx` and the ticket is non-evidence and auto-mergeable; put one line in `MhtmlViewer.tsx` and it acquires the full ADR-0004 obligations, human review and no auto-merge. It also stays testable: `tests/components/CaptureViewer.test.tsx:17-19` mocks MhtmlViewer out entirely, so a banner inside MhtmlViewer would be invisible to the existing suite and would need webview stubbing in jsdom.

WHAT TIMESTAMP FIELD. `capture.timestamp` (`src/shared/types.ts:93`), not `capture.createdAt` (`:95`). `timestamp` is the value anchored in the signed manifest capture entry (`captureLifecycle.ts:189` feeds `withCaptureEntry`, and `manifest.ts:238` is the `type:'capture'` entry's `timestamp`); `createdAt` is only the main-process clock at DB insert (`captureRepo.ts:118,131`). The app's existing label for it is "Captured at" (`ForensicsTab.tsx:59`) and "Captured" (`CaptureDetailsPanel.tsx:345`). Both are already visible in the right-hand details panel while the Page tab is open, so the banner is a third statement of the same fact on one screen; that is the point of the ticket, but the formats must agree.

FORMAT. Reuse `formatCaptureTimestampFull` (`src/renderer/lib/formatRelativeTime.ts:56-60`), which is UTC-pinned with the comment at `:10-12` giving the exact reason ("this string is the hover disclosure on an evidence row, so it must not read differently depending on where the machine happens to be"), and has a known-answer test at `tests/lib/formatRelativeTime.test.ts:74-86`. Do NOT copy the `toLocaleString()` pattern sitting two lines away at `CaptureViewer.tsx:136,145` or at `ForensicsTab.tsx:59` ,  those are machine-local and would make an evidence banner read differently per timezone.

`RECAPTURE` MODEL: the ticket's wording survives it. `recapture.ts:99` sets a fresh `timestamp` and `captureLifecycle.ingest` writes a new Capture row with its own id; the original is never mutated. So the banner reads this capture's own anchored time and cannot drift. `CaptureViewer.tsx:130-147` already renders the sibling links ("Recapture of <t>" / "Recaptured <t> ->") in the toolbar directly above, which disambiguates. One nuance worth a code comment rather than a change: `recapture.ts:99` stamps the timestamp when the job is dequeued, BEFORE the render at `:151`, so a slow render's anchored time precedes the actual fetch by the render duration.

TRUSTED-TIME MODEL: it does not reach this banner, and cannot without new plumbing. The Capture row carries only `trustedTimeStatus` (`types.ts:131`); the TSA's asserted time `stampedAt` lives only on `HashVerification` (`types.ts:654`) and is produced by `shared/verify/trustedTime.ts:52`. The stamp is appended later by a worker and always postdates `capture.timestamp`, and it attests the content hash, not the operator-asserted time. So the banner must state the capture time and only the capture time; it must not imply corroboration.

The ruling's guard ("verification state stays with ProvenanceBadge") is directionally right but its premise is `false` against this tree - see `prepDocCorrections`.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** No tier hit at all, provided the banner mounts in `CaptureViewer.tsx`. That file is explicitly excluded by name at assessment line 321, and no glob in the include list covers src/renderer/components/captures/** broadly - only the eight enumerated files at lines 230, 252, 259-264. The ticket adds no IPC channel, no shared type, no schema, so it does not even brush the advisory cross-process block. Two things flip it: (1) any edit to `MhtmlViewer.tsx`, blocking tier, which converts the ticket to full ADR-0004 obligations plus human review plus no auto-merge; (2) resolving open question 2 by adding `webRequest` blocking to the mhtml-sandbox partition, which would land in `src/main/index.ts`, also blocking tier. Keep both out of this branch.

**Mock findings.**

- The banner is a single flex row at `template.html:10367-10374` with THREE parts, not one: an amber pill 'Archived copy' with the #i-archive icon (10368-10371), a truncating muted 12px middle line reading 'scripts and network disabled - rendered from page.mhtml - 2026-08-02 14:02 UTC' (10372), and a right-aligned 10px mono 'sha256 e3b1c4...b855' (10373). It sits above a flex:1;min-height:0 scroll container holding the rendered page (10375). (template 10367-10375). The app has nothing here. The ruling says the banner states the capture time; the mock also states a truncated content hash and two capability claims. Follow the mock for the pill and the time; see the two divergences below for the other two parts.
- The middle line asserts 'scripts and network disabled'. Only half of that is `true` in the app. `MhtmlViewer.tsx:47` sets webpreferences="javascript=no,`contextIsolation`=yes,sandbox=yes" so scripts are genuinely off, and `src/main/index.ts:235-245` blocks post-initial-load will-navigate. But nothing in src/main/ or src/renderer/ configures the mhtml-sandbox partition at all - grep for 'mhtml-sandbox', '`webRequest`', '`onBeforeRequest`' and '`setPermissionRequestHandler`' across both trees returns only `backgroundRenderer.ts`, `pdfExport.ts` and the MhtmlViewer partition attribute itself. will-navigate does not fire for subresources, so a non-inlined <`img`> or stylesheet in an MHTML would still be fetched. (template 10372). DIVERGE from the mock. Shipping the string verbatim puts a `false` security claim on an evidence surface, which is exactly the class of defect `docs/specs/2026-08-16-evidence-claims-audit.md` exists to catch. Narrow the copy to what is enforced ('scripts disabled', 'links inert' per `MhtmlViewer.tsx:20`) and file the network gap separately. Record the divergence on #708.
- The mock's Wayback tab carries a structurally identical sibling banner in the same slot - same flex row, same flex-shrink:0, same padding:7px 14px, same #i-archive icon - reading 'Side-by-side reference - archive.org's copy renders independently. Corroboration is your call; Birdbrain doesn't diff the two.' (template 10488-10491). Confirms the ruling's 'no collision' call: the two banners are on different tabs and make different claims. But they share one visual shell, and #401 builds the Wayback one. Per the wave merge order #401 lands first, so #704 should reuse whatever shell #401 leaves behind rather than shipping a second near-identical bar.
- The banner is density-invariant in the mock. Its padding is hardcoded 7px 14px, the pill 2px 9px / 10px, the middle line 12px, the hash 10px - none of them read a var(--d-*). The mock does use density vars elsewhere (`template.html:9620`, 10820, 11731, 11740) and defines them at 13949-13958, so the omission is a choice, not an oversight. (template 10367-10373). Matches the app: `CaptureViewer.tsx` uses no density var at all (its own toolbar is a hardcoded h-11 at line 109), and the only --d-* consumer under components/captures/ is `CaptureItem.tsx:183`. So the answer to 'how does it render at each of the three density steps' is: identically. The DoD obligation is only legibility at all three, which 12px/10px fixed text satisfies. Do not invent density scaling here.
