**Open questions.**

- Does the banner also appear on the legacy `capture.format === 'html'` branch at `CaptureViewer.tsx:247-258`, and if so with what copy?
  - Why blocking: The mock has no legacy concept, so it answers nothing. An implementer guessing "MHTML only" leaves legacy captures rendering an archived copy in an <iframe `srcDoc`> with no statement that it is one - the exact misreading the ticket exists to prevent, unfixed on the captures most likely to be old and most likely to be argued about. An implementer guessing "both" and reusing the mock string ships 'rendered from page.mhtml' over a capture that has no page.mhtml.
  - Recommendation: Show it on both. Drive the middle line off `capture.format`: MHTML gets the artifact name, legacy gets a shorter line with no artifact claim. The app already has the precedent for treating legacy differently in this exact area - `ForensicsTab.tsx:24-32` renders a dedicated amber 'Legacy HTML capture' banner in the same colour family.
- Does the banner carry the mock's 'scripts and network disabled' claim, or narrowed copy?
  - Why blocking: Scripts are genuinely disabled (`MhtmlViewer.tsx:47`, javascript=no) but network is not: nothing configures the `mhtml-sandbox` partition anywhere in src/, and `src/main/index.ts:235-245` blocks will-navigate only, which does not fire for subresources. Shipping the mock string verbatim puts a security claim the app does not enforce onto an evidence surface, which is a defect of the class `docs/specs/2026-08-16-evidence-claims-audit.md` was written to catch. An implementer guessing "just follow the mock, it is pixel truth" ships it, and a reviewer who does not know the partition is unconfigured will not catch it.
  - Recommendation: Narrow the copy to what is enforced - scripts disabled, links inert (`MhtmlViewer.tsx:20`) - record the divergence on #708, and file the mhtml-sandbox subresource-blocking gap as its own issue. Do NOT fix the gap in this branch: the fix lands in `src/main/index.ts`, which is blocking tier (assessment doc, Integrity core), and would drag a renderer-chrome ticket into the full evidence gate.
- Does the banner render the truncated content hash (mock `template.html:10373`, 'sha256 e3b1c4...b855')?
  - Why blocking: The ruling names one fact, the capture time, and says verification state stays elsewhere. The mock shows a second value the ruling does not mention. An implementer treating the ruling as exhaustive drops it and silently fails the wave's own pixel-conformance DoD; an implementer treating the mock as exhaustive adds it and may be read as re-litigating the verification-state boundary. Either way an unrecorded decision ships.
  - Recommendation: Include it, sourced from `capture.hash` and truncated. It is a content-address identifier, not a verification verdict - it says nothing about verified/tampered - so it does not cross the boundary the ruling drew, and the same value is already rendered in full at `ForensicsTab.tsx:36`. If the maintainer prefers to drop it, that is a mock divergence and needs a #708 entry.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Three things. (1) The issue carries no acceptance criteria - its body still says "No acceptance criteria yet, which is why this is needs-triage" and the 2026-08-23 ruling answers the three triage questions without converting them into criteria. (2) It carries no label that lets the gates see it: `gh api repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/704/labels` returns exactly `enhancement` and `redesign` - no `ready-for-agent`, and the `needs-triage` the body claims is not there either. (3) The three open questions above, plus one explicit instruction that is not in the issue or the ruling and that decides the merge gate: mount in `CaptureViewer.tsx`, do not edit `MhtmlViewer.tsx`.

### #695 captures: per-list search input in the capture-list header

**Verdict.** A small, purely renderer feature: thread a case-insensitive title+URL substring query through the already-pure `computeDisplayedCaptures`, render the mock's second header row, and consolidate the app's three separate clear affordances into one - the list is genuinely fully loaded, so the ruling's premise holds.

Size: medium. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** The ruling's trap is clean: the list IS fully loaded. `captureRepo.listCaptures` is `SELECT * FROM captures WHERE case_id = ? ORDER BY timestamp DESC` with no LIMIT/OFFSET (`src/main/services/db/`captureRepo.ts:14`-19`), the handler is a bare passthrough (`src/main/`ipcHandlers.ts`:271`), the preload is a bare bridge (`src/preload/index.ts:67`), `capturesQueryOptions` fetches once with no pagination (`src/renderer/lib/api/captures.ts:6-11`), and CaptureList maps every displayed capture into a `motion.div` with no windowing (`CaptureList.tsx:372-401`). No `react-window`/`react-virtual`/`@tanstack/virtual` in `package.json`. So a client-side filter searches 100% of the case's captures. No `false` negative is possible. The ruling does not need revisiting.

At 5,000 captures the client-side filter is the CHEAPEST thing on the screen and strictly reduces work. Two pre-existing costs dominate and exist on main today independent of #695: (1) `useQueries` at `CaptureList.tsx:82-84` fires one `captures:getMatchingSelectors` IPC call per capture, unconditionally (`enabled: !!captureId`, `captures.ts:38-44`) - that is 5,000 concurrent IPC round trips at mount; (2) 5,000 unvirtualized rows under `AnimatePresence mode="popLayout"` with `layout` on every child. #695 makes both better once a query is typed, because `displayedCaptures` shrinks. It does not make either worse. Worth filing separately as a scaling defect on the list; I did not file it (read-only phase).

Mechanism, reusing what exists rather than writing new machinery:
- `computeDisplayedCaptures` (`captureListModel.ts:76-95`) is already the pure, injected, tested filter pipeline and already takes `filteredCaptureIds` (the selector-filter intersection). Adding `query` to `DisplayInput` (`captureListModel.ts:40-46`) and one `.filter()` step is the whole composition story. Intersection is automatic and free - the selector filter is applied first at line 83, the query would apply in the same chain. No precedence question arises because both are conjunctive set narrowings.
- `useCaptureListFilters` (`useCaptureListFilters.ts:105-137`) already owns the local, non-store filter state and memoizes the `filters` object precisely so it can be a `useMemo` dep. Query state belongs there, NOT in `appStore`. Nothing outside CaptureList consumes it, and `CaseWorkspace.tsx:26` already clears selector filters on case change while local state resets on unmount for free.
- Selector-filter state is store-side: `activeSelectorFilters` and `filteredCaptureIds` (`appStore.ts:28-29`), populated asynchronously by `useSelectorFilters` (`hooks/`useSelectorFilters.ts:29`-51`), mounted once at `CaseWorkspace.tsx:19`, cleared by `clearSelectorFilters` (`appStore.ts:168`) which resets both fields atomically.
- "One control clears both" in state terms = one handler calling `setListQuery('')` (local) AND `useAppStore.getState().clearSelectorFilters()` (store, which zeroes `activeSelectorFilters` and `filteredCaptureIds` together). The open question is whether it also calls `clearAllFilters()` (`useCaptureListFilters.ts:119-123`, format/date/favorites) - see `openQuestions`.

Two concrete defects the change must fix, not just add to:
- The empty-state branch at `CaptureList.tsx:402-409` keys on `filteredCaptureIds || activeFilterCount > 0`. A query that matches nothing sets neither, so the operator would see `CaptureListEmptyState` ("No captures yet") on a case full of captures. The condition must gain the query.
- `countActiveFilters` (`captureListModel.ts:66-72`) feeds the Filter button's badge (`CaptureList.tsx:235-239`). If the query is folded into it, the badge shows a count for something absent from the Filter menu. Keep the badge menu-scoped and introduce a separate `isNarrowed` predicate for the clear affordance and the empty-state branch.

No IPC, no shared types, no schema, no main-process code. Renderer only.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Neither blocking nor advisory. `CaptureList.tsx` is on the assessment's explicit exclusion list at `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:322` ("Renderer chrome"), and src/renderer/stores/** at :335 ("UI-only state") - and the recommended design keeps the query out of the store entirely, so `appStore.ts` need not be touched at all. `captureListModel.ts` and `useCaptureListFilters.ts` are absent from the document in both directions. ADR-0014's advisory tier is "the cross-process contracts block, the software distribution block except `scripts/build-verifier.mjs` and `sea-config.json`, and nothing else" (ADR-0014:54) - this ticket touches neither block: no `src/shared/ipc.ts`, no `src/shared/types.ts`, no lockfile, no main process, no migration. The 2026-08-23 ruling comment states "Not evidence-affecting: no new IPC, no query-path change, no FTS work. It filters an array the renderer already holds." I verified that claim rather than repeating it: the IPC path (`ipcHandlers.ts:271` -> `captureRepo.ts:14`) is untouched by any design I can see for this ticket. So: no evidence-affecting label, no Evidence impact section, and it is eligible for the non-evidence auto-merge path (ADR-0014, exit bar keyed on the blocking tier only, :96-99).

Paths: `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx`, `src/renderer/components/captures/captureListModel.ts`, `src/renderer/components/captures/useCaptureListFilters.ts`, `src/renderer/stores/appStore.ts`

**Mock findings.**

- The search input exists exactly where the ticket says, at the top of the capture-list header. Row one is `display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px` holding a relative wrapper (`flex:1`) with an inset search icon and the input, then the collapse button, then the New-capture (+) button and its menu. Row two (`margin-top:4px`) holds sort, filter, a `flex:1` spacer, and the view-toggle group. (template 9948-9975 (row one; input at 9952, collapse at 9954, capture menu at 9957-9975), 9976-10042 (row two; view toggle at 10034-10041)). The app puts all of it in ONE row: collapse, sort, filter, spacer, view toggle, CaptureMenu (`CaptureList.tsx:176-343`). `CaptureList.tsx:172-174` carries an explicit comment saying #397 deliberately kept the header a single row rather than reserving a hole, and names #695 as the ticket that adds the input. Follow the mock: this is a two-row restructure that moves the collapse button and CaptureMenu up beside the search input, not a one-line insertion.
- The mock's input is INERT. It carries no `sc-camel-on-change`, no `value` binding, no `type`, and no `aria-label` - it is `<input placeholder="Search captures...">` plus styling. Compare the mock's own working inputs: the global search at 9221 has `type`, `aria-label`, `value={{ searchQuery }}` and `sc-camel-on-change={{ onSearchInput }}`; the notes-list search at 11224 has `value={{ noteQuery }}` / `onNoteQuery`; the Data screen's at 11683 has `value={{ dQuery }}` / `onDQuery`. (template 9952, against 9221, 11224, 11683). The mock therefore specifies placement and pixels but zero behaviour. Every behavioural decision comes from the 2026-08-23 ruling, not from the mock. It also means the mock supplies no clear (x) button inside the input, unlike the app's global SearchBar (`SearchBar.tsx:123-131`). The new input needs an `aria-label` the mock does not give it - and it must differ from `SearchBar.tsx:116`'s `Search case captures`, or a screen-reader user hears two identically labelled search fields on one screen.
- The mock has exactly ONE clear handler, `clearFilters`, reached from three places: the "Clear all filters" item in the filter dropdown (gated on `hasActiveFilters`), a "Clear filters" button in the list's narrowed empty state (gated on `capEmptyNarrowed`), and the same button in the viewer's narrowed empty overlay. (template 10025, 10084, 10171). This is the mock's answer to `one control clears both`, and it is broader than the ruling's literal wording: one handler clears everything narrowing. The app today has THREE disjoint clear paths - `clearAllFilters` in the filter dropdown (`CaptureList.tsx:299-308`, format/date/favorites only), `clearSelectorFilters` on the selector banner's x (`CaptureList.tsx:352`), and nothing at all in the empty state (402-409 is plain text). This is the substance of the open question below.
- The mock's capture screen has NO selector-filter indicator. Greps for "selector filter" and "filters active" across the whole 16,161-line template return nothing, and the header rows at 9948-10042 contain no banner element. (template 9948-10042 (absence), 10083-10085 (the mock's only narrowing signal, the empty-state button)). The app has one: `CaptureList.tsx:345-356` renders an accent strip reading "N selector filters active" with its own x. Ruling 4 outranks the mock here - it says the operator must `never be left staring at an empty list wondering which of two invisible narrowings caused it`, which requires a visible indicator the mock does not have. Do NOT delete the banner to match pixels. The right move is to widen it into a single narrowing strip that names every active narrowing and whose one x is the single clear control. Record the divergence on #708.
- The mock's list footer is `Showing {{ capCount }} of {{ capTotal }} captures`, matching the app almost exactly. (template 10141). The app already has it (`CaptureList.tsx:413-417`). It needs no change: `displayedCaptures.length` shrinks with the query for free, so the footer is already a correct narrowing readout. The mock's footer container carries `display:flex;gap:8px` with only one child, which could hint at an intended second element, but nothing is there - do not invent one.

**Conflicts.**

- #701 in `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx` at header block, lines 170-357 vs the row map at 372-401, append-only. #701's ruled scope is four kinds (capture, note, selector, tag) and the mock attaches the capture menu to the ROW (template line 10090, `sc-camel-on-context-menu` on the row div), which is `CaptureItem.tsx`, not `CaptureList.tsx`. If #701's implementer mounts shared menu state or a portal at list level instead, it lands in a different region of the file (the row map, or a new wrapper) than #695's header rewrite. Second-to-land rebases; neither blocks the other. #701 also waits on #686/#687 per ruling 1, so #695 will almost certainly land first.
- #398 in `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx` at `displayedIds` at 124, `useCaptureSelection` at 125-134, disjoint. No file overlap - #398 works the export chain (`export.ts`, `manifest.ts`, verify) and at most `CaptureSelectionBar.tsx`. But there is a semantic interaction worth naming in both PRs: `visibleSelectedIds` is `displayedIds.filter(id => selected.has(id))` (`useCaptureSelection.ts:27-30`) and the selection bar's actions are scoped to it by deliberate design (`useCaptureSelection.ts:11-16`, 60-67). Once a query narrows `displayedIds`, a typed query silently shrinks what a selection-scoped export would ship. That is the existing and intended contract for the selector filters already, so #695 does not change the rule - it just adds a second way to trigger it. Neither ticket needs to change; the #398 reviewer should know.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) "Paperwork items (#699, #663, #708, and the #695 and #707 outcomes) take no slot and can land any time after their rulings."
  - Correction: #695 is not paperwork and does not have an "outcome" in the sense the other four do. #663, #699 and #707 were closed with no work; #708 is a list to send back to the design side. Ruling 4 rules #695 to be BUILT: a client-side title/URL filter that intersects with the selector filters, with one control clearing both. That is renderer code across three source files plus tests, and it needs a dispatch slot like any other build.
  - Evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md`, "Track shape and merge order" section, against the same document's ruling 4 ("A client-side title and URL filter over the loaded list, not FTS") and the 2026-08-23 ruling comment on the issue. The doc's own "What the rulings changed" section lists only #663, #699 and #707 as closed, so #695 is counted in the thirteen as a build and then reclassified as paperwork two sections later.
- (changes-the-plan) Merge order: #398 -> #392 -> #391 -> #399 -> #401 -> #393 -> #701/#702/#704 -> #405. And: "Track C, independent. #391 first, then the ruled extras #701, #702, #704 as their rulings land."
  - Correction: #695 appears in neither the merge order nor any track, a direct consequence of the paperwork misclassification above. It belongs in track C beside #701/#702/#704 - it is independent of the export chain and of the extension track, and it has no ordering constraint against anything in the wave.
  - Evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md`, `Track shape and merge order`. Independence verified: #695 touches only src/renderer/components/captures/{`CaptureList.tsx`, `captureListModel.ts`, `useCaptureListFilters.ts`}, none of which appear in the doc's conflict map.
- (cosmetic) "Five evidence reviews, not four: #392, #398, #399, #401, #803."
  - Correction: Correct as it applies to #695 - #695 is not among them and should not be. Confirming rather than contradicting: `src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx` is on the assessment's explicit EXCLUSION list, and `src/renderer/stores/**` likewise. Neither the blocking nor the advisory tier is hit.
  - Evidence: `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md:322` (`src/renderer/components/captures/CaptureList.tsx` | Renderer chrome) and :335 (`src/renderer/stores/**` | UI-only state). `captureListModel.ts` and `useCaptureListFilters.ts` appear nowhere in the document, included or excluded.

**Test plan.** Unit, pure model - `tests/renderer/captureListModel.test.ts`. This is the highest-value target because the filter is pure and injected. The `BASE` fixture at lines 38-41 gains `query: ''`, which mechanically touches every existing assertion, so the diff is wider than the new cases. New cases, following the file's existing shape (the `filteredCaptureIds` intersection case at 118-127 and the combined-filters case at 190-200 are the two to mirror): case-insensitive title match; URL match where the title does not match; whitespace-only and empty query are no-ops returning the unfiltered set; query intersected with `filteredCaptureIds`; query intersected with format + date + `favoritesOnly` simultaneously; zero-match returns []. The `NOW` injection pattern at line 13 stays as-is - the query filter is time-independent.

Unit, component - new `tests/components/CaptureListSearch.test.tsx`, `jsdom` project (`.test.tsx` is matched by the web Vitest project, not the Electron node one). Copy the harness from `tests/components/CaptureListSelection.test.tsx:1-100` verbatim in shape: the `// @vitest-environment jsdom` directive, the `CaptureMenu` and `CaptureSelectionBar` module mocks, `fakeBridge` with `captures.list`/`listFavorites`/`getMatchingSelectors`/`getThumbnail`, and the `useAppStore.setState` reset in `beforeEach`. Assertions: typing into the input narrows the rendered `capture-item` rows; the footer text at `CaptureList.tsx:415` tracks `displayed of total`; a zero-match query renders the narrowed empty state and NOT `capture-list-empty-state` (this is the regression guard for the `CaptureList.tsx:403` defect); the single clear control empties the input AND zeroes `useAppStore.getState().activeSelectorFilters` and `filteredCaptureIds` in one gesture; Escape inside the input clears the query and does NOT clear the multi-select, which the `inEditable` guard at `useCaptureSelection.ts:74` and 82 already permits but nothing currently tests; Ctrl+A inside the input types normally rather than selecting all rows (same guard, line 76).

E2E - extend `e2e/captures-layout.spec.ts`. It already seeds two captures over the capture server and already asserts on `capture-list-collapse` (line 114) and the 40px rail (115-122), so it is both the natural home and the spec most at risk from the two-row header restructure. Add: type a query, assert the row count falls, click the clear control, assert it returns. Verify the collapse and rail assertions still pass after the restructure - that is the real E2E deliverable.

No known-answer fixtures and no `tests/shared/verify/` work: nothing here touches canonicalization, hashing or the manifest chain.

Verify loop per the background-jobs carve-out: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` (six projects; the new `.test.tsx` lands in the web project by the `.tsx`-anywhere rule), `BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 pnpm test`, `pnpm build`, then `pnpm test:coverage` and `pnpm coverage:diff` re-run after any edit. No `pnpm build:extension` - `extension/` is untouched. Diff coverage is the one to watch: the pure-model cases carry it cheaply, but the header JSX in `CaptureList.tsx` is changed lines that need the component test to reach 90%.

**Open questions.**

- What exactly does the one clear control clear - only the search query plus the selector filters, as ruling 4 says literally, or everything narrowing the list including format, date and favorites-only, as the mock's single `clearFilters` handler implies?
  - Why blocking: It is the only acceptance-shaped clause the ruling contains, and the app already has three disjoint clear paths that the answer either consolidates or leaves in place: `clearAllFilters` in the filter dropdown (`CaptureList.tsx:299-308`) clearing format/date/favorites, the selector banner's x calling `clearSelectorFilters` (`CaptureList.tsx:352`), and nothing in the empty state. An implementer who reads "clears both" narrowly ships a THIRD clear control whose scope partially overlaps the other two, which is precisely the confusion the ruling is trying to prevent, and it is not fixable in review without redoing the header. It also decides whether `countActiveFilters` (`captureListModel.ts:66-72`) stays menu-scoped or becomes a general narrowing count, which changes what the Filter badge means.
  - Recommendation: One handler clearing everything: query, selector filters, format, date, favorites. It matches the mock, where a single `clearFilters` serves the dropdown item (template 10025) and both narrowed empty states (10084, 10171), and it satisfies the ruling's actual concern - the operator never faces an empty list with an invisible cause. Surface it in three places, all calling the same function: widen the existing selector banner at `CaptureList.tsx:345-356` into a narrowing strip that names every active narrowing with one x; keep the dropdown's "Clear all filters" item pointed at the same handler; add the mock's clear button to the narrowed empty state, which the app lacks entirely. Keep `countActiveFilters` menu-scoped for the Filter badge and add a separate `isNarrowed` predicate for gating the clear affordance and the `CaptureList.tsx:403` empty-state branch.

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: Two things. (1) The `ready-for-agent` label. `gh api repos/thebristolsound/birdbrain/issues/695/labels` returns exactly `enhancement` and `redesign` - the 2026-08-23 ruling comment says `Relabelled off ready-for-human`, and that much is `true`, but nothing was put in its place. Without the label the dispatch routine cannot pick it up, and `pre-pass-gate.yml` will not see the resulting PR as an agent PR. (2) Acceptance criteria. The issue body predates the ruling and is written entirely as three open questions; the ruling comment answers them in prose but writes no checkboxes. An agent has enough to start once the clear-control scope above is settled, but there is no checklist for a reviewer to score the result against, and the two concrete defects the change must fix (the `CaptureList.tsx:403` empty-state branch, the Filter-badge count) appear in neither the body nor the ruling. Everything else is present: the mock settles the pixels, and the code is unambiguous.

### #803 Data screen rebuild as an artifact browser over the case's real evidence files

**Verdict.** This is a rewrite, not a rebuild: nothing in the current DataExplorer survives, and three of the five mock tabs (MHTML Parts, Headers and TLS, Manifest Ledger) need new main-process readers and new IPC because the data they show is either not enumerable today (MIME parts), only half-present (response headers but no request headers), or has never been exposed to the renderer at all (manifest entries).

Size: very-large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** Against origin/main at 0bf207e5.

REUSABLE AS-IS (renderer-side, no new IPC):
- `captures:list` already delivers the whole `Capture` record to the renderer, including `hash`, `screenshotHash`, `textHash`, `sizeBytes`, `timestamp`, `manifestIndex`, `prevHash`, `entryHash`, `headers`, `httpStatus`, `userAgent`, `tlsCertChain`, `lastVerifiedStatus` (`src/main/services/db/`captureRepo.ts:337`-372`, `src/shared/ipc.ts:624`). So the artifact table's NAME/SOURCE/TYPE/`SHA-256`/CAPTURED columns, the Properties tab and most of Headers and TLS are pure renderer work over data already on the wire.
- `captures:getContent(captureId, 'txt')` (`src/shared/ipc.ts:627-630`, handler `src/main/`ipcHandlers.ts:654`-663`) serves the Extracted Text tab exactly. Read the FILE, not `capture_texts`: the `.txt` bytes are what `textHash` binds (`captureLifecycle.ts:147-150`), and `capture_texts` is an unauthoritative mirror. Same reasoning as #234 in `captureLifecycle.ts:346-355`.
- `extractedData:reprocess` backs the Reprocess button the mock keeps (template 11725).
- Density: `--d-tree`, `--d-row`, `--d-head`, `--d-rowpad` already exist at the mock's values (`src/renderer/styles/globals.css:168-210`). No new tokens.
- Route needs only a component swap (`src/renderer/routes/__root.tsx:284-288`).

MUST BE WRITTEN (main process, all new):
1. Artifact inventory. On-disk layout is FLAT: `{caseId}/{captureId}.{mhtml|html|png|txt}` plus `{captureId}_thumb.jpg` (`captureStore.ts:17-18`, 98-110). The layout has no raw/derived directory; that grouping is a UI synthesis. Per-artifact SIZE is not stored for png/txt/html (only `sizeBytes` for the MHTML), so the SIZE column and the "missing on disk" flag both need a main-side `statSync` sweep per case. New service, new IPC channel.
2. MHTML part inventory. `extractHtmlFromMhtml` (`mhtmlDecoder.ts:14-55`) walks the boundary but keeps only `text/html` parts and returns concatenated text. It never yields Content-Location, Content-Type, encoding, size or index. The MHTML Parts tab needs a new enumerating parser. New service, new IPC channel.
3. Manifest ledger reader. `readManifestSnapshot`/`readEntries` (`manifest.ts:142-177`) and `verifyManifestChain` (`manifest.ts:541-553`) exist, but NO IPC channel exposes manifest entries to the renderer ,  `grep -n manifest src/shared/ipc.ts` returns only comments (lines 194, 521-547). New IPC channel returning entries plus a live `ChainVerifyResult`.
4. Keyword Hits snippets. `selector_matches` is (`selector_id`, `capture_id`) only (`migrations.ts:116-123`, `selectorRepo.ts:160-162`). No offsets, no snippets. The mock's pre/hit/post must be re-derived by re-running the selector matcher over `capture_texts.content`, honouring `is_regex`.

CANNOT BE PRODUCED AT ALL (must be omitted or explicitly labelled absent):
- Per-part `SHA-256`. `grep -rn "partHash|partsHash|perPart|mimePart" src/` returns nothing. Nothing hashes a MIME part, ever. See the open question.
- Request headers. `capture.headers` is `JSON.stringify` of `normalizeResponseHeaders` output (`captureLifecycle.ts:222`, `extension/src/utils/headers.ts:11-20`) ,  RESPONSE headers only, lowercase keys. No request headers are captured anywhere. Of the mock's Request panel only Host (derivable from `capture.url`) and User-Agent (`capture.userAgent`) exist. `Capture.method` is 'extension'|'background' (`shared/types.ts:40-41`), the CAPTURE method, not the HTTP verb.
- "TLS chain at capture time" (template 11868). `tlsCertChain` is a post-storage re-fetch, explicitly corroboration-only, and ADR-0002 says in terms: "Cert-chain fields presented to a court must be labelled corroboration, never binding origin proof." The heading must state `refetchedAt`, not capture time.
- Per-artifact integrity flag. Verification is per CAPTURE, not per artifact: `verifySidecars` returns one human-readable reason string on the first sidecar mismatch and the whole capture becomes 'tampered' (`captureLifecycle.ts:346-417`). A per-file "`SHA-256` mismatch vs manifest" badge cannot be produced without parsing that prose string.
- ed25519 signer. The app signs RSA-2048, RSA-`SHA256` PKCS#1 v1.5 (`signingKey.ts:202-206`, 238-241). No key fingerprint is exposed anywhere today.
- `network.har`. Confirmed absent, per the ruling and #804.

BACKSTOP GAP WORTH NAMING: `src/renderer/components/dashboard/**` is excluded from the evidence path list except two named files, one of which is `DataExplorer.tsx` (assessment lines 65-70, 268). If the rebuild moves the screen into a new directory such as `src/renderer/components/data/**`, the mechanical backstop stops firing on the app's most integrity-dense screen forever. Either keep `DataExplorer.tsx` as the mounted entry file, or amend the include list in the same PR (there is precedent: the #227/#387/#400 amendments at assessment lines 28-46). New main-process services under `src/main/services/` also match no glob ,  there is no blanket `src/main/services/**` entry.

TRAP: adding a new React Query key domain requires appending it to `QUERY_DOMAINS` in `src/main/services/logSafe.ts:219` or `tests/main/services/queryDomains.test.ts:51-57` fails.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Blocking, and the prep doc is right that #803 is evidence-affecting. `DataExplorer.tsx` is an explicitly named blocking-tier entry (assessment line 268), listed as an exception to the excluded src/renderer/components/dashboard/** glob (assessment lines 65-70). The label is already on the issue, so the full ADR-0004 obligations apply regardless of what the diff touches. Two things the tier list does not currently catch, both worth stating in the PR. First, the new main-process services (artifact inventory, MHTML part enumeration, manifest ledger reader) match NO include-list glob: there is no blanket src/main/services/** entry, only the named files. Second, if the rebuilt screen moves into a new directory such as src/renderer/components/data/**, the backstop stops firing on the app's most integrity-dense screen permanently, because dashboard/** is excluded and only two files are carved back in. Mitigation: keep `DataExplorer.tsx` as the mounted entry file and nest the new components beneath dashboard/cases/, or amend the include list in the same PR - there is precedent for exactly that at assessment lines 28-46 (#227, #387, #400 all added moved-out files). If the ticket is split, every split branch must keep the evidence-affecting label or the pieces that carry the real risk will merge unreviewed.

Paths: `src/renderer/components/dashboard/cases/DataExplorer.tsx`, `src/main/services/db/** (read paths: captureRepo, extractedDataRepo, selectorRepo)`, `src/main/services/mhtmlDecoder.ts (if the parts parser refactors it rather than adding a sibling)`

**Mock findings.**

- Full Data screen subtree: root at 11676, left rail 11679-11710, main pane 11713-11768, detail pane 11770-11936, status footer 11941-11949. Search input 11683 with clear button 11685. Tree loop 11692-11708, row button 11697 carrying both n.click and n.ctx. Table header 11731-11738 with the six columns Name/Source/Type/Size(right-aligned)/`SHA-256`/Captured. Row buttons 11739-11763. Empty state 11765 reads 'No files match this filter.' Detail header 11771-11781, tab bar 11783-11789, tab panes 11792-11935. (template 11676-11949). The app has no equivalent at all. Ruling 7 says build it, re-presenting existing data only.
- Tree model. Three group nodes (template 15376-15391): Data Sources = one node per capture, label `id + ' · ' + host`, each with `raw` and `derived` child folders; Views = one child, File Types, whose children are the distinct detected types; Results = Keyword Hits (children = the keyword list), Extracted Text, Integrity Exceptions (alert: `true`), Manifest Ledger. Counts at 15398: ledger nodes count ledger entries, ft/kw nodes count their children, everything else counts matching files; group nodes set `showCount` false. Initial state at 12970-12971: all three groups expanded, first capture expanded, first `mhtml` selected, tab 'text'. (template 15376-15419, 12970-12971). Follow the mock. `raw`/`derived` is a UI synthesis only: the real layout is flat, `{caseId}/{captureId}.{ext}` (`captureStore.ts:17-18`). Map mhtml/html/png to raw and `txt` to derived. Capture ids are UUIDs in the app, so the node label should be title or host plus a short id, not the raw `uuid`.
- The search input filters the TABLE, not the tree. Template 15423-15424: when the query is non-empty, `entries` is replaced by a filter over name + capture + type + sha; the tree is built at 15392-15419 before the query is consulted and never references it. The placeholder says 'Search files, hashes, text...' (11683) but text is not among the four fields searched. (template 15423-15424, 11683). The issue body says 'A search input above the tree filters it' and AC 6 says 'Tree search filters by file name and by hash'. Both misread the mock. Follow the mock: filter the table over name, source, type and hash, and either drop 'text' from the placeholder or wire it to `capture_texts`.
- Per-artifact `SHA-256` column in the MHTML Parts tab (column header at 11819, values at 15501 via `dShort`(`dHex`(p.seed))), with the caption at 11833: 'Every part is hashed at capture time. Re-hashing on open compares against the manifest ,  a part that no longer matches is listed as an integrity exception rather than silently re-rendered.' (template 11819, 11833, 15500-15503). The caption is `false` for this app and must not ship. Nothing hashes a MIME part: `grep -rn 'partHash|partsHash|perPart|mimePart' src/` returns zero, `mhtmlDecoder.ts:14-55` does not enumerate parts, and the manifest capture entry carries only `contentHash`, `screenshotHash` and `textHash` (`manifest.ts:232-241`). Diverge: either omit the column, or compute at display time over the raw encoded bytes and label it explicitly as not anchored. See the open question.
- Manifest Ledger tab. Chain banner at 11894-11898: 'Chain intact through seq 0024' plus 'signer: bb-local-key · ed25519 · 9c4f...a017'. Columns at 11901: Seq, Time (UTC), Event, Target, Entry hash, Prev hash. Rows at 11904-11912. Caption at 11915 draws the distinction that matters: 'the ledger still verifies, but the files on disk no longer match what was recorded.' Fixture events at 15319-15331 are capture.begin, artifact.write, capture.seal, verify.mismatch, verify.missing, case.verify. (template 11891-11917, 15319-15331, 15508-15517). Diverge on three points. (1) The signer is RSA-2048 / RSA-`SHA256`, not ed25519 (`signingKey.ts:202-206`, 238-241), and no key fingerprint is exposed today. (2) The real event vocabulary is the six ManifestEntryInput types (`manifest.ts:232-330`): capture, deletion, timestamp, export, archive-export, import. The real event vocabulary has no artifact.write or verify.* entry ,  verification never writes to the manifest, it writes captures.`last_verified_status` (`captureLifecycle.ts:441-446`). One capture entry covers `mhtml`, screenshot and text, so Target is a `captureId` or a `caseId` and can never be a file path. (3) Chain state must be computed live from `verifyManifestChain` (`manifest.ts:541-553`); an empty or missing manifest returns valid:`true` with zero entries (543-550), so the banner must not say 'intact' for a case that has recorded nothing. Follow the mock on the caption at 11915: it is the one thing on the screen that correctly separates chain validity from bytes-on-disk.
- Headers and TLS tab. Request panel at 11842-11851, Response panel at 11854-11865 with a status line, TLS block at 11867-11889 headed 'TLS chain at capture time' (11868) with role/cn/issuer/valid/fingerprint per cert (11873-11880) and a free-text analyst note at 11885. (template 11838-11889, 15504-15507). Diverge on both halves. Request: the app captures response headers only (`extension/src/utils/headers.ts:11-20`, `captureLifecycle.ts:222`). Of the mock's six request fields only Host (from capture.url) and User-Agent (capture.userAgent) exist; there is no HTTP method, `Accept-Language` or `Referer`. Render the two and state plainly that request headers are not captured, or omit the panel ,  do not draw a panel that reads as a captured request record. TLS: ADR-0002 forbids the 'at capture time' framing outright. The heading must carry `refetchedAt` and the word corroboration. The analyst note at 11885 is fixture prose with no backing field; omit it.
- Properties tab, two groups (15518-15537). File: Name, Path, Type, Size on disk, `SHA-256`, Manifest state. Source capture: Capture, URL, Captured (UTC), Remote address, HTTP status, Collector. (template 11919-11935, 15518-15537). Mostly buildable. Path must be the real flat path, not the mock's 'cases/nightjar/CAP-0007/raw/page.mhtml'; the root comes from settings.storagePath (`shared/types.ts:206`). 'Size on disk' needs a stat for png/txt/html ,  only the MHTML has a stored `sizeBytes`. 'Remote address' has no field anywhere in Capture (`shared/types.ts:85-132`); omit it. 'Collector' can be built from `toolVersion` + `extensionVersion`, but 'headless profile' is fixture text; the real analogue is capture.method ('extension' vs 'background') and `consentSuppression`. 'Manifest state' is per-capture, not per-file (see the integrity finding).
- Row heights. The Data tree hardcodes {compact: 21, default: 24, comfortable: 28} at 15400, but the mock's own `densityVars()` sets `tree: 22/24/28` at 15952-15954. (template 15400, 15949-15959). The mock contradicts itself by 1px at compact. The app already carries --d-tree at 22/24/28 (`globals.css:180`, 194, 208) matching `densityVars` exactly, and --d-row 26/30/36, --d-head 24/28/32, --d-rowpad 10/14/18 all match the mock's table usage at 11731 and 11740. Use the existing tokens; hardcode nothing. Send the 21-vs-22 inconsistency back on #708.
- Context-menu action sets for the four kinds #701 defers. file (15656-15666): Open file, Reveal in Finder, Copy path, Copy `SHA-256`, Verify against manifest, Re-extract text, Run selectors on this file, Export... submenu, Rename (F2), Delete. folder (15695-15703): Show only this group, Expand all below, Collapse all below, Copy group path, Reprocess these files, Run selectors on group, Verify group against manifest, Export.... part (15705-15713): Open part, Open Content-Location, Copy Content-Location, Copy `SHA-256`, Verify against manifest, Create selector from URL, Quote into note, Export.... ledger (15715-15722): Show target artifact, Copy entry hash, Copy previous hash, Verify chain from here, Show signature detail, Quote into note, Export.... (template 15656-15722, 15600-15609). Diverge on the destructive items. The shared `del` item (15637) is appended to the `file` set, and `Rename` with it. Deleting or renaming one artifact out from under a signed manifest entry is precisely the operation the chain exists to detect; the app's only delete path is a whole capture with a manifest deletion entry (`manifest.ts:434-452`). Drop Delete and Rename from the file kind and record it on #708. 'Verify against manifest' maps to captures:verify but is per-capture, so on a file row it verifies the capture, not the file ,  label it accordingly. 'Verify chain from here' and 'Show signature detail' have no backing API; `verifyManifestChain` is whole-chain (`manifest.ts:541`).
- Status footer at 11941-11949: '<n> data sources · <n> artifacts · <n> integrity exceptions', right-aligned storage path (15538-15542). (template 11941-11949, 15538-15542). Buildable. 'data sources' is captures, not hosts. The path comes from settings.storagePath. The exception count must not read as zero-means-clean when nothing has been verified ,  see the integrity finding.
- Multi-select on artifact rows: cmd/ctrl-click toggles, selected rows get a left accent bar and a tinted background (15437-15441). (template 15437-15441, 11740). Not mentioned anywhere in the issue body or acceptance criteria. The app has a comparable pattern in `e2e/capture-multiselect.spec.ts`. Flag as scope the ticket does not cost.
- No zero-state. `dataVals` falls back with `sf = this.dFiles.filter(...)[0] || this.dFiles[0]` (15447), which is `undefined` for a case with no captures, and every downstream read of `c` throws. (template 15447). The mock has no empty-case design at all. The app needs one ,  the current screen has a real empty state at `DataExplorer.tsx:188-198`. Diverge and write one; do not treat the mock's absence as a spec.

**Conflicts.**

- #701 in `src/renderer/components/ui/ (new menu primitive) and its per-kind action registry` at the registry module #701 creates, serialize. #701 lands first. #803 adds the file/folder/part/ledger entries to the registry #701 wrote. Verified: #701's 2026-08-23 ruling says those four kinds are 'deliberately deferred' to the Data-screen ticket, and `ui/index.ts` has no menu export today. The dependency direction in the prep doc is RIGHT, but it is soft for three of #803's four deliverables: the tree, the artifact table and the five tabs need nothing from #701, and #701's own ruling requires every menu action to be reachable inline or by keyboard anyway, so the menu is strictly additive. Recommend splitting the menus into their own follow-on so #803's bulk is not gated behind a track-C ticket scheduled near the end of the wave.
- #398 in `src/main/services/manifest.ts` at 291-303 (export entry) vs #803's read-only use of `readEntries`/`readManifestSnapshot` at 142-177, disjoint. Disjoint provided #803 adds nothing to `manifest.ts` and puts its ledger reader in a new module that imports the existing read functions. State that as a branch constraint; if #803 edits `manifest.ts` it becomes strict-serial behind #398 and #399.
- #399 in `src/main/services/manifest.ts` at same as #398, disjoint. Same constraint. #803 also does not migrate, so it has no interaction with #399's v31 bump of LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION.
- #392 in `src/shared/ipc.ts` at IPC_CHANNELS block and the channel-map interface, append-only. Second-to-land rebases. Advisory tier since ADR-0014.
- #398 in `src/main/ipcHandlers.ts` at handler registrations, append-only. Second-to-land resolves. Advisory tier.
- #399 in `src/shared/types.ts` at type additions, append-only. Second-to-land resolves. #399 adds a Case field; #803 adds three new interfaces. No shared symbol.
- #401 in `src/preload/index.ts` at the bridge() enumeration, append-only. Second-to-land resolves. Advisory tier. The prep doc's conflict map omits preload entirely.
- #405 in `src/renderer/components/onboarding/tourSteps.ts` at tour targets, disjoint. No conflict. The Data screen is not a tour target: `tourSteps.ts` targets browser, nav-captures, `viewertabs`, `caseswitcher`, nav-signals, selectors, nav-notes, `noteeditor`, nav-overview, `linkmap`, export, `newcase`. Verified at `tourSteps.ts:52-152`.

**Prep doc corrections.**

- (changes-the-plan) 'All of it already exists. This ticket presents it; it acquires nothing new.'
  - Correction: True for the artifact table, Extracted Text and most of Properties. False for MHTML Parts: no part inventory exists (`mhtmlDecoder.ts:42` filters to text/html and line 54 returns concatenated text) and no per-part hash exists anywhere in the tree. It is also `false` in a second sense for the Manifest Ledger: the data exists on disk but has never crossed the IPC boundary, so the ticket adds new main-to-renderer evidence surface rather than re-rendering an existing query.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`mhtmlDecoder.ts:14`-55`; `grep -rn 'partHash|partsHash|perPart|mimePart' src/` returns nothing; `grep -n -i manifest src/shared/ipc.ts` returns only comments at 194, 521, 530, 538, 544, 547
- (changes-the-plan) 'A search input above the tree filters it' and AC 'Tree search filters by file name and by hash'.
  - Correction: The search filters the artifact TABLE over four fields (name, capture, type, hash) and leaves the tree entirely untouched. The tree is built before the query is read and never consults it.
  - Evidence: /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html:15392-15419 (tree built, no q reference) and 15423-15424 (`if (q) entries = this.dFiles.filter(...)`)
- (changes-the-plan) 'Headers and TLS: `Capture.headers`, `Capture.tlsCertChain`.'
  - Correction: Understates two constraints. `Capture.headers` is response headers only, normalized to lowercase keys, with no request headers and no HTTP verb anywhere in the schema. `Capture.tlsCertChain` is a post-storage re-fetch that ADR-0002 requires be labelled corroboration and never presented as bound to the captured transaction, so the mock's 'TLS chain at capture time' heading cannot ship as written.
  - Evidence: `extension/src/utils/headers.ts:11-20`; `src/main/services/`captureLifecycle.ts`:222`; `docs/adr/0002-tls-capture-corroboration-only.md` paragraph 3; `template.html:11868`
- (changes-the-plan) 'Integrity Exceptions: `lastVerifiedStatus` / `lastVerifiedHash`, the same source VerifyBar and ProvenanceBadge already read.'
  - Correction: Correct as a source but understates two things. First, `lastVerifiedStatus` is `undefined` until a verify has been run, so a case that has never been verified shows an empty Integrity Exceptions node that means 'unknown', not 'clean' ,  `overviewModel` already handles this by counting a third `unverified` bucket. Second, verification is per capture: a sidecar mismatch returns a single prose reason and marks the whole capture 'tampered', so the mock's per-file badge cannot be produced without parsing that string.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/`captureLifecycle.ts:346`-417` and 441-446; `src/renderer/components/overview/`overviewModel.ts:49`-56`
- (cosmetic) 'Artifacts and hashes: `Capture.mhtmlPath`, `screenshotPath`, `htmlPath`, `contentHash`, `screenshotHash`, `textHash`, `sizeBytes` (`src/shared/types.ts`).'
  - Correction: There is no `Capture.contentHash`; the field is `Capture.hash`. `contentHash` is the manifest entry field name. Also, `sizeBytes` is the MHTML size only ,  `png`, `txt` and legacy `html` have no stored size, so the SIZE column needs a main-side stat pass. And `htmlPath` is written by no current ingest path, so `dom.html` rows appear for legacy and imported captures only.
  - Evidence: `src/shared/types.ts:92` and 114; `src/main/services/manifest.ts:236`; `src/main/services/`captureStore.ts`:188`; `grep -rn "format: 'html'" src/main/` returns nothing
- (changes-an-estimate) 'Keyword Hits: selectors and `selector_matches`, via `selectorRepo`.'
  - Correction: `selector_matches` stores only (`selector_id`, `capture_id`). No offset, no snippet. The mock's pre/hit/post snippet (template 15333-15345, rendered at 11745-11749) must be re-derived by re-running the selector against `capture_texts`.content, and it must use the same matcher so `regex` selectors behave identically.
  - Evidence: `src/main/services/db/migrations.ts:116-123`; `src/main/services/db/`selectorRepo.ts:158`-162`
- (changes-an-estimate) AC: 'Row height is density-driven (compact: 21, default: 24, comfortable: 28)'.
  - Correction: The mock contradicts itself: its own `densityVars` sets tree at 22/24/28, and the app's `--d-tree` already matches that exactly. Use `var(--d-tree)`; the quoted 21 is a mock bug for #708.
  - Evidence: /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html:15400 vs 15952-15954; `src/renderer/styles/globals.css:180`, 194, 208
- (changes-an-estimate) Prep doc conflict map, 'Shared IPC spine. #391, #392, #398, #399, #401 all append to `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/types.ts` and `src/main/`ipcHandlers`.ts`.'
  - Correction: #803 belongs in that list and is omitted ,  the map predates the ticket. #803 appends to all three plus `src/preload/index.ts`, which the map does not mention at all despite it enumerating every channel by hand.
  - Evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-23-redesign-wave3-ultracode-prep.md` 'Shared IPC spine' paragraph; `src/preload/index.ts:54-63`
- (changes-an-estimate) Prep doc treats #803 as one dispatchable branch in the wave's ticket table.
  - Correction: It is a rewrite spanning a new main-process service cluster, three new IPC channels, a full renderer screen with five tabs, four context-menu kinds, and the rewrite of an E2E screenshot spec plus two published docs. Realistically 1500-2500 changed lines. It should be split; see the open question for the proposed order.
  - Evidence: File list in this report; `e2e/readme-screenshots.spec.ts:525-560`; `README.md:62-65`; `website/content/docs/screenshots.mdx:78-84`
- (changes-the-plan) Ticket AC: 'The existing extracted-data browsing this screen replaces is either preserved somewhere or its removal is stated and justified in the PR.'
  - Correction: Sharper than the AC implies. `DataExplorer.tsx` is the ONLY renderer consumer of all five `extractedData` query channels; removing it leaves the `extracted_data` table and its FTS index written on every ingest, archived, counted in Diagnostics, and readable nowhere in the product. Extracted data does not appear in the evidence export or report either.
  - Evidence: `grep -rn 'extractedData' src/renderer/ | grep -v lib/api` returns only `DataExplorer.tsx` and two count labels; `grep -rn extracted src/main/services/export.ts src/main/services/reportHtml.ts` returns nothing

**Test plan.** Unit, node project (`tsconfig.test.node.json`):
- `tests/main/services/mhtmlParts.test.ts`, beside the existing `tests/main/services/mhtmlDecoder.test.ts`. Known-answer fixture MHTML with a quoted-printable text/html part, a base64 image part and a part with a folded header: assert index, Content-Location, Content-Type, Content-Transfer-Encoding and raw byte length for each. If a per-part digest ships, assert a fixed expected hex over fixed bytes, and assert the digest is over the encoded bytes as documented rather than the decoded ones.
- `tests/main/services/manifestLedger.test.ts`, beside `tests/main/services/manifest.test.ts` and `tests/main/services/manifestPackageHash.test.ts`. Build a temp case dir through `appendManifestEntry` with a capture, a deletion, a timestamp and an export entry; assert the row mapping (index -> Seq, timestamp -> Time, type -> Event, `captureId`/`caseId` -> Target, `entryHash`/`prevHash`). Assert an empty manifest yields zero rows and a chain state the UI must not render as 'intact'. Assert a hand-corrupted line surfaces `brokenAt` and reason rather than a shorter clean list.
- `tests/main/services/artifactInventory.test.ts`. Temp store via `createCaptureStore({ getRoot })` (the injection seam at `captureStore.ts:83`): `mhtml` present, `png` present, `txt` deleted from disk after ingest, a legacy row with `htmlPath`, and a `_thumb.jpg` on disk. Assert the `txt` row reports missing-on-disk, the thumbnail is excluded, and sizes come from stat rather than from `capture.sizeBytes` for anything but the MHTML.

Unit, web project:
- `tests/renderer/dataModel.test.ts` over the pure model, mirroring how `overviewModel` is tested. Tree construction for zero captures, one capture with no screenshot, and a capture with every artifact; node counts; the search filter across name, source, type and hash; the raw/derived split.
- `tests/components/DataScreen.test.tsx` replacing `tests/components/DataExplorer.test.tsx`. Three groups render with counts; the empty-case state renders and does not throw (the mock's own model crashes here, template 15447); the Integrity Exceptions node carries the alert dot and reads as unknown rather than clean when nothing has been verified; tab switching; the 'request headers are not captured' and 'TLS re-fetched at <time>, corroboration only' statements render. Port or delete the two existing behaviours at `DataExplorer.test.tsx:70` and :86 depending on the IOC-browsing ruling; the selector-origin test at :99 goes with the 'To selector' affordance.

E2E:
- `e2e/readme-screenshots.spec.ts:525-560` must be rewritten. It clicks a literal 'Infrastructure' button, then 'Domain Reference', and falls back to `.divide-x > div` nth(1) - all four disappear. Regenerate `website/public/assets/screenshot-recon.png` and update the captions at `README.md:62-65` and `website/content/docs/screenshots.mdx:78-84`.
- New `e2e/data-screen.spec.ts` following the shape of `e2e/forensics-tab.spec.ts`: ingest a fixture capture through the capture server the way `e2e/mhtml-capture.spec.ts` does, navigate to `/cases/$caseId/data`, assert one row per real artifact and no HAR row, assert the `SHA-256` cell equals the capture's stored hash, open the Manifest Ledger tab and assert the entry hash matches `capture.entryHash`.

Known-answer / fixture work: the MHTML fixture and the manifest fixture are both known-answer tests in the ADR-0004 sense and are what discharges the obligation for the two new evidence readers. Put shared verify-adjacent fixtures in `tests/shared/verify/` only if verify-core is touched; it should not be.

Gates: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` (six projects, tests included), `BIRDBRAIN_REQUIRE_OPENSSL=1 pnpm test`, `pnpm build`, then `pnpm test:coverage` and `pnpm coverage:diff` re-run after any edit. `pnpm build:extension` is not needed - nothing under `extension/` changes. Watch `tests/main/services/queryDomains.test.ts`: it fails on the new query-key domain until `QUERY_DOMAINS` in `src/main/services/`logSafe.ts`:219` is extended.

**Open questions.**

- The MHTML Parts tab shows a per-part `SHA-256` column. The app has never hashed a MIME part. Omit the column, or compute a digest at display time over each part's raw encoded bytes and label it explicitly as computed-now and not anchored in the manifest?
  - Why blocking: An implementer who guesses 'compute it' ships a hash column on an evidence screen that looks identical to the manifest-anchored hashes one pane above it, and the mock hands them a caption (template 11833) asserting the parts were hashed at capture time and are re-checked against the manifest. Both claims are false. An implementer who guesses 'omit' silently drops a column the acceptance criteria name. Either way the decision is unreviewable after the fact, and it is the single highest-risk interpretation surface in the ticket.
  - Recommendation: Compute at display time, over the raw encoded bytes as they sit inside the archive, and label the column header '`SHA-256` (computed now)' with a one-line statement that only the whole MHTML file is anchored in the manifest. The reasoning: the whole-file hash IS anchored and verified, so given an intact file a part digest is deterministic and adds real navigational value; but it must never render a shield, a checkmark or the word 'verified', and the mock's 11833 caption must be replaced wholesale. Delete the part context menu's 'Verify against manifest' item (template 15710) for the same reason. If the maintainer prefers zero new hashing on an evidence screen, omit the column - that is also defensible and cheaper.
- What happens to extracted-data (IOC) browsing? The mock's Data screen has no category-to-subcategory indicator browser, and `DataExplorer.tsx` is the only renderer consumer of all five `extractedData` query channels.
  - Why blocking: Deleting the screen makes the `extracted_data` table and its FTS index write-only: still populated on every ingest (`captureLifecycle.ts:463`), still archived (`caseArchive.ts:119`), still counted in Diagnostics, and readable nowhere. It is also absent from the evidence export and report. The acceptance criterion says 'preserved somewhere or its removal is stated and justified', which is exactly the decision an unattended agent should not make alone, because 'preserved somewhere' means building a second screen this ticket does not budget for.
  - Recommendation: Preserve it as a fourth node under the mock's Views group - a 'Extracted Indicators' tree node whose table columns are category, subcategory, value, page count - and keep the 'To selector' affordance from `DataExplorer.tsx:145-163`. This costs one more table renderer, keeps every existing IPC channel and its tests alive, and fits the mock's own information architecture without inventing a screen. The alternative, moving it to Signals, collides with #400's finished work and should be a separate ticket if wanted.
- Does #803 ship as one branch, and if not, what is the split and the merge order?
  - Why blocking: At an estimated 1500-2500 changed lines across a new main-process service cluster, three new IPC channels, a five-tab renderer screen, four context-menu kinds, an E2E rewrite and two published docs, this will not complete unattended in one cycle, and it is evidence-affecting so it cannot auto-merge. A dispatcher needs to know how many slots it consumes before it starts.
  - Recommendation: Split into four, all carrying the evidence-affecting label. (a) Main-side readers: artifact inventory, MHTML part enumeration, manifest ledger reader, plus IPC, preload, types and the three known-answer test files. No UI. (b) Screen shell: tree, artifact table, Properties tab, route swap, replacing `DataExplorer.tsx`, plus the E2E and docs updates. (c) The three remaining tabs: Extracted Text, Headers and TLS, Manifest Ledger, plus MHTML Parts once the hash question is answered. (d) Context menus for file/folder/part/ledger, after #701. Merge order a -> b -> c -> d. A defensible minimum is two branches, a+b then c+d. Only (d) depends on #701, which decouples the bulk of the work from a track-C ticket scheduled late in the wave.

**Ready for agent.** no. Missing: Four things. (1) The per-part `SHA-256` ruling - the ticket asserts 'all of it already exists' and that is `false` for this one column, so the acceptance criteria cannot be met as written. (2) A disposition for the extracted-data IOC browser this screen deletes. (3) A split decision with a merge order; the ticket is a multi-day branch as scoped. (4) The acceptance criteria need correcting on three points the mock does not support: the search filters the table not the tree (template 15423-15424), per-artifact integrity flags cannot be produced because verification is per capture (`captureLifecycle.ts:346-417`), and the tree row height should read var(--d-tree) rather than the mock's self-contradictory 21px. Separately, the ticket should state that the Headers and TLS tab must not present request headers the app never captured and must not label the TLS chain 'at capture time' - ADR-0002 already decides that, but leaving it to the implementer invites the mock's wording to ship verbatim.

### #708 docs(design): corrections to send back to the design side from the 2026-08-21 standalone mock

**Verdict.** A docs-only consolidation ticket whose real work is merging three scattered sources (the #708 body's 14 items, the #708 comment's 37 wave-2 constraints, and six of the eight 2026-08-23 rulings) into one handover document, and whose current content is missing six ruled items, not the three the prep doc names.

Size: medium. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: none (differs from prep doc: no).

**What it requires.** No src/ code, no tests, no schema. The mechanism is: read three sources, deduplicate, classify each item as "design must decide" versus `recorded deviation, no action wanted`, and write one tracked file.

What already exists and can be reused: the #708 issue body (14 items, written 2026-08-22, verified via `gh`); the #708 birdbrain-agent comment of 2026-08-22 (37 implementation constraints from wave-2 PRs #709-#715); `docs/plans/2026-08-21-wave2-phase1-findings.md` sections B1-B4, G1-G7, C1-C12 (lines 137-184), which is where most of the body items originate.

What must be written: the six ruled items the current #708 content does not carry (enumerated in `mockFindings`), plus the V2-bundle-provenance item, plus the classification split.

Two of the wave-2 phase-1 items are already resolved and must NOT be sent to design. C11 ("per-signal Export CSV has no backing query") was fixed in code: `src/main/services/db/`selectorRepo.ts:290`-315` now takes an optional `selectorId` and the doc comment at :291-295 says so explicitly, with the handler at `src/main/`ipcHandlers.ts:468`-471` passing it through. C8 ("list-header search slot stays empty") was answered by ruling 4 (#695 builds it). Copying findings forward without checking which ones the tree already closed is the main correctness risk here.

The V2 branch situation is confirmed and is worse than the standalone README states. All six named files exist at origin/prototype/design-handoff-2026-08 (e967542e, `docs(design): refresh handoff bundle to V2`, 2026-08-12): `HANDOFF.md`, `MOTION.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md`, `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`, `github.md`, `style_sync_patch/SCREEN_NOTES.md`, all under `design_handoff_birdbrain_prototype`/. Three of the six ALSO exist on main under docs/design-handoff/2026-08-10-birdbrain-prototype/ with different blobs (`ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md` branch 079eb36c vs repo 97c73baa; `github.md` 98a3dc0c vs 43db7900; `style_sync_patch/SCREEN_NOTES.md` 9e3363cd vs a0a2b399). So a reader told to "read `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`" finds an in-repo copy, reads the older V1 text, and never learns there is a newer one on the branch. `HANDOFF.md`, `MOTION.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md` exist nowhere on main (git ls-files returns only the three V1 paths). The branch is a full unmerged prototype branch that nothing in CI protects.

Two pointer defects in the repo's own handoff docs, found while checking that claim: `docs/design-handoff/README.md:14` says corrections go per the round-trip protocol in each bundle's `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`, but the current source bundle (docs/design-handoff/2026-08-21-birdbrain-standalone/) contains only `Birdbrain-standalone.html` and `README.md`, so that pointer resolves to nothing for the bundle that matters. And the V2 `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`'s "round-trip protocol" is one sentence at its line 131 ("Anything Modified/Rejected: send back the constraint, not a redesign"), not a protocol, so the standalone README:79 description of it overstates what is there.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** grep 'docs/' over `docs/specs/2026-07-31-evidence-affecting-paths-assessment.md` returns zero hits, so no docs/** path appears on the include list at either tier. The diff is documentation only and makes no claim about acquisition, hashing, the manifest chain, or verification. Note the one precedent the assessment records at line 116: #532 was a threat-model documentation change labelled evidence-affecting by triage judgment with no glob hit at all. That precedent does not reach here - a round-trip list of design corrections asserts nothing about evidentiary behaviour.

Paths: `docs/specs/2026-08-23-standalone-mock-corrections-brief.md`, `docs/design-handoff/README.md`, `docs/design-handoff/2026-08-21-birdbrain-standalone/README.md`

**Files.**

| Path | Lines | Risk | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `docs/specs/2026-08-23-standalone-mock-corrections-brief.md` | new file | low | The deliverable. Consolidated round-trip register: the 14 body items, the actionable subset of the 37 wave-2 constraints, the six missing ruled items, and the V2-bundle provenance request. Named `-brief.md` per `docs/README.md:11`, which lists `-design.md` / `-spike.md` / `-assessment.md` / `-brief.md` as the specs/ naming set. |
| `docs/design-handoff/README.md` | 14 | low | Optional. The sentence points readers at 'each bundle's `ENGINEERING_REVIEW.md`'; the current source bundle has no such file (ls shows only `Birdbrain-standalone.html` and `README.md`). Recommend filing separately rather than widening this diff, per `CLAUDE.md`'s file-every-defect rule. |
| `docs/design-handoff/2026-08-21-birdbrain-standalone/README.md` | 73-81 | low | Optional. The 'keep reading these from the branch' list does not warn that three of the six also sit on main in the older V1 bundle with different content, so the in-repo copy silently wins. Recommend filing separately. |

**Mock findings.**

- MISSING FROM #708 (ruling 2, #702). The mock derives the Recording indicator from auto-capture alone: `const sessionOn = s.autoCapture` (template 13468), consumed at `recActive: sessionOn` (13538) and `recDotColor: sessionOn ? '#ef4444' : ...` / `recLabel: sessionOn ? 'Recording' : 'Idle'` (13569). (template 13468, 13538, 13569). The app has a separate session model: `appStore.ts` carries `sessionActive` and `connectedToExtension` as distinct fields, and TopBar renders SessionControls and ConnectionStatus separately. Ruling 2 (2026-08-23, on #702) says the indicator binds `sessionActive`, NOT `autoCaptureMode`, and that auto-capture stays a Signals concern. Diverge from the mock; record it here. The #708 body does not contain this item.
- MISSING FROM #708 (ruling 2, #702). The mock hides the Connected chip while recording: `showConnected: extConnected && !sessionOn` (template 13537). (template 13537). Ruling 2 rejects this outright: during recording is exactly when the operator needs to know the extension is connected, because it is the only signal that captures can arrive. The Connected chip never hides. Diverge from the mock. Not in the #708 body.
- UPGRADE NEEDED (ruling 5, #707). The mock's top bar carries a Browser button (template 9307, `sc-camel-on-click="{{ goBrowser }}"`, `data-tour="browser"`, label at 9309) routing to a simulated Chrome window screen (`vBrowser` at 13521, `goBrowser` at 13523, the screen itself at template 12176 carrying `data-screen-label`="Chrome Extension"). (template 9307-9309, 12176, 13521-13523). #708 body item 14 records this only as 'tracked separately'. Ruling 5 (2026-08-23) makes it a permanent NO: Birdbrain gets no in-app browsing surface, ever, because capture happens in the operator's real browser through the extension and a second acquisition path was never asked for. #707 closed on that basis. The item must be rewritten from 'tracked separately' to the ruled rejection, or the design side will keep drawing it.
- MISSING FROM #708 (ruling 7, #803). The mock's Data-screen artifact table lists a HAR log the app cannot produce: `F('CAP-0007', 'network.har', 'raw', 'HAR log', '896 KB', 16)` at 15170, a second at 15276 flagged 'In manifest, missing on disk', a ledger event `verify.missing` targeting `CAP-0019/network.har` at 15330, and an icon rule keying on `.har` at 15147. (template 15147, 15170, 15276, 15330). The app captures no HAR. #803's acceptance criteria say 'No HAR row, and no placeholder row for any artifact the case does not have', and HAR acquisition is split out as #804 (needs-triage) because it is a new acquisition path with a credential-disclosure problem. The mock's artifact table therefore ships three rows and one ledger event that no case can populate. Not in the #708 body.
- MISSING FROM #708 (ruling 1, #701). The mock binds 23 `sc-camel-on-context-menu` handlers across 13 entity kinds, all routed through one `openCtx` helper (for example `ctx: function (e) { self.openCtx('selector', sel.id, sel.label, e) }` at template 13929). (template 23 occurrences of sc-camel-on-context-menu; example at 13929). Ruling 1 scopes the app to FOUR kinds (capture, note, selector, tag). `file`, `folder`, `part`, `ledger` are deferred to #803 because they live on a screen the app has not built. The remaining five are unwired, not designed out, because the mock's menu actions duplicate inline controls and add no capability. The design side should know only four of thirteen are being built and why. Not in the #708 body. Verified current state: grep -rn `onContextMenu` src/renderer/ returns one hit, a comment at `SignalRow.tsx:25`.

Its only demo affordance is a tour button, `tourDeleteDemo` (template 13427-13430), which sets `demoDeleted` and filters the card out of the dashboard list (13491, 13502); the button label 'Delete demo case' is at 12657. Nothing in the mock's export dialog or certification surface distinguishes a demo case. (template 12657, 13427-13430, 13491, 13502). Ruling 6 adds a demo flag on the Case, surfaced in the export dialog and stated in the Certification, riding #399's v31 migration. That is a design surface the mock never drew, invented to answer #707's question about an operator exporting fixture data to a court. The copy and treatment are undesigned and should go back for specification. Not in the #708 body.

The mock's Page-tab 'Archived copy' banner carries three things: the pill, a caption reading 'scripts and network disabled - rendered from page.mhtml - 2026-08-02 14:02 UTC', and a truncated hash `sha256 e3b1c4...b855` in font-mono at the right edge. It does not rule on the truncated hash or the 'scripts and network disabled' clause. If #704 ships time-only, the banner drops two of the mock's three elements and that is a divergence worth recording; if it keeps the hash, it does not. Flagging as a candidate rather than asserting it, because the ruling is silent.

Worth folding into item 9 so the design side is not asked twice.
