What proves it: (1) `vale sync` once, then `vale docs/specs/2026-08-23-standalone-mock-corrections-brief.md` with zero errors. Note that .vale/styles/ currently contains only `config` in this worktree, so the Google package is not fetched and `vale sync` is a real prerequisite, not a formality. .vale.ini turns off `Google.We`, FirstPerson, Will, Acronyms, Colons, Contractions, Passive, Semicolons and `Parens`, so the house register passes. Only *.md is linted.

(2) Every template citation in the new document re-verified with `sed -n 'Np' /tmp/birdbrain-mock/template.html` before it is written. The unpack to check against is md5 45cc353308009126ede962d1a6cb0067, 16161 lines. This is the single highest-value check on this ticket, because the #702 ruling comment already shipped citations 393 lines off and this document is the artifact the design side will act on.

(3) The V2 bundle claim re-verified with `git ls-tree -r --name-only origin/prototype/design-handoff-2026-08 -- design_handoff_birdbrain_prototype`, and the stale-copy claim with `git rev-parse origin/prototype/...:<path>` against `git hash-object docs/design-handoff/2026-08-10-birdbrain-prototype/<path>` for the three overlapping files.

(4) Each carried-forward wave-2 finding checked against the current tree before it goes on the list. C11 and C8 from `docs/plans/2026-08-21-wave2-phase1-findings.md` are already closed and must be dropped.

Gate behaviour on a docs-only PR: `pnpm lint` covers .ts/.tsx only, `pnpm typecheck`'s six projects include only src/**, tests/**, extension/**, e2e/**, and `scripts/diff-coverage.mjs:173` sets `passed = !scored || pct >= min`, so with no instrumented source lines changed it prints 'NOT SCORED' and exits 0. Nothing in the standard verify loop can fail on this diff, which means the prose checks above are the whole gate.

**Open questions.**

- What form does the deliverable take, and does producing it close #708 or leave #708 open as the standing intake queue?
  - Why blocking: The ticket body says 'there is no other place to collect them' but never says where they go. The three plausible answers produce incompatible artifacts. If an agent guesses 'a third comment on #708', the register stays inside GitHub where the next wave's readers cannot grep it and where it is already failing (two comments, 51 unreconciled items, six ruled items missing). If it guesses 'a tracked document' while the maintainer intended #708 to remain the live register, there are then two sources that drift. And if the doc is written and #708 closed, there is nowhere for wave-3 implementers to send their own constraints, which is exactly the failure #708 was opened to prevent.
  - Recommendation: Tracked document at `docs/specs/2026-08-23-standalone-mock-corrections-brief.md`, and #708 stays OPEN. `docs/README.md:11` puts design briefs and assessments in specs/ with the `-brief.md` suffix; this is a brief handed to a third party, not an implementation checklist, so plans/ is wrong, and it is neither an ADR nor published site reference. Do not put it in docs/design-handoff/ - that folder's README:14 says 'Do not edit bundle contents', and the bundles are kept byte-faithful as received. Post a comment on #708 linking the document, and keep #708 open as the intake queue with the document as the dated snapshot the design side is actually sent. Relabel needs-triage off once it has acceptance criteria.
- Do all 37 constraints from the 2026-08-22 comment go to the design side, or only the subset where design has a decision to make?
  - Why blocking: The 37 are a mix of three different things: items where the design side must rule (the missing extension mono token, the undesigned map empty-state copy, the 'N matches in M of K captures' count that the schema cannot produce), items shipped as deliberate deviations that need no design response (UTC-pinned hover timestamps, `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` wording, `ProseMirror` backspace semantics, the Floating UI flip threshold), and items that are pure merge-order artifacts now resolved (#390's MENTION_ROUTES pointing at /selectors until #400 landed). Sending all 37 undifferentiated produces a 51-item list where roughly two thirds need no action, and a list that long gets skimmed rather than answered - which loses the dozen items that do need a ruling.
  - Recommendation: Two sections, explicitly labelled: 'Needs a design decision' and 'Recorded deviations, no response wanted'. Put the count of each in the opening line. Drop merge-order artifacts entirely once verified resolved against origin/main, and drop C11 and C8 from the wave-2 phase-1 findings for the same reason (C11 is fixed at `src/main/services/db/`selectorRepo.ts:296`-298`, C8 is answered by ruling 4).

**Ready for agent.** yes-after-answering-questions. Missing: The ticket carries needs-triage, not ready-for-agent, and has no acceptance criteria at all. Before the label: (a) answer the two blocking questions above - deliverable form/path, and whether all 37 wave-2 constraints are in scope; (b) write acceptance criteria, which should at minimum name the six missing ruled items, require every template citation to be re-verified against the canonical unpack, and require the V2-bundle provenance item; (c) state whether the two optional README pointer fixes are in scope or get their own issue (recommend their own issue). Everything else the ticket needs already exists and is verified: the sources, the rulings, the branch, and the file hashes.

### #405 feat(onboarding): case tour and chain-verified demo Case

**Verdict.** The ten-step case chapter already exists and matches the mock line for line, so #405 is not a tour ticket: it is a bundled Case Archive, its import path on a fresh install, a demo mark that survives export and round trip, and a Delete-demo ending.

Size: large. Confidence: high. Evidence tier: blocking (differs from prep doc: yes).

**What it requires.** Against 0bf207e5 the case chapter is already built. `tourSteps.ts:63-147` is the mock's ten-step case chapter transcribed exactly (`template.html:13318-13327`), all ten `data-tour` anchors are mounted in the app (`NoteEditor.tsx:118`, `Sidebar.tsx:91`, `TopBar.tsx:89`, `CaptureViewer.tsx:151`, `AutoCaptureCard.tsx:94`, `HeroSection.tsx:29`, `ExtensionBanner.tsx:24`, `ExportMenu.tsx:121`, `BacklinkMap.tsx:66`), the case chapter auto-fires on first case open for fresh installs (`OnboardingTour.tsx:67-73`), jump-ahead works (`useTourEngine.ts:300-315`), and `CoachMark.tsx:123` already labels the final step `Keep exploring`. So the reusable half is large.

What must be written: (1) a bundled demo Case Archive and a main-process service that resolves and imports it; (2) a "Delete demo case" button on the final coach mark and an ending that deletes the case and returns to the Dashboard; (3) consumption of the demo flag #399 lands in v31; (4) restoration of the "we seeded a demo case" clause of the intro copy, which `tourSteps.ts:36-50` deliberately dropped, WITHOUT restoring the simulated-browser clause that ruling 5 killed; (5) the case-tour e2e, which does not exist (`e2e/onboarding-tour.spec.ts` covers the intro chapter only, 106 lines).

Three mechanism facts that decide the shape of the branch, all verified:

FIRST, the import cannot run on a fresh install as the code stands. `importCaseArchive` throws "Operator name required" when settings.operatorName is empty (`caseArchive.ts:347-353`). DEFAULT_SETTINGS.operatorName is '' (`settings.ts:62`) and the only writer is Settings -> Operator (`OperatorConfig.tsx:42-47`). A fresh install is exactly the state that gets the demo Case and exactly the state that has no operator name. This is unaddressed anywhere in the ticket, the ruling, or the prep doc.

SECOND, the demo mark's round trip runs through `caseRepo`, not `caseArchive`. `collectCaseRow` is SELECT * (`caseRepo.ts:140-146`) so export carries any new column for free, but `importCaseRow` has a hard-coded nine-column INSERT (`caseRepo.ts:148-169`). A column absent from that list is silently dropped on import. The ruling's AC ("survives a case-archive round trip") therefore lands on `caseRepo.ts:151-153`, which is also where #399 must add `case_number`.

THIRD, schema drift is already handled in the safe direction. `inspectCaseArchive` rejects only archives NEWER than CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION (`caseArchive.ts:248-252`, currently 3 at :64); older imports are accepted and `importCaseRow` defaults every missing key with ?? `null`, a behaviour `tests/main/services/`caseArchiveRoundTrip.test.ts`:357` and :375 already pin for pre-v30 and old-epoch rows. So a fixture frozen at archive version 3 keeps importing as LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION (`core.ts:6`, currently 30) moves on; new columns take their DDL defaults. The residual risk is a future migration that adds a NOT NULL column without a default, or a CASE_ARCHIVE_SCHEMA_VERSION bump whose new data the frozen fixture lacks. Nothing exercises the bundled fixture today, so that breaks on an operator's first launch rather than in CI. A test that imports the shipped fixture is the cheap fix.

Two more verified gaps the branch has to absorb. The `viewertabs` anchor does not mount unless a capture is selected: `CaptureViewer.tsx:82-90` returns early before the tablist, and `CaseWorkspace.tsx:22-27` clears `selectedCaptureId` on every case entry. So the case tour's first coach mark is `anchorMissing` and renders centred even on a populated demo Case. And `deleteCase` (`caseRepo.ts:50-57`) deletes only the DB row; no service removes the case directory, so the Delete-demo ending leaves the fixture's mhtml/png/txt files and its manifest on disk. The ticket's "removes it cleanly" is not what the code does.

**Evidence tier reasoning.** Two independent reasons, and either alone settles it. First, the issue already carries the evidence-affecting label, and ADR-0014 is explicit that 'a labelled PR owes the full obligations whatever its diff touches' - the tiers govern the mechanical backstop only. Second, the diff hits blocking-tier paths on its own merits: the demo mark's round trip runs through `caseRepo.importCaseRow`, and the operator-name gate question sits inside `caseArchive.ts`. So: full ADR-0004 obligations, Evidence impact section, known-answer test, preserved backward verification, human review, never auto-merges.

Paths: `src/main/services/caseArchive.ts (blocking: 'Archive export/import with id remapping')`, `src/main/services/db/caseRepo.ts (blocking: src/main/services/db/** kept blocking deliberately)`, `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx (blocking, if #399 does not land the demo statement)`, `src/shared/ipc.ts, src/preload/index.ts, package.json (advisory only)`

**Files.**

The final step at 138-146 gains the delete ending. `completionAfter` at 276-290 must decide what a delete-demo close persists. |

**Mock findings.**

- The final case-tour coach mark carries two buttons: 'Delete demo case' (outline) and 'Keep exploring' (accent), rendered only when the step carries final: true. (template 12655-12658, gated by `tourFinal` at 13424, set by final: `true` at 13327). `CoachMark.tsx:119-123` already renders one button labelled 'Keep exploring' on the final step, so half the mock is built. Only the Delete-demo button and its handler are missing. Follow the mock.
- `tourDeleteDemo` ends the tour, marks the case chapter done, sets `demoDeleted`, and navigates to the Dashboard with no active case. (template 13427-13429; the deleted case is then filtered out of the dashboard cards and the palette at 13491 and 13502). The app has no equivalent. It needs cases:delete (`ipcHandlers.ts:169`) plus a navigate to '/'. Follow the mock on the flow, but note `deleteCase` (`caseRepo.ts:50-57`) removes no files, so the fixture's bytes survive the delete.
- The intro chapter's extension step promises two things the app does not have: a button that 'opens a simulated browser', and 'We seeded a demo case for you; open it and the tour continues there.' (template 13307). `tourSteps.ts:36-50` deliberately dropped both clauses and its docstring says the copy must not promise either. #405 restores the demo half only; the browser half stays dropped per ruling 5 on #707. An implementer who pastes 13307 verbatim reintroduces a promise the maintainer killed.
- The mock's demo case is 'Nightjar Phishing Kit', type malware, 8 captures, with selectors, notes, tags and activity built around it. One capture (c8, Telegram: Nightjar Support) carries status 'tampered'. (template 12976 (the case row), 12988 (the tampered capture), 13001-13080 (captures, selectors, extracted values), 15958 (thread content)). Diverge on the tampered capture. `inspectCaseArchive` counts a capture whose file hash does not match as a failure (`caseArchive.ts:290-300`), which makes `overallValid` `false` and blocks import unless `overrideTamper` is passed (361-365) - and an override is permanently recorded in the case manifest. The fixture must verify clean. Everything else is transcribable as content.
- The mock says nothing about marking a demo Case anywhere in an export surface. A grep for 'demo' over the whole template returns six hits: the final-step button, the intro copy, the state field, the handler, and two dashboard filters. (template 12657, 12973, 13307, 13429, 13491, 13502). The demo flag in the export dialog and the Certification is a maintainer ruling (ruling 6, 2026-08-23), not a mock transcription. The mock has no pixel truth to match; #399 sets the shape and #405 consumes it.
- The ten case-chapter steps, their routes, targets, titles, bodies, `kbd` hints and the final flag. (template 13318-13327). Already shipped by #404. `tourSteps.ts:63-147` matches this list step for step including copy. This is the strongest single reason #405 is smaller as a tour ticket and larger as a fixture-and-import ticket than the issue body reads.

**Conflicts.**

- #399 in `src/main/services/db/caseRepo.ts` at `importCaseRow`, lines 148-169 (the nine-column INSERT at 151-153), serialize. #399 lands first per the wave merge order and adds both v31 columns (`case_number` and the demo flag) to the INSERT list in one edit. #405 then only adds the known-answer round-trip test. If #399 adds `case_number` alone, #405 re-opens a blocking-tier file to add the second column.
- #399 in `src/renderer/components/export/ExportDialog.tsx` at whole file, serialize. Ruling 6 assigns the demo statement to #399. #405 must verify it is there rather than assume it; if it is not, #405 inherits a blocking-tier renderer file it was not scoped for.
- #401 in `src/renderer/components/onboarding/tourSteps.ts` at CASE_STEPS step 2, lines 72-77, disjoint. No file overlap - #401 rewrites `WaybackTab.tsx` and CaptureViewer, #405 edits the copy. But #405 must land after #401 or the tour tells the operator there are four tabs when there are three. Same-file conflict none; ordering dependency real.
- #771 in `src/renderer/components/onboarding/useTourEngine.ts` at start(), lines 176-201 (the unconditional `persistCompletion` at 184-185), serialize. #771 is a behaviour ruling, not a fix; options 2 and 3 both edit this function and `completionAfter` in `tourSteps.ts`, which #405 also edits. Rule #771 and land it before #405 starts, or explicitly declare it out of #405's scope in the issue.
- #391 in `src/renderer/components/notes/NoteEditor.tsx` at `data-tour`="`noteeditor`" at line 118, disjoint. #391's selection bar lands in a new sibling per the prep doc, so the anchor should survive. #405 only needs the anchor to keep existing; a smoke assertion in the case-tour e2e is the cheap guard.
- #803 in `src/renderer/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx` at NAV_ITEMS, lines 6-12, disjoint. The Data entry is not toured, in the app or in the mock. No interaction.

- (changes-the-plan) #405 is evidence-adjacent and carries no label.

- (changes-an-estimate) #399 also round-trips the field through `caseArchive.ts`.
  - Correction: `caseArchive.ts` holds no cases column list. It delegates to `caseRepo.collectCaseRow` (SELECT *) and `caseRepo.importCaseRow`, whose nine-column INSERT is the actual bottleneck. The contended region is `caseRepo.ts:148-169`, not `caseArchive.ts`, and the conflict map points #399 and #405 at the wrong file.

  - Evidence: `tourSteps.ts:76` ('Screenshot, full page, extracted text, and archive.org snapshots - one tab each') against `CaptureViewer.tsx:22` (TABS includes 'wayback') and #401's body ('The Wayback tab becomes a right-side slide-out panel')
- (cosmetic) The ticket has no acceptance criterion covering that, and it needs one before it is dispatchable.

  - Evidence: `gh` issue view 405 --json body: five checkboxes, none mentioning demo, export dialog, or Certification

**Test plan.** Unit, main process. A new `tests/main/services/`demoCase.test`.ts` covering: the bundled archive resolves in both packaged and dev shapes; a second import is refused without explicit confirmation; the imported case comes back marked demo. Extend `tests/main/services/`caseArchiveRoundTrip.test`.ts` (beside the exclusion-column case at :357 and the old-epoch case at :375) with the known-answer round trip the ruling's AC names - create a demo case, `exportCaseArchive`, `importCaseArchive`, assert the demo mark is still set on the new case row. That test is the ADR-0004 known-answer artifact.

Unit, fixture integrity. A test that runs `inspectCaseArchive` over the shipped resources fixture and asserts `overallValid` `true`, then `importCaseArchive` against the current schema. This is the one test that catches schema drift: without it, a future migration that the frozen fixture cannot satisfy fails on an operator's first launch instead of in CI. Cheap and load-bearing.

Unit, renderer. Extend `tests/renderer/`tourSteps.test`.ts` (262 lines) with the restored intro copy (asserting the demo clause is present AND that no simulated-browser clause is), the final step's two endings, and what `completionAfter` persists on a delete-demo close.

E2E. Extend `e2e/onboarding-tour.spec.ts`, reusing `launchAsFreshInstall` at :16-24. One spec walks the whole ten-step case chapter on the demo Case, asserting the coach-mark counter runs 1 of 6 to 6 of 6 and the screen cards run Screen 1 of 4 to Screen 4 of 4, and ends on Delete demo case: the case disappears from the dashboard, the chapter is recorded complete, and no re-import happens on reload. A second, shorter spec ends on Keep exploring and asserts the demo Case remains as an ordinary Case. `e2e/case-archive.spec.ts` is the model for driving archive flows past the native dialogs.

Not covered by any of the above and worth naming in the PR: the Certification's demo line and the export dialog statement land in #399's tests, not here. #405's job is to prove the mark reaches them, which the round-trip test plus one assertion on a package built from a demo Case does.

Coverage. `pnpm` test:coverage plus `pnpm` coverage:diff before the push, re-run after any edit (#508). A binary fixture is not scored, but `demoCase.ts` is, and its error paths (missing fixture, failed verification, refused re-import) are where the 90% changed-line bar will bite.

**Open questions.**

- How does the demo Case get past the operator-name gate on a fresh install? `importCaseArchive` throws 'Operator name required' when settings.operatorName is empty (`caseArchive.ts:347-353`), DEFAULT_SETTINGS.operatorName is '' (`settings.ts:62`), and the only writer is Settings -> Operator (`OperatorConfig.tsx:42-47`). A fresh install is exactly the state that gets the demo Case and exactly the state with no operator name.
  - Why blocking: An implementer who does not hit this until integration will either relax a gate that exists to keep unattributed evidence out of the chain, or bolt on a first-run operator prompt that nobody scoped. Both are decisions with evidence consequences, made under time pressure at the end of the wave, in a blocking-tier file.
  - Recommendation: Carve out the bundled demo path with an explicit synthetic attribution rather than relaxing the gate: import the demo with a fixed operator string such as 'Birdbrain demo fixture' recorded in the import custody entry (`caseArchive.ts:424-437` already writes `operatorName` into it), so the manifest says truthfully who imported it, the gate stays intact for real archives, and the custody record itself becomes a second place the package says what it is. The alternative worth considering is seeding the demo Case lazily on the first case open once an operator name exists, which keeps the gate untouched but breaks the mock's 'we seeded a demo case for you' promise on the intro card.
- Where does the fixture archive come from - a binary checked into the repo, or one generated at build time by a script? And how is it kept valid as the schema moves?
  - Why blocking: It decides whether the branch adds a binary to git, a build script, a CI step, and a signing key, or none of those. It also decides whether the archive's manifest signature is stable across builds. That is a different-shaped branch either way and it cannot be deferred to review.
  - Recommendation: Check in a pre-built archive under resources/ and ship it via build.extraResources beside the extension entry (`package.json:72-79`), because the archive is self-verifying - `inspectCaseArchive` verifies the chain against the key bundled inside the archive (`caseArchive.ts:275-277`), not against the local install's key - so a checked-in file needs no build-time signing and gives a stable, reviewable artifact. Keep it small: four artifact types per capture (`captureStore.ts:24-29`) means `png` screenshots dominate the size, so use few captures with modest screenshots rather than the mock's eight. Pair it with the fixture-import test above so drift fails CI rather than an operator's first launch.

**Ready for agent.** no. Missing: Three things. (1) The ruling's acceptance criterion exists only in a comment; the issue body still carries the original five ACs and none mentions the demo mark, the export dialog, or the Certification. Paste it. (2) The two blocking questions above - the operator-name gate and how the fixture ships - have no answer anywhere in the ticket, the ruling, or the prep doc. (3) The issue carries no ready-for-agent label (enhancement, evidence-affecting, redesign only). Separately, #399 must be merged and its treatment of `caseRepo.importCaseRow` known before this branch is cut.

Suggested AC block to paste onto the issue, replacing nothing and appended to the existing five:

- [ ] The bundled demo Case is marked with the Case demo flag that #399 lands in v31. The mark is set by the demo import itself, not read from the fixture's `data.json`, so regenerating the fixture cannot silently produce an unmarked demo Case.
- [ ] The export dialog states plainly, on a demo Case, that this is a demonstration Case, for both export classes. Export is not blocked and no option is disabled.
- [ ] The Certification of any Evidence Package produced from a demo Case states that the Case is a demonstration Case. The package still verifies and the standalone verifier's verdict is unchanged from a non-demo package.
- [ ] The demo mark survives a case-archive round trip: exporting a demo Case to .birdbrain and importing it yields a Case still marked demo. Proven by a known-answer test in `tests/main/services/`caseArchiveRoundTrip.test`.ts`, not asserted.
- [ ] A test imports the shipped fixture archive against the current schema, so a later migration the frozen fixture cannot satisfy fails CI rather than an operator's first launch.
- [ ] The demo import's custody entry names who imported it. If the fresh-install operator-name gate is carved out for this path, the carve-out is documented in the Evidence impact section and the substituted attribution is visible in the manifest.
